# Changelog

All notable changes to Atticus. Versions follow [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org); contracts C1 to C6 change only with an ADR, a schema version and a migration (`atticus upgrade`).

## 2.0.0-beta.26 (2026-11-11)

### Changed

- **The app is laid out as an editor (ADR 0194).** A title bar whose box opens Ctrl+K (search, go to a page, ask, or run a command); an activity bar of the six sections, each icon named in a tooltip, with More pages, Settings at its foot, and the count of open questions on Questions; a side panel you can resize or hide; a tab per page you open (a change's pages share the change's tab, Home stays first, at most 12, kept when the app reloads; arrows, Enter, Delete and Ctrl+Shift+arrows on the tabs), the address still the page shown so links, Back and Forward work as before; the bottom panel under the page; and a status bar with the metarepo, repos, open questions, what runs, the local API, you and your role, and the version. A tab you leave keeps what you typed, what you opened and its scroll. In VS Code's panel there is no second activity bar, title bar or status bar; under 1000 px the side panel opens over the page, under 600 px the activity bar is at the foot. The shell asks the server for nothing of its own: Home's requests are unchanged. **Settings › Layout** and Ctrl+K's "Switch to the classic layout" keep the classic layout (the menu on the left) for this release; every page is reachable and renders in both.
- **The new shell's bottom panel holds the Console with Questions, Problems and Runs as tabs**: the shown run's live lines with follow, search, copy and Stop; the open questions, each linking to its answer; failed runs, blocking findings and repos whose facts the verifier rejected; and the recent runs, one pick showing it in the Console. Its top edge resizes by drag or arrow keys and keeps its height, Show in the Console opens it on that run, and Questions and Problems are read only when their tab is first shown.
- **The app's new side panel has an Explorer**: the changes in flight with their stage (and which wait on you), the systems as a tree of their repos (marked lib, gw or outside) opening the map on each box, the open questions by repo, and pinned pages; each section folds, a filter narrows them all, it is a keyboard tree (arrows, Home, End, Enter, a letter), and a section reads its data only once it is open and the page's own requests are answered.
- **Home fits one screen, as a control screen.** A line of status with who you are and your role; six tiles, each a number with one place to go, quiet at nothing and loading or failing on its own: what waits on you (the open needs and manual steps, said apart under it), the questions to answer, the changes in flight by stage, corrections to apply on the map, blocking findings (with the open findings by source), and the facts in the catalog (with interfaces and repos trusted). Then three short panels with their top few and See all: Next for you (Run next in one row with its button, the command folded; what to propose; your role's questions; Bring in what merged), what needs a person by section with counts (the whole list, with its filter and bulk actions, opens under it on a click; the digest is in its menu), and your changes with the current one's gate pipeline, whom each waits on, and the map one link away. Setting up Atticus and the slash commands are folds at the foot. The whole page is in view at 1440 by 900 (5,650 px tall before; about 550 px of Home now, which also fits the IDE shell's Home tab), with a short scroll at 1024 by 768 and one column on a phone. The embedded system map, the catalog's bars and the findings' charts left Home, so it asks for no graph or diagram, and for set-up and the work to propose once each (41 requests to 37 on a 12-repo estate).
- **The map's canvas takes most of the page, in both layouts (ADR 0194).** Its controls are one slim bar: the lens as a segmented control, where you are, Add to the picture, the tools, Keep this arrangement, Tidy up and Reset as icon buttons named in words (each with a tooltip), and the zoom, fit, focus, full screen and Find at its right. The tools open as a drawer over the canvas (its × or Esc closes it, the focus back on its button); the legend folds in the canvas's lower left corner and stays folded in this browser; the details are a panel at the canvas's right, beside it rather than over it, that Hide details closes and Show details opens (adding, connecting, declaring or picking a link opens it again). A picture's height is what is left of the page that scrolls it, so in the IDE layout it ends above the bottom panel: at 1440 by 900 the map's canvas shows 588 px of its height in the shell (362 px before) and 678 px in the classic layout (370 px before).
- **Questions, Knowledge and Set up read as the editor's pages.** Questions keeps its title and filters in view, the counts and Ask AI side by side, then the list on the left and the question on the right, each scrolling on its own (opening one, from the list or a link, no longer moves the page); a question is a card with the question as its heading, where it was asked and its id in muted monospace, the lines it read in a box that scrolls with the cited line marked, its options as one column with each radio beside its words, Ignore's reason and your own words opening under their option, and Save at its foot; an answer given is one short box with Change the answer opening the form in place. Owner questions and Map › Trust's answer boxes are the same cards. Knowledge's and Set up's pages have small sentence-case titles and labels above their fields, fields of one width, help muted, commands as short lines with Copy, words at a readable measure and code that scrolls in its own box; AI no longer says How AI runs here twice, and Across the changes' views are one strip of tabs. Same words, controls and requests.
- **A change's pages are laid out like an editor, compact and tidy.** All changes is the list beside the change (each row its title, its id muted under it, cut short with the whole on hover) and the change's phases are a row of small steps, its next step and actions in one card, then its artifacts and findings, tasks and AI runs, files and prototype side by side with no gaps. At a glance, Feature design, Stories and Tasks start with a head naming the change, its id and its stage with the page's links and change picker grouped at its end; the work (the stage's conversation, drafts, stories, tasks) is on the left and the files and what the stage rests on beside it, one column in a narrow panel and no sideways scroll at 420 px. Panels have small sentence-case labels and compact buttons, empty states are a short line, a long explanation shows two lines with More, a task's commands fold under From a terminal, and Release, Model runs, History and Flows follow the same look in light and dark.

### Fixed

- **The Explorer's Pinned heading is named "Pinned"**: its Pin button stands beside the heading, not inside it, so a screen reader no longer read the heading as "Pinned Pin this page: …". It looks as it did.
- **The status bar's version is the server's**, the same field the classic top bar shows and `atticus --version` prints (it read 2.0.0-beta.24 only because the shell was built on beta.24's CLI; with beta.25 merged it reads 2.0.0-beta.25); the app spells no version of its own, and a test says so.
- **The new shell's second review, fixed.** IntelliJ's App tab is an editor's panel as VS Code's is (it loads the app with `?host=intellij`, kept for the session), so it shows no second title, activity or status bar; Esc in Ctrl+K no longer also closes the side panel over the page; the Explorer reads again when shown and keeps the activity bar's and status bar's counts current; the bottom panel never squeezes the page (its height follows the window and the column's bars); two changes whose ids differ by a dot or an underscore get their own tabs; a closed tab's draft doesn't come back; with the side panel over the page, the scrim, opening a page from it and any move of the address close it with the focus kept; the side panel's edge takes only the main button, ends a cancelled drag and isn't scrolled by touch; pins stay when storage is full; an app opened with no address starts on the tab that was shown without mounting Home first; the focus stays on the page after closing a tab by mouse, switching layout or unpinning with Delete; the tab shown is scrolled into view; the shown row's stage and counts keep 4.5:1 in the light theme; and Ctrl+K opens the Console, the open questions, the problems and the recent runs in the bottom panel.

## 2.0.0-beta.25 (2026-11-11)

### Added

- **`absorb`, a sketch stroke that folds a component into another.** Its flows move with the same entries, it is retired, and its callers now call the other at the same entry, so their contracts don't change. One Undo takes it all back. `into.prefix` puts the moved entries under a path, so components that serve the same paths can merge; absorbing again later takes only what is new in the repo since (ADR 0193).
- **`atticus change sketch <change> --from strokes.yaml|.json`** reads the canvas's strokes from a file, applied in order, with the same preview and `--save`; an unknown component id gets the nearest ids as "Did you mean" (ADR 0193).

### Fixed

- **A gateway's target that is an estate service is asked about, never sent outside.** A forwarding call's host in a public-looking domain went outside the estate when no service served its whole path exactly as it is, though a service serves it under its own base path (`https://accounts-svc.forge-apps.example/accounts-api` to a service whose context path is `/accounts-api`). The path is now tried under each service's declared base (the manifest's `basePath`, a Route's or Ingress's path, a context path setting); when a service serves one of the paths the proxy sends to that URL under its base, the URL is that service's, and every route the proxy forwards there is asked about (`unknown-host`). A service's catch-all route under its base serves too (`GET /accounts/**` under `/ledger-api` takes `https://ledger.forge-cloud.example/ledger-api`); a gateway's pass-through route doesn't. A URL whose path is, whole, a service's own base is that service's though none of its routes matches, and though none of its entries is read yet (its context path setting alone), unless the base is a common one: an unrelated service whose context path is `/v1`, `/v1.0`, `/api`, `/rest` or `/services` leaves a vendor's `https://api.northwind.example/v1` outside.
- **A Kubernetes namespace is no public domain.** `forge-wallets.qa:8080`, `forge-wallets.dev`, `forge-wallets.app` and `wallets.forge.int` went outside the estate as public names. A two-label host now counts as public only under a common generic name (`com`, `net`, `org`, `io`…) or `example`; a country code, `dev`, `app` and the like need three labels (`api.northwind.dev`); `int` and `api` place nothing. Anything else is asked about.
- **A base URL whose host placeholder nothing sets keeps its path** (`https://${GATEWAY_HOST}/northwind-wallets/1.0`), so the proxy's BasePath joins the call, or a question is asked; the calls were left unasked.
- **A join by a proxy's BasePath stays verified only for a chained proxy; a caller whose host is the gateway's is joined by its host.** A call whose host nothing resolves is still joined by the BasePath, but as a guess (inferred, `joined-by-base-path` then `joined-by-path`), shown as found "by the proxy's BasePath; the caller's host is unknown" and correctable, and listed so, not as "their path alone", in a verified link's Also; a gateway's forwarding call that names no host (a chained proxy, Apigee's LocalTargetConnection) stays verified.
- **A verified link that merged a pairing is shown by the code's how.** A link verified by its host with another call the AI or a person paired said "The AI paired this call…" as ✓ Verified; now its how is the verified call's, and the pairing is an "also". A link whose only how is a pairing is never shown verified.
- **A deploy tool's token belongs to its own configuration and file.** The verifier refuses a token whose `env` or `target` isn't the configuration and file it sits in, and the join takes a token only for the file that writes the call's URL, whose element its XPath's root is (a `/ProxyEndpoint/...` token never replaces a target's URL). A `config.json` beside any of the org pack's `gatewayBundleFolders` (`sharedflowbundle` included) is read, not only `apiproxy/`.
- **A config fact's value must be the line's whole value.** `https://api.northwind.example` was accepted on a line setting `https://api.northwind.example.evil.example/v1` (YAML, properties and tokens alike). The same value written another way still holds: a properties value continued with `\` or with an escaped `\:` (claimed as written, `https\://` or `C:\\data`, or read, `https://` or `C:\data`), a YAML `''` or a `!!str` tag.
- **A placeholder call must cite code that passes the key's segment.** A README line or a log message that only mentioned it (`log.warn("createWallet failed")`) was kept as the call.
- **No secret gets past the verifier in a token's part or source.** A config fact under a secret's key with the credential in `cite.with` or `source` was accepted; the verifier refuses it (`secret-value`), never repeating it: no message of a config fact under a secret's key quotes its value, source, parts, quote or line (a part not on its line, a quote that doesn't match), and every message of a fact refused for a secret is redacted. Every message of a rejection now passes one sanitiser, whichever way it is shown (`<repo>.rejected.jsonl`, `<repo>.verify.yaml`'s `rejections`, the terminal, `--json`): secrets are redacted, a line the fact cites (or one either side) that holds a secret (a key naming one, whatever its value, or a value shaped like one) is never quoted, nor its value, and a config fact citing such a line is treated as a secret's own fact whatever key it claims (`forge.base-url` cited on `admin-password: Summer2024!`, `forge.tokenx` on `token: ${FORGE_TOKEN:changeme9}`); before, a quote that didn't match printed the cited line raw (`client-secret: …`, `password: hunter22`). Under a secret's key a `source` is a reference (`env:`, `vault:`, `${NAME}`, `${env:NAME}`, `${ssm:/path}`, `${file(./secrets.yml):db}`, `${NAME:?message}`, `${{ secrets.NAME }}`, `$NAME`, `%NAME%`, `#{…}`, `secretKeyRef:`, `file:`, `kvm:`…), a name as code writes one (`DB2_PASSWORD`, `forge-secrets-v2`, `clientSecretV2`), a file path, or the assistant's `literal:<file>` (`literal:Dockerfile`, `literal:App.config`), or it is the secret, however weak (`hunter22`, `p4ssw0rd`, `changeme123`, `Summer2024!`, a Basic credential written `basic:admin:hunter22`); a plain variable's literal default (`${DB_PASSWORD:hunter22}`, `${DB_PASSWORD:-changeme}`) is the secret too, a scheme's reference or a required variable's message never; and an AWS secret access key with a `/` in it is caught in a part. In a part a name is no credential, digits and all (`DB2_PASSWORD`, `OAUTH2_CLIENT_SECRET`, a Secret `northwind-oauth2` cited as `secretKeyRef`, `db2-credentials`, `forge-secrets-v2`, `secret1`): only a strong literal, or one with a password's marks (`Summer2024!`), that no name of the fact's own or its source gives is one. A `Basic` authorization value is a secret by its shape, in the verifier and in Trust's secrets check. The facts schema is beta.24's as released, so a verified beta.24 facts file is still read whole.
- **`<repo>.rejected.jsonl` keeps no secret.** A facts line refused for a secret (beta.24's value check included) was kept there verbatim; its value, source, parts and quote are now redacted, and a line that isn't JSON has them redacted, however weak, when its key names a secret (`"value":"hunter22" BROKEN`).
- **Trust's secrets check leaves tests and fixtures out** (`src/test/**`, `test/**`, `fixtures/**`, the org pack's not-product folders), the config files the source rules don't count included, as beta.24 left a test's env file out. An environment's folder named `test` (`env/test/kvms.json`) is the product's and still scanned.
- **An outside system the profile lists by its bare host takes a call whose host has a port** (`pay.northwind.example:8443`).
- **A proxy chains to a sibling proxy of its own gateway repo.** A LocalTargetConnection from one proxy to another in the same repo was never joined (they share the repo's component); each proxy now owns its routes. A call citing a `targets/` file is never given a `proxies/default.xml`'s token, or the reverse.
- **Real-shapes fixes in the verifier**: a Kubernetes list at its key's own indentation (`containers:` then `- name:`) keeps its key; a TargetServer's `host:port` cited on its host and port lines is kept; a target whose whole URL a deploy token replaces (`https://replaced_by_config_json`, path `/`) is kept.
- **Trust's secrets check finds more**: a blueprint property written value-first (`<cm:property value="…" name="ledger.password"/>`), a container env pair (`- name: NORTHWIND_CLIENT_SECRET` / `value: "…"`, said by its name), and a config file the source rules don't count (the Apigee config plugin's `env/<env>/kvms.json`).
- **The first placeholder in a value is the one taken**, whatever its form (`{{forge.ledger.base-uri}}/accounts/${header.id}` names `forge.ledger.base-uri`); a Camel Simple expression (`${header.id}`, `${body}`) is never a setting.
- **A library's "depends on" arrows are drawn from builds as people write them** (ADR 0192 addendum). A pom whose `<project xmlns=… xsi:schemaLocation=…>` start tag is written over two lines (the Spring Initializr and Maven archetype default) published nothing, so no service's arrow to it was drawn; a pom is now read as one text with its lines kept, so any tag, comment or CDATA section broken over lines reads, and a `${property}` in a group or name is resolved from the pom's `<properties>` or `<parent>` (every line it resolves through cited), or from a parent pom's `<properties>`. A module's parent pom is found through its `<relativePath>` (`../parent/parent.xml`, read as a pom), a pom whose `<modules>` lists it (`<module>../cards-api</module>` in a sibling `parent/` folder), or `../pom.xml`; a plugin's generated `dependency-reduced-pom.xml` and `.flattened-pom.xml` are no poms. `<scope>system</scope>` is recorded as `provided`, how it is drawn, so the facts file stays at version 5 and beta.24 reads every file beta.25 writes.
- **A dependency is its module's, and a module is a component only when the manifest lists it.** A dependency cited in `wallets-api/pom.xml` but said to be `wallets-batch`'s drew the arrow from the wrong box, and one that named no module was drawn from the repo's component; the verifier now refuses a component that isn't the build file's module (the manifest's `modules`) and gives one that names none its module. A repo whose manifest entry lists no modules ships as its one component: a multi-module build (`component: forge-cards`, its root pom listing `cards-api` and `cards-core`) is no longer split into components named for its folders, so the service box its endpoints are in gets its one arrow to the library, a dependency of one module on another draws nothing, and a dependency naming `forge-cards` on `cards-api/pom.xml` is kept. In such a repo an entry (or any other fact but a build's coordinates) naming a component the manifest doesn't is placed in the repo's component by `atticus verify` only when its folder is a module the repo's root build lists (`<modules>` in the root pom, or a settings include), said as a note (`componentsPlaced`, not a rejection), so its endpoints are in the box its depends-on arrow starts from; a monorepo's folders with builds of their own (a web app and an API) stay separate components, and a prototype's components stay its own. A root Gradle build's `subprojects { dependencies { } }` line is each subproject's dependency (the repo's component when the manifest lists no modules; one fact per component when it lists several).
- **A Gradle build is read with its blocks**: a `dependency`, `mavenBom`, `constraints`, `force` or `classpath` line in `dependencyManagement { }`, `constraints { }`, `resolutionStrategy { }` or `buildscript { }` is no dependency, nor is `url 'https://…'`; `integrationTestImplementation` and other test suites' configurations are test-scoped, `providedCompile` provided, `developmentOnly` runtime; a tool's configuration (`checkstyle "g:a:v"`, `pmd`, `spotbugs`, `jacocoAgent`, `detekt`, `ktlint`) is no dependency of the component. A multi-project Gradle library's subprojects publish under the names settings.gradle includes them by (`include 'x'`, `project(':x').name = 'y'`, a Kotlin `include(` over several lines), with the group the root build sets for them; a Kotlin string configuration (`"implementation"("g:a:v")`) is read.
- **A dependency declared through a Gradle version catalog is kept** (`implementation(libs.forge.ledger.api)`): cited on `gradle/libs.versions.toml`'s `[libraries]` entry with the alias's line in `cite.with`, its `version.ref` resolved from `[versions]`, its scope from the alias line's configuration and its module from that line's build file. A parent pom named `shared-pom.xml` (or `parent-pom.xml`) is a pom, and a `${project.groupId}` resolved to the group a module inherits needs no other line cited.
- **This link is wrong takes the arrow away.** A "depends on" link declared on more than one line (two module poms, a `<profile>`, `implementation` and `runtimeOnly`) offered only its first line, so the arrow came back after Apply; the form now lists every line with the coordinates it names, each chosen at first, and marks the chosen ones wrong in one write, all or none (POST /api/links/corrections), never one write per line. Two coordinates of one library component (`kit-api` and `kit-core` of `kit`) are one arrow, not two links of one id of which a view showed one; the arrow says a scope only when every dependency on it has that scope (a compile `kit-api` and a runtime `kit-core` say none). An arrow merged from more than 20 dependencies shows them all (GET /api/graph/edge-calls). Local API 3.11.0: a `depends-on` join's `dependency.facts`, each with its coordinates.
- **A facts file a newer Atticus wrote is read.** A fact of a kind this version doesn't know, or of a known kind with a value or a field it doesn't know (`scope: "shaded"`, a new field), is left out on its own and said ("written by a newer Atticus"), and `atticus verify --write` (and `atticus map`) keeps its line in the file as written, never moving it to the rejected facts, where the whole file was refused; the repo's read names it as why it is incomplete. A slip is no newer fact: it is refused, as any wrong fact is, so the assistant is told and writes it again, rather than kept unread for good. A value within two edits of one of its list's (`scope: "complie"`, `conf: "declard"`, `verified: "lnie"`, `by: "asistant"`) is refused saying the value it may have meant, and `scope: "system"` saying to record a system-scoped dependency as provided; a field one edit from one the kind has, starting with its letter (`pathh`, `scpoe`, `verison`), is refused, while a word of its own (`lines`, `scopes`, `repos`, `named`, `path`, `note`, `title`) is a newer field. Releases up to beta.24 still refuse a file holding a new kind; beta.25 writes none.
- A batch prompt carries the build coordinates' rule and the `artifact` and `dependency` lines only when it holds a build file (pom.xml, build.gradle(.kts), settings.gradle(.kts), a version catalog), not for every deploy file; a parent pom named for what it is and `gradle/libs.versions.toml` are read in the first batches with the other build files. The reading rules' words change, so their hash does (a build's and a framework's `component` is the one the manifest names, a module only when the manifest lists modules); no rule set is added, so nothing is read again for it.
- **A big build verifies quickly.** A pom's reading, its dependencies by line and a Gradle build's blocks are read once per file, not once per fact: a pom with 3,000 dependencies verifies in a few seconds.
- **The arrow's panel on the map is laid out, not jumbled.** A head with the arrow's name, its number of calls and Close stays in sight while its calls scroll; each call is a card (its method and path on one line, cut short when long with the whole text on hover, focus and Copy; its two ends; the verified or inferred chip beside them, never wrapped apart), its cited lines grouped under even labels, then This link is wrong and its form in place. The correction forms' choices keep each radio beside its words at every width. A declared topic's Undo is found on the system that declared it and lists only that topic's declaration; closing a correction form, or writing All calls on this arrow are wrong, keeps the keyboard focus (on the button that opened it, or on what was written).
- **The map's details panel for a box, and its popups, are laid out so nothing spills.** A box's name keeps its own case, with its kind, state and gaps as tags; its actions wrap (a long label no longer squeezed into a column over the facts) in two groups, what it does here and the pages it leads to; each section has the same heading and spacing, its lists are rows, and a gap's fix is a sentence, not capitals. Connect to… names both ends, lists each loose call as a row (method and path in mono, cut to one line and whole on hover or focus) instead of a table wider than the panel, and keeps Preview and Write at its foot; it and the other popups (Owner and system, Declare a link, Add to the picture) are brought into view with focus on their heading, and give focus back on Close. The bar of corrections not applied yet puts Apply beside what waits.
- **Trust's secrets check never shows a key that is a secret itself.** A credentials file's `AKIA…:…` line, an `.env` `AKIA…=…` line or a YAML `- AKIA…: x` took the access key id as the line's key, so Map › Trust and `facts/<repo>.verify.yaml` (committed) showed it. A key with a secret's shape, a run of 24 hex digits or more (in either case, digits and letters both), 16 letters and digits or more that switch between letter and digit (or case) at least every other character, or one piece of 32 characters or more (no `_`, `-` or `.`) of base64's, base64url's or hex's alphabet with digits and letters both, is now said as "a key that looks like a secret" (`key: ""` in the report), and Trust scrubs an older report's too. A key of words, abbreviated or not (`MSSQL_SA_PWD_2019`, `svc_acct_k8s_ns_1`, `spring.datasource.hikari.maximumPoolSize`, `x509Certificate2Path`), is shown as it is. That scrub is on screen only: `facts/<repo>.verify.yaml` keeps the key until the next `atticus verify <repo> --write` writes it again, and a key that was committed is in the repo's history, so rotate it. Local API 3.11.0.
- **A correction on file for a line the call has left no longer blocks a new one.** All calls on this arrow are wrong skipped an item as already on file when an older correction of the same fact said the same, even when it named the line the call cited before the code moved, so it met nothing, nothing was written and the arrow stayed after Apply. An item is skipped only when the correction its fact meets now (by its id and cite, as verify meets it) says the same; otherwise the new one is written and wins.
- **An arrow of more than 200 calls can be corrected.** POST /api/links/corrections took at most 200 items while GET /api/graph/edge-calls gives every call; it takes up to 5000 now, still all or none, in a body of up to 16 MB. Local API 3.11.0.
- **All calls on this arrow are wrong leaves another arrow's call alone.** On a line that holds two calls of one method to two paths, both were corrected; now only the arrow's own. On each of the caller's lines, the call of the link's method whose path (normalised as the join normalises it) is the link's, or ends with it at a segment (a link joined by a proxy's base path: `GET /api/ledger/accounts/{id}` joined to `/accounts/{id}`), is corrected when it is the only one. Where Atticus can't tell (no call on the line has the path, or more than one does: two hosts, or another arrow's call of that path), nothing is guessed: the form lists the calls of the method on those lines, each with its method, path, host and line, those of the link's path ticked, and writes exactly the ones the person leaves ticked.
- **The arrow's corrections answer as the API says.** A corrections file another process holds past the wait answers 409 with `written: []` (it was a generic 500); a problem's `written` is in the API's `Problem`; the 500's description no longer says every 500 wrote something; GET /api/graph/edge-calls requires `edge` and one of `node` or `focus`, and takes the node view's `depth` and `all` (an edge of a depth-2 or all view answered 404). One correction from its citation answers 409 with `written: []` too while another process holds the file, and an arrow of more calls than one request takes (5000) says so in the form instead of a bare 400. Local API 3.11.0.
- **Two spellings of one repo in one batch make one plan.** `forge-ledger` and `Forge-Ledger` in one request, on a file system that ignores case, were two plans from one file's text, so one repo's lines overwrote the other's; a repo is now the one its facts file spells (so a manifest's `forge-ledger` with only `Forge-Ledger.jsonl` there, on a file system that minds case, writes beside that file), else the one the manifests spell, and a name none spells is refused (404), as is one two facts files spell but for case (422). A facts folder's `.rejected`, `.reviewed` and `.questions` files are no repo's. One correction from its citation (POST /api/facts/{repo}/corrections) takes the repo the same way. Local API 3.11.0.
- **Corrections to apply follows every write.** The bar above the map reads again after a request that failed but wrote some files (a 500 naming them in `written`; the arrow's callers' facts are read again too) and on the server's file events, so a correction the CLI or an assistant writes shows without a reload: only for a corrections file, an answered question Apply reads or a repo's `verify.yaml`, once a burst. Events heard in the two seconds after the app's own write (its echo, or another writer's change at the same moment) are read once, when those two seconds end, never dropped.
- **Apply to every repo's map keeps its errors.** When it applied two repos of three (one with no clone here), the bar swapped it for the one repo's Apply and its error lines and Sync went with it, even mid-job, as each repo's verify read the bar again; the bar now holds the run, so it stays while it runs and, with its errors and Sync, until dismissed or the next Apply. Dismiss these errors takes them away with two repos or more left too, and puts the keyboard on the bar's Apply, or, when no repo is left to apply and the bar goes, on the page's main region.
- **Two corrections of one fact written at the same moment: the later line wins.** Verify took the older when two had the same time (one batch's, say); it now takes the one later in the corrections file, so the last of a batch's corrections of a fact is the one applied.
- The beta.24 entry for a link a gateway's routing made is corrected: it said the correction rests on the forwarding call's line at the hop, which beta.24 never does (a link with no line of a caller's never corrects the route's shared forwarding call).
- **Map › Trust never offers to delete a real system.** A box made from an answer's words is now only what a person's own words made: an answer whose words read as a sentence (a comma, a full stop ending a word, or two or more of a sentence's small words, as in "ignore this, its cicd related"), and the profile's system those words made (its name two or more of a sentence's small words, with no host of its own; a system a topics answer made from typed words, the topic under its `topics`, is one too). A system is no longer judged by the shape of its id, nor its name by its marks, so `payments-api-gateway-north-east`, `it-service-desk`, `plan-a`, `to-do`, "Northwind, Inc.", "Harbor Co.", a system the picker or an option added, and the outside system a hosts answer added for a long host (`api-payments-northwind-example-com`, its host as its alias) are never listed, and Ignore can't take them out of the profile. A sentence's small words are the language's function words only ("ignore" and "related" are a name's words). Typing a profile system's exact name, marks and all ("Northwind, Inc."), names it.
- **Putting a box right writes all or none, and only what was previewed.** Its answers and its profile are worked out and checked first; each file is locked against Atticus's other locking writers (an answer typed in the CLI or the app takes no lock), checked unchanged, and written whole and renamed into place. A failure puts back only a file that still holds what the fix wrote to it: a file someone else changed meanwhile is left as they wrote it and named, never put back over their change, and a file the fix wrote that someone changed after it (or that can't be put back) is named as left written (500, `written`). The preview returns a `stamp`, and a write with it is refused (409, nothing written) when a file it writes changed since the preview. In the app, a preview that answers after you switched from Ignore to Rename is no longer shown under "Yes, rename it".
- **A rename names something real.** Renaming a box to its own name (in its words, its id or its name in other case), or to another box made from words, is refused; a box is no longer counted as a system Atticus knows, and words that read as a sentence never name a known system by its name.
- **A system's name is lenient for names.** "IT Service Desk", "Plan A" and a five-word name are names; only what reads as a sentence (or is over 40 characters, or has marks besides letters, digits, spaces and dashes) is refused.
- **The join's questions read their host back.** A hosts question the join raised retires, saying why, once the join no longer asks about its host from any repo (the profile or the manifest names it now); before it stayed open. A token host's answer (Ignore too) is matched by its host in the repo, not by the lines it cited, so a line added above the call no longer asks it again. A token with spaces in it (`{{ vendor_url }}`) is read back whole, so its Ignore is honoured, it retires, and an outside system's answer names it.
- **Editing the profile keeps its comments and its formatting.** Taking a system out keeps the comment above it (a group's heading) above the next one; every flow list and map keeps the padding the file had (`[a]` stays `[a]`); and every profile edit (an outside system, a system named, a box put right) is written the same way, with no line folded, so a preview shows only the lines that really change. A file with no newline at its end keeps none, and an inline comment's spacing (`host   # the vendor's`) is kept on the lines an edit doesn't touch. Taking out (or renaming) a system that holds a YAML anchor other lines use (`&own`, used as `*own`) is refused with what to do by hand (422), where before it failed with a server error.
- **The AI never pairs a call on a host a person answered Ignore**, as it already didn't for a targets question a person answered.
- **An Ignore's reason is at most 300 characters** from every caller (`atticus questions answer --reason`, the library, and an Ignore given as words, `Ignore: <reason>`), as the app's API already took.
- **A join host question is read by its host whole.** A targets answer for a host with a `?` in it (`${A_URL ?? B_URL}`) is matched by the join's words for that host, not read up to its first `?`, so its answer (Ignore too) is honoured.
- **Local API 3.11.0:** the problem a 500 answers when a write was made only in part declares `written` (`[{ repo?, file }]`, the files left written).

### Changed

- The change diagram marks a component retired when the change removes every flow it has today (ADR 0193).

### Tests

- **A real-shapes corpus** (`fixtures/real-shapes/`): invented Forge repos written after the shapes of public Apache-2.0 projects (spring-petclinic, gs-multi-module, the Apigee deploy and config plugins' samples, api-platform-samples, camel-spring-boot-examples, okhttp's Kotlin DSL and version catalog, `helm create`'s chart, Kubernetes manifests), quirks kept byte for byte: `<project>` start tags over several lines, CRLF, a BOM, CDATA, Spring's Gradle `dependencyManagement`, `config.json` tokens per environment, a LocalTargetConnection, TargetServers and KVMs, Camel `{{property}}` URIs and a Fuse blueprint. Tests run the build-coordinates reader, the settings and secrets checks and the verifier on it, the estate and dependency joins against hand-checked goldens, and seeded property tests for the pom and Gradle readers and placeholder resolution. Where today's code is wrong the test keeps the right expectation as `it.fails`, so each fix flips it. The build-coordinates ones (multi-line `<project>` start tags, CDATA, Spring's `dependencyManagement`, a tool's configuration, test source sets, `shared-pom.xml`, a version catalog, `include` and `project(":x").name` artifacts) are flipped by the fixes above; the settings, secrets and gateway-chain ones stay.

## 2.0.0-beta.24 (2026-11-10)

### Fixed

- **Services that call a proxy through the gateway join its flows, in that direction.** The verifier keeps a call whose path is a setting's placeholder a helper puts together (a `@ConfigurationProperties` prefix, a map of named paths and the name a lookup passes) when the cited line is the code that passes the key's last segment and one cited file proves the rest of the key nests so; such calls were rejected, so only an AI pairing found them. A call whose host nothing resolves joins the proxy whose BasePath, the longest that does, starts its whole path (`joined-by-base-path`, verified), when no other service serves that whole path as it is; two flows under it, or such a service, are a question listing each.
- **A link with no line of a caller's never corrects the route's shared forwarding call.** A link a person declared offers to withdraw the declaration (its Undo, by the declared-links undo; one written by hand in the profile says to change it there), and any other (a gateway proxy's routing) says to correct the proxy's forwarding on the proxy's own box, as that changes the route for every caller. Before, "This link is wrong" wrote its correction on the gateway route's forwarding call, which moved or dropped the route for every caller and left the declared arrow drawn.
- **All calls on this arrow are wrong corrects every call on it**: every call an Interface merged, each on its own line (only the first was corrected, so the arrow stayed after Apply); every call on an arrow of more than 20 (the view lists 20; the form reads the rest, GET /api/graph/edge-calls); the count it says is the number of calls it corrects; it names the arrow by its boxes' names; and What moved follows each line corrected.
- **All calls on this arrow are wrong writes all or none, and never twice.** Every corrections file involved is held for the whole batch, each written whole and renamed into place; a failure puts back what was written, or answers naming exactly the repos whose corrections are on file. A correction whose newest line on file says the same already is skipped (`skipped`), and once written the form offers no second submit until it is opened again.
- **A placeholder call must cite the code that makes it.** A call whose path is a setting's placeholder (`${forge-remote.uris.createWallet}`) was kept when it cited only the settings line that defines the key, with any method, or a line making no call with every piece of the key a part, or a commented-out call, or pieces of a hyphenated segment from unrelated lines; the key's parts were never checked to nest. Now the cited line is code in no settings file naming the key's last segment in its own case, no comment, and one cited file proves the nesting (YAML indentation, a properties line with the whole key, or the `@ConfigurationProperties` prefix and its field); otherwise it is rejected. A word built from `cite.with` parts always leaves some of it to the cited lines, and `-` no longer splits one.
- **A config fact's value and key are checked on its line.** Its `value` was never compared with the line, and a key under the wrong parent passed, so the join could put an invented value into the catalog. The value must be what the line sets; a YAML key must sit under the parents its dotted key names, a properties key must end with it, and a deploy tool's token must have its XPath and value in one token object of the bundle's `config.json`.
- **No credential from a deploy tool's token reaches the facts file.** A key naming a secret in an XPath (`Header[@name='x-api-key']`, `AssignVariable[Name='private.clientSecret']/Value`, `…/KeyStorePassword`) wasn't recognised; it is now, and the schema also refuses a config value shaped like a secret (an access key id, a URL's password, a bearer token), never repeating the value. The map rules say to record a token only when it sets a URL, a host or a path. The reading rules' hash changes, so `atticus map --all --stale` tops up the repos read before.
- **Trust's secrets check reads the files secrets are most often left in**: `.env*`, `secrets/` and `credentials*` files are scanned for the report (still never a fact's citation, their lines held only for the check), and so are XML (Maven's `settings.xml`) and `.config` files (a `web.config` connection string's `Password=`, an `appSettings` key). A value shaped like a secret (an `AKIA…` key, `jdbc:postgresql://user:pass@host`, `Bearer eyJ…`) is caught whatever its key, and a config.json token's key is its XPath. A reference is no secret: `classpath:`, `file:` and a file's path (`/etc/forge/api.key`, `keys/jwt.pem`).
- **A gateway proxy that fronts a system outside the estate goes there, never back to the services that serve its paths.** A forwarding call whose target `<URL>` is a build token (`@name@`) is resolved from its own bundle's deploy tokens (the Apigee config plugin's `config.json`, read as one setting per token: its XPath, value, target file and environment). It goes outside, named by the profile's outside system that lists one of its hosts, else by the host, when the profile lists one of them, or when every environment's host is a public name in a registrable domain no estate host is in (`co.uk`-style suffixes counted) and no estate service serves the whole path it sends. Anything else is asked about, as before: a host in the estate's domain, one an estate service may answer on, and one Atticus can't place (one label, a Service and its namespace, `.corp`, `.local`, `.svc`, an address, localhost). A forwarding call whose host nothing resolves is asked about, never joined by its path any more (beta.23 joined such a proxy to whichever service served the same path, backwards).
- **A link is never shown "✓ Verified" beside a guess's words**: a link with no way it was made but a guess (by path, a host's label…) is inferred in Why is this linked?, and a verified link keeps the words of the call that made it verified, the guesses its other calls rest on given as its join's `also` (local API 3.10.0), never as the reason.
- **A link a gateway's routing made says where to correct it**: when its caller side cites no call's line, "This link is wrong" says to correct the proxy's forwarding on the proxy's own box, where it said "No line cited at this end" and offered nothing. (Corrected after release; see beta.25.)
- A value written `{{name}}` is resolved through the setting of that name, as `${name}`, `env:name` and `@name@` are; a host still written as a token is no host.
- **Two proxies in one gateway repo never share their deploy tokens.** Each bundle's `targets/default.xml` takes only the tokens of its own folder's `config.json` (and, where a token's lines name its placeholder, only that placeholder's), so one proxy's host never joins the other's calls.
- **A proxy chained to another (Apigee's LocalTargetConnection) joins it.** A gateway's forwarding call that names no host joins the proxy whose BasePath starts its path, else is joined by its path or asked about, as in beta.23: never dropped without a word.
- **Nested BasePaths** (`/northwind-wallets` and `/northwind-wallets/1.0`) take the longest, as Apigee does, where the join gave up.
- **A gateway repo read before beta.24 says how to pick up its tokens**: the question about a forwarding call whose target URL is a token nothing sets says to read the repo again with your assistant (Read with my assistant), which records the deploy tool's tokens.
- **The join of a large estate finishes in seconds.** It looked at every entry for every call; it now looks candidates up by the path's shape and the repo, with the same result (40 repos of 250 calls each: about 2 s, where it didn't finish in 10 minutes).
- **An answer's words are never drawn as a box.** A join question (hosts, targets, an ambiguous call, an unknown host, topics, the estate's call-outs) answered in a person's own words that name no repo, component or system (say "ignore this, its cicd related") made a system box of those words, with arrows to it. Such words are refused now, with what to do instead: pick a repo or system, choose "An outside system (new)" and type its name (a short name: letters, digits, spaces and dashes, up to 40 characters, not a sentence), or choose Ignore. "An outside system (new)" on a hosts question still names the system by its host.
- **Every join question takes Ignore: not a real call (e.g. CI/CD, a test, a template)**, with an optional reason, in the app and with `atticus questions answer <id> --ignore [--reason "..."]`. The join leaves those calls (as "None of these" does), draws no box for them, and doesn't ask again; the answer says "Ignored by <person>". No contract changes: Ignore is the option it adds to a question asked before it, or the words `Ignore: <reason>` (question files stay at version 6). Local API 3.10.0: `ignore` and `reason` on POST /api/questions/{id}/answer.
- **Map › Trust lists the boxes already made from an answer's words** ("A box made from an answer's words: <name>": a system of a profile whose id or name reads as a sentence, or an answer's words that the join took as a target), each with **It was 'ignore this'** (its answers become Ignore and the system leaves its profile) and **Rename to a real system**, each previewed first and written here, then Apply and Propose for review. Local API 3.10.0: GET /api/trust's `answerBoxes`, POST /api/trust/answer-boxes/ignore and /rename.
- **The estate join's questions are question files, on the Questions page and Home, not only a count.** A host no repo, component or profile system is known by, and a call more than one entry could answer, were counted by verify ("N asked") and never asked, so a person working in the app never saw them and the calls stayed loose. Now each write (atticus verify <repo> --write, Apply in the app, with its lines in the Console) raises them, once per subject: a gateway's forwarding call to a token target URL no file sets (`@targetVendorUrl@`) is a targets question in the join's words, saying to read the gateway repo again, with the estate's repos that serve HTTP, an outside system (new) named by the token, None of these and Ignore; any other host is a hosts question when none asks about it yet; a call two proxies' equal BasePaths (or an entry serving the whole path) could answer offers each candidate with the line it serves it on. The next write honours the answer (the repo picked joins, the outside system named takes the calls, Ignore leaves them, answered none), and an open one the join no longer asks (a read since recorded the token's values) retires, saying why.
- **No hosts question about a "host" read out of a build token.** `@targetUrl@` in a call's URL was asked as "Which repo answers on targeturl@?", whose answer wrote that into the manifest; such questions retire, and the join asks about the calls instead.
- **A hosts question answered Ignore leaves the calls on its host**, as a targets question's Ignore does.
- **New hosts and targets questions offer Ignore from the start** (it was added only when picked).
- **A library of the estate's own is drawn as what its services build on, not as one more service** (ADR 0192). A service whose pom or Gradle build declares a dependency on coordinates another repo of the estate publishes now has a dash-dot **depends on** arrow to that library on the Architecture map, aggregated at system level like every arrow and never merged into its calls or their counts; a vendor's or an open-source library, which nobody in the estate publishes, draws nothing and asks nothing, and nor does a test-scoped one. Why is this linked? shows the coordinates, the dependency's line in the depending build file and the lines that publish them, and This link is wrong marks that dependency wrong (verify leaves it out and the arrow goes). The library's own calls stay the library's. The reading rules tell the reader a library it builds on is a dependency, never a call; their hash changes, so `atticus map --all --stale` tops up the repos read before with the new rule set (`build-coordinates`) alone.
- **Why is this linked? says what a verified link's other calls rest on**: the guesses its merged calls were joined by (`also`, local API 3.10.0) are a short "Also:" line under the call that made it verified, never its reason.

### Added

- **Build coordinates and dependencies are facts** (facts schema 5, ADR 0192): an `artifact` fact is what a component's build publishes (a pom's own `<groupId>` and `<artifactId>`, a module's group from its `<parent>`, a Gradle project's `group` and name), a `dependency` fact what it builds on (a `<dependency>` in `<dependencies>`, a Gradle `implementation 'g:a:v'` line), each cited on the line that writes the name with the group's line in `cite.with`. The verifier reads the build file to check the name is the project's own (an artifact) or a dependency's (never a `<dependencyManagement>` entry or a plugin's), the group's line is cited and says the same, and the scope is the one declared; it notes the coordinates a read build file writes that no fact records (`buildUnrecorded`). Facts files written before read unchanged. Local API 3.10.0: a graph edge may be `depends-on`, its join's `how` `coordinates` with `dependency` (group, name, version, scope).
- The `fixtures/library-deps/` fixture: an invented library of the estate's own, three services that build on it (a single pom, a multi-module Maven repo with a test-scoped module, a Gradle build), and a vendor SDK two of them build on too.

- **All calls on this arrow are wrong…**: one button above an arrow's calls in its Details, and one small form: they all go to another box, to an outside system, or none is a call. One submit writes every call's correction, each repo's lines in one write of its corrections file (calls from several caller repos write each repo's), then Corrections to apply takes over. The per-call "This link is wrong" is unchanged. Local API 3.10.0: POST /api/links/corrections.
- **Map › Trust warns of a secret in a config file**: verify notes each config file's line that holds what looks like a live secret (`api_key`, `client_secret`, `password`, `token`… with a value that is no placeholder or reference, or a value shaped like a secret), by file, line and key, never its value; Trust shows "A secret sits in <file>:<line>". Local API 3.10.0: GET /api/trust gains `secrets`.
- The `fixtures/gateway-facts/vendor-proxy/` fixture: an Apigee proxy to an invented vendor, and two services calling it through the gateway with their settings laid out two ways.

## 2.0.0-beta.23 (2026-11-10)

### Fixed

- **A lock left by a crash never blocks a stage, its thread or the stories for good**: the lock of a quick write is stale after two minutes whoever holds it (on Windows a crashed process's id is soon another program's), and Propose for review never lists a lock file.
- **A story the business analyst adds while the stories are drafted never shares a number with one the draft gives**: the draft's numbers are reserved before the model is asked.
- **The Console doesn't blank a hand-off's log while its Done prints**: lines a running Done adds are added to what is shown, and the old lines stay until a new version is read.
- **A product owner's or business analyst's Home still says what their own install needs.** The alert that a newer Atticus upgraded this metarepo and a failing "This machine has what Atticus needs" step were inside the closed "Setting up Atticus" fold; both are shown above it now, the step with a link to Settings › This machine. The rest of setting up stays folded.
- **Home and the setup checklist read again when a step's job ends.** After Sync cloned the repos into the workspace (which no file event names), Home and the checklist kept showing the step to do until the page was reloaded; they now read again when a job ends, and the checklist when files change too.
- **The machine's setup step points at the page that has its checks.** It said "Set up › Doctor", which isn't a page; it now leads with Settings › This machine and Check again (the terminal's `atticus doctor` after), and the checklist links to it.
- **Your changes knows your roles on every change in flight.** Your roles were read only from the first five changes' question panels, so a sixth change you own wasn't in Your changes, the fold or the findings' level; the changes list now says your roles on each (local API 3.9.0). A change in its post-release review counts as in flight there too, as it does in Your changes.
- **Home follows the catalog where the org pack's layout puts it.** Home read the counts, the graph and the map again only for files under a folder named `catalog` or `org-pack`, so a catalog moved by `layout.catalog` never refreshed them; each file event now says whether it is the catalog's, by the layout (local API 3.9.0).
- **"Waits on" names the owner however change.yaml writes the role.** At a glance and a hand-off's reviewers read only the exact owner key, so a change naming its team lead as `tech-lead`, a key in another case (`Product-Owner`), or a custom role of roles.yaml waited on nobody named, though Your changes showed it as that person's; they now read those keys too. Developers and the delivery lead are never taken for the tech lead.
- **Ask and search read inputs/ with the checks a sync makes.** With no snapshot of inputs/ yet, Ask Atticus, knowledge search and a section opened in place read its files as they were, so a page above the classification limit, under view restrictions or with front matter that doesn't read was shown; such a page is now left out, as `atticus sources sync` lists it and doesn't read it.
- **The command shown under an answer or a search can't run anything you didn't mean.** `atticus knowledge ask "…"` and `atticus knowledge find "…"` were built from the question with double quotes, so a question from a link holding `$(…)` or backticks ran code when the command was pasted; only letters, digits, spaces and plain punctuation are kept in the quoted words, safe in bash, zsh, PowerShell and cmd.
- **A passage shows only what the page shows.** A section of a wiki page or inputs/ file showed HTML comments, hidden or aria-hidden elements and display:none text as what the page says, and a page's YAML front matter as its first section; both are left out now, and line numbers stay the page's own.
- **"This link is wrong, it goes to X" sticks**: a person's edit of where a call goes was ignored whenever the call had a pairing, even the AI's. It now stands over any AI pairing and over an older pairing of a person's, and AI matching (Match with Copilot, a correction's spread) skips the calls a person said go elsewhere.
- **A line dropped on a box on the estate view opens Connect with that box picked**: a system holding one repo means that repo, and Connect to… now holds it (and the boxes outside the opened one), so the drop never opens with nothing chosen.
- **A line to or from a system with several repos says what to do**: it was swapped into an outside system and the panel said "No outside system of the profile is …"; it now says to open the system and draw from one of its repos, and Connect to… lists such a system by none of its repos.
- **A system box names every repo drawn in it**: one whose System record lists fewer repos than it holds no longer reads as one repo, so a line drawn on it never silently means only that repo.
- **A line dropped on the inspector, the Connect panel or the minimap connects nothing**: it connected to the box hidden beneath.
- **A line drawn from a box's incoming dot means the other box calls it**, as a drop on a dot always did, also when dropped on the box's body.
- **Undo after "rule for all" says what it didn't undo**: the pair lines are removed, and the panel now says the target question's answer stays and how to take it back.
- **A slow write in the VS Code panel is no longer "the app isn't answering".** A task run's Done (build, fix, commit) or putting work items in a live tracker could take longer than the 30 s the extension waited, and the page then showed the app as down while the write went through. Done and the work-item sync now run as jobs the Console shows (API 3.9.0: `job: true`), the page follows them to their answer, a write waits up to 10 minutes, and a running app that is slow says it is still working instead.
- **Run all ready tasks and Scans finish in the VS Code panel.** They learned a job ended only from live events, which the panel never gets, so they said "working" for good; they now also ask the job how it stands.
- **The Console's entry for a hand-off run stops saying "Waiting for your assistant" after Done.** Its lines are rewritten as the run ends, and the page only added past what it held; an entry now says which version of its lines it gives (`rev`), and the page reads them again when that changes.
- **A panel no one is looking at asks seldom, and a failed start isn't retried on every ask.** The Console asked every few seconds even in a hidden panel, and each ask started `atticus ui` again after a failed start, filling the output channel; a hidden page now asks once a minute, and after a failed start only Atticus: Open the app (or five minutes passing) starts it again.
- **Sync the repos says why it failed**: the app showed only "failed", or the repos it synced, and its progress said Synced either way. Its result now gives the code and the errors, the app shows them, and a failed sync says Stopped.
- **No clone of a repo here ends at Sync the repos, not a command**: Apply, Apply to every repo, the map's corrections bar and Read with my assistant ended at "clone it (atticus workspace sync) or give its folder with --dir". In the app they now offer Sync the repos for those repos (API 3.9.0 `next`); the terminal keeps its words.
- **What a Done resumes shows in the Console and runs alone**: the next batch's prompt, or a read's `verify <repo> --write`, ran after Done's Console entry had finished, beside Apply, Propose or Bring in. It now runs as its own local job, its lines live, and the other local steps wait for it.
- **A read run with the server's model is put on the map**: Run with the server's model on `map <repo>` wrote the facts but never ran `verify <repo> --write`, so the map didn't change. It now does after a read that kept every fact, as a hand-off's Done does.
- **The local steps' buttons say what to press, not what to type**: Apply to every repo, Join the estate's flows and the other local steps showed "atticus spec draft <repo>" or "/atticus-map <repo>"; they now name the app's pages and buttons.
- **Propose for review fails when its branch wasn't pushed**: on a manual Git host it said the PR was a step for you on Home, for a branch the Git host didn't have. Now nothing is proposed, the job fails with the push's reason, and no step is added.
- **A reply sent while a stage's prompt waits is read by the next round**: it wasn't in the prompt the assistant answered, yet reading that answer in marked it read, so no round ever saw it. Only the answers the prompt carried are marked read now (the loop file keeps them as `carried`), for the proposal's thread too.
- **One answer is never read in twice**: Done and the app's own check at once, a second Done, or the command line's `--resume` and then the app's could each resume the same run. A stage refuses a resume of a run it isn't waiting on, Done stays busy until its resume has run, and the command line's own `--resume` forgets the resume the app kept.
- **A reply or a kept question during a long run stays**: the run wrote its loop file whole and dropped a reply posted meanwhile, or brought back a question a person kept; the thread and the run now take the file's lock, and the run keeps what the thread added.
- **"Send and start the next round" says the reply is saved when the round doesn't start**: the box clears and the thread shows the reply, with why the round didn't start, instead of keeping the words as if nothing was sent.
- **The thread's "checked by" says who ran the last check**: a run the command line validated, only resumed by the app, no longer reads as checked by the app, and a person's Done after the app's failed check takes the app's mark off.
- **A reply sent twice in a few seconds is kept once**: the same words from the same person, still unread, are the reply already on file.
- **The business analyst's shaping of the stories reaches the tech lead.** `stage/stories-shape.yaml` now rides with every hand-off beside the stories' numbers, so after the plan PR merges a clone shapes the stories, tasks.md and each task's context the same way (it built the stories unshaped). The stories stage also records the file at its path in the change folder, so a later run sees whether it changed.
- **A rename, split, merge or added story recorded while the stories are drafted is kept.** The draft wrote back the shaping and the numbers it read when it started, so the business analyst's shaping was lost and the number reserved for it could be given to another story; both files are now written over what is on file, one writer at a time (a lock across the app and the CLI), and a shaping the draft didn't read waits for the next one.
- **No two tasks of a story share a number.** A task moved to another story kept its number there even when a new task of that story had been given it (two 1.3s), and could take the number of a removed task; a moved task's number is now kept in the stories' numbers, the next free one comes after every number the story used, and a task that stays in a story keeps its number when another moves out.
- **The stories' shaping in the app no longer shows a split or added story twice**, nor says falsely that it has no task yet: the shaping was applied to stories that already had it.
- **`atticus upgrade` rewrites question files at version 5 to version 6**, as it does older ones, and its summary says version 6.
- **Accepting a suggested owner no longer clears an owner someone else just named.** Accept sent all four roles from the page's copy; it now sends only the role it accepts.
- **A blocking business finding on the brief stays on the product owner's Home.** Words a brief uses of the business too (an alert, monitoring, a queue, a topic, an interface) made such a finding design; on the brief, only the architecture lens or words no business sentence uses (a timeout, a retry, a circuit breaker) do now.
- **A story the business analyst added can be merged with a drafted one.** It was refused as another repo's work, as an added story has no repo until it is given a task.
- **The person raising a hand-off is never named its reviewer, whatever the case of their handle**, and a next-role owner written in another case or with `@` is still told the hand-off waits on them.
- **A Console log no longer freezes when the server dropped lines**: when the server answers from a later line than asked (a long run's log keeps only its last lines), the Console says how many earlier lines were dropped and goes on from the server's lines, asking on until it has caught up.
- **A Copilot ACP step's progress shows in the Console**: its "copilot: …" lines go to the command's run log and the app job's lines as well as the terminal, so a long Copilot step no longer looks idle.
- **A burst of output past 5,000 lines reaches the Console, to its end summary**: a command's log is tailed from where it was read, a few thousand lines at a time and on at the next look, instead of jumping to the file's end.
- **A long run stays in the Console while fifty quick ones run beside it**: a run still running (its process there) is never pruned from `.atticus/logs/`, and the Console's list always keeps what runs, so the strip doesn't vanish behind a hundred newer entries.
- **A chatty command no longer makes the app's server busy**: the watcher tells the Console about a command's run files at most every 200 ms (and once after the last write), and the logs are read only from where they were last read.
- **A run whose process id was reused is shown ended**: on Linux the run keeps when its process started and a different process with the same id no longer counts; elsewhere a running command writes its record every 30 seconds and one silent for over five minutes is shown as ended without saying.
- **The raw editor checks a file whatever the case of its name**: `Runtime.yaml` or `Profiles/forge/repos.manifest.yaml` was written unchecked (no schema, no secret or model-address check), and on Windows or macOS that is the same file Atticus reads. A name in another case is now refused, naming the right one.
- **A raw manifest edit can't list a repo id another profile lists**: it was written, and then every command that reads the repos stopped. It is now refused, naming the manifest that lists it, as adding repos does.
- **An adapter form never overwrites a newer edit**: the form follows runtime.yaml when it is read again while you haven't changed it; if you have and the file changed meanwhile, the page says so and the write is refused (409) instead of putting the older values back. Local API 3.9.0.
- **An adapter's other settings keep their types**: `strictSsl: false`, `retries: 3` and `orgs: [forge, forge-labs]` were written back as the words `"false"`, `"3"` and `forge, forge-labs`. Values now read as YAML does, and a setting you didn't change is sent as the file had it.
- **Setting an adapter changes only its lines**: its comments were dropped and the file's `[claude-code]` became `[ claude-code ]`; the entry is now edited in place and the file's own flow style is kept (adding repos to a manifest keeps it too).
- **The raw editor can save a fix over a runtime.yaml that doesn't parse**, and content with an empty adapter (`gitHost:`) is refused with what is wrong instead of "Something went wrong".
- **Add a wiki never writes a user name or token into sources.yaml**: an address such as `https://oauth2:<token>@host/…` was written as it was. It is refused (in the app and `atticus sources add`), saying to let Git's credential helper or an environment variable give them; the write is now atomic and refused when sources.yaml changed meanwhile.
- **Discover a folder of clones works with several platforms**: it always failed, asking for a `--platform` flag the app has no way to give. The page now asks which platform first and reads the folder for it.
- **Home reloads fast on Windows too**: Windows reports a file only read as changed (its last-access time), so Home reading its files threw away every answer it had kept, and a reload with nothing changed took over a second there against 0.3 s on Linux. The app's server now counts a change on Windows only when a file's size or times moved since it last saw them (or since it started), and Map › Trust and the cockpit judge a base clone by the files that say what is checked out, never by folders' times git moves as it reads.
- **Questions are worked out again once a base clone moves**: `/api/questions` was kept until a file the server follows changed, though its checked proposals read the code they cite in the base clones; it is kept by the checkouts' stamp too, as Map › Trust and the cockpit are.
- **The cockpit sees a commit on a task or change branch**: the checkouts' stamp read only the checked-out branch, so a commit in a change's or task's worktree (or a fetch of the change branch) left the cockpit as it was; it now stamps every `task/…` and `change/…` ref file, here and as last fetched, and each worktree's HEAD and reflog (files only, never folders).
- **`atticus skills import` names a file it kept with forward slashes on Windows too**, as every other line it prints does.
- **Nothing scrolled to hides behind the Console bar**: a Tab to a button or a link to a heading stops clear of the bar fixed at the foot of the app (or of the open Console), not under it.
- **The app builds on Windows again**: two files of the canvas differed only in the case of their names (`tools.ts` and `Tools.tsx`), which Windows can't tell apart; one is renamed, and the portable-paths check now refuses such a pair.
- **Home folds set-up away once, not three times, for a product owner or business analyst**: how steps run, the checklist, an upgrade and the estate sit in one "Setting up Atticus" fold; the upgrade's fold showed empty when no upgrade waited.
- **The map says when corrections wait to be applied, with Apply right there**: a bar above the estate canvas (and a change's Architecture tab) names the repos whose corrections aren't in the map yet, with Apply to every repo's map when there are several, and goes once they're applied. It reads again after every change the app writes.
- **After correcting the map, one Apply for every repo you corrected**: Home's list of what waits for review offers Apply to every repo's map when more than one repo has corrections (a line drawn between two boxes corrects both), beside each repo's own Apply. Every Apply links its Console entry while it runs.
- **Set up's "Choose how AI runs here" is open again on a new metarepo**: `atticus init` now writes the manual git host into runtime.yaml, and the step read that as a choice made. Adapters aren't how AI runs, so the step ignores them.
- An Apigee proxy links to the backend endpoint it forwards to:
  - **The route's path suffix is added to the target's path.** A forwarding call recorded with the target `<URL>`'s path only (`/forge-fees/1.0/fees`) was matched as it is, so the proxy's link reached the service with "No line cited at this end". The join now adds each of the proxy's routes' suffix (the part after its `<BasePath>`), as Apigee does, and links each route to the entry that answers it, citing the route's line, the target's `<URL>` line and the entry's; a `<BasePath>/**` pass-through links to every entry under the target's path (at most 50), each by the entry's own method. A forwarding call already written with the suffix keeps it, and `target.copy.pathsuffix` set false keeps the target's path as it is (and is cited).
  - **The service's base path is taken off before matching.** Its entries may leave out the path they sit under (`/fees/retrieve` under `/forge-fees/1.0`): the base is read from the manifest's `basePath`, an OpenShift Route's or an Ingress's path, or the service's `server.servlet.context-path` and `spring.mvc.servlet.path` settings, in that order, and the link is verified when all its lines are read from code. With none of them, the longest part of the target's path that starts the entry's is taken, as a guess: inferred, flagged `joined-by-path-overlap`, with what closes it on the canvas (give the repo its `basePath`).
  - **A caller of the proxy's route is walked on to the service's entry**, and a forwarding call that names no host, base or target (the route's own "forward GET /retrieve") no longer stops the walk when the proxy's target call says where.
  - `/atticus-map` asks for the target's `<URL>` or `<Path>` path as written when the suffix can't be put on, `target.copy.pathsuffix` as a setting, and a context path's setting with its whole key (the servlet and WebFlux base paths too) whether or not the entries' paths include it; the verifier keeps them as settings (golden `fixtures/gateway-facts/passthrough-suffix/`).
  - **A flow is matched by its whole URL.** When nothing names the backend's base path, a flow whose URL (the target's path and the flow's suffix, `…/fees` + `/retrieve`) ends in exactly one endpoint's whole path (`/fees/retrieve`) at the backend its host names is linked to it, verified, not a guess. The match must include part of the target's own path, so the proxy's `GET /ping` (sent to `…/fees/ping`) is never linked to the backend's root `/ping`. Only a pass-through's base path is still a guess.
  - **A base path the service declares that doesn't fit the URL is never matched past.** A service whose context path, manifest `basePath` or Route path the target's URL doesn't start with was still linked by its whole URL, verified, taking any front of the URL as its base; it is now at most a guess (inferred, flagged `joined-by-path-overlap`).
  - **A flow is never matched by its suffix alone.** A forwarding call written with the suffix on (`…/forge-fees/1.0/fees/ping`) was linked, verified, to the service's `/ping` with `/forge-fees/1.0/fees` as base; the matched path now keeps at least one of the target's own segments, a forwarding call the suffix joins nowhere is no longer joined by its path's end, and a service given two bases by URL matches has neither verified.
- The canvas, drawing a line (found by the person using it):
  - **A line dropped anywhere on a box connects to it.** It connected only when dropped on the box's small dot, so a drop on the box did nothing.
  - **A line drawn from or to a system that holds one repo is that repo's.** On a canvas showing systems, every drawn line was dropped silently.
  - **A line that can't connect says why** (two systems with several or no repos, or both ends in one repo) instead of doing nothing.
- App-only audit (tasks, release, archive):
  - **A change is never released without its tasks' code.** A task drafted and committed on `task/<change>/<task>` but never raised or merged let Mark done, Build ready and Raise the release PRs pass, and the release PR carried only the hand-down commit. While a task branch has commits the change branch hasn't, here or on the Git host, Build ready and go/no-go are held (`task-not-merged`) and no release PR is raised for its repo, each naming the task and what to do (Build and raise PR, raise its PR, merge it). Mark done on such a task warns on its card and asks for the person's reason; the decision is kept in `tasks/<task>/decision.yaml` and holds only while the branch is where it was decided. The Release page says so, and the release PR's words are now true. Local API 3.3.0.
  - **A task branch in a second repo is held too.** Task branch names are the same in every repo, so a task's unmerged branch in a repo other than the one its run recorded was matched to that record and skipped.
  - **A commit pushed to a task branch after its PR merged holds the change.** A task ticked because its PR merged was never looked at again; now only what its PR merged (the commit its run raised) is let go.
  - **Home's cockpit never waits on the Git host.** Assessing a change in build or release (on every Home load) ran `git ls-remote`, and sometimes `git fetch`, per repo, waiting up to 30 s each when the host couldn't be reached; it now reads the branches here and the host's as last fetched, and only Sync and the release steps ask the host.
  - **An unverified task can be built and raised from the app.** Once the build command is set on Set up › Repos, an UNVERIFIED task's card says so (not the old text) and offers Build and raise PR (built where it was drafted, then its PR raised on the person's confirmation, ADR 0186, without running the AI again) beside Run with AI again; a task a person took over gets Build and raise PR once their fix is committed. Run all ready tasks says where an unverified task's step is.
  - **The archive is all or nothing, and a delta it would refuse is caught when drafted.** A MODIFIED delta for a spec openspec/specs doesn't have passed `openspec validate --strict` (OpenSpec says so only as INFO) and failed at the archive with a bare 500, leaving change.yaml archived and the folder unmoved. The plan hand-off, the archive's own check, and the stories and small stages' OpenSpec gate now refuse it with OpenSpec's words and what to do; the change drafter's prompt names the specs that exist and asks for ADDED for any other, and its checks refuse the rest; an archive OpenSpec refuses anyway puts the change back as it was and says why (422, never "Something went wrong").
  - **A manual Git host's release and task PR steps move things on.** Recording "Merged on the Git host: yes" on a release PR's step syncs the change (its release PRs read back), and on a task PR's step settles the task (ticked once merged), as the hand-off's steps already did; a "Post a comment on the PR" step left open once its PR is merged or closed is closed with why.
  - **A hand-off branch whose push failed is kept, and pushed again from the app.** The publisher makes the local branch before the push, so "push it yourself" names a branch that exists; the change page says the branch isn't pushed and offers Push again (the person's confirmation, never forced), saying git's words when it fails.
  - **Preparing an AI step again cancels the older waiting run.** A second Prepare left Round 1 waiting for ever on Model runs; the older open run of the same step is now cancelled, saying which run superseded it.
  - **Set up › Scans names the built-in readers as a cross-check.** "Scan now" is "Check with the built-in readers", says what it is, says what it added, changed and removed (its Console entry too), asks host questions only for the repos the assistant has read unless the person ticks to ask for every repo, and links to Map › Trust to read again with the assistant. The note that a scan or sync started goes once its job is DONE.
  - **Bring in what merged says what it does, and fixes a missing upstream.** It shows that it is fetching, with its Console entry, and on a branch that follows no remote branch while the remote has it, offers Set the upstream to `origin/<branch>` (a local setting).
  - **A rejected fact no longer holds a repo without a way out.** Map › Trust says nothing is written to the catalog while a fact is rejected, and each rejected fact offers Drop this fact: the person's correction that it is wrong, with their reason, then Apply writes the rest.
- Review round 50 (knowledge):
  - **A quote matches whole words.** "safe to deploy" was taken as on a page that says "Unsafe to deploy", and "The timeout is 5" on one that says 500 ms; every match is now tried, and one starting or ending inside a longer word or number is not one. The conflicts' quotes, the values' grounding and the kept items' re-check all go through it.
