  - **An also-on quote is checked again when its page changes.** A rescan kept every also-on quote of an item whose main page didn't change, even when its own page now said something else or was gone; each also-on quote of a changed page is checked against the new snapshot (line and snapshot updated), and one no longer on its page is dropped with its evidence, the item kept.
  - **An id another page's item has is refused.** An item the AI wrote with the id of a kept item of another page (or another source, scanning one with `--source`) overwrote it, and a person's review then applied to a different item. The scan hands it back ("id x is used by a.md; choose another"), and writing a scan refuses one that slipped through, listing it among the failed items (`knowledge/scan.yaml`).
  - **A rejected item stays out after a rescan.** A rescan that changed a rejected item made its review stale, and the item came back into plans and answers; a stale rejection now keeps it out, listed as "rejected, page changed", until a person checks it again. A correction made before the item changed is no longer applied silently: it waits too ("correction made before the page changed"). An item's flags and tags are no longer part of what a review checked, so a conflict found or gone no longer turns reviews stale; a review written before, whose hash had them, still reads as current.
  - **A conflict with a page not read again stays.** A rescan of one page dropped every conflict with it and compared only the items it read; the check now compares the items read again with the kept items of the same kind and title, an old conflict stays unless both its items were read again and no longer differ (or one is gone), and every item's conflict flags are worked out again, so none names a conflict that is gone.
  - **Two pages never share a path or a document id.** A Confluence export's "C# coding standard" and "C++ coding standard" were both `C-coding-standard.md` (one copy over the other); a colliding title takes its page id (`C-coding-standard-111.md`). A SharePoint export's `x.aspx`, `x.html` and `x.markdown` beside `x.md` take their extension (`x.aspx.md`), `x.md` keeping its path. A snapshot lists a page path once (a second page with it is skipped, with why), and pages whose names slug alike ("Retry Policy.md", "retry_policy.md", "retry-policy.md") get document ids of their own (a short hash). A path or id no other page has is unchanged.
  - **A page's status banner is a banner.** Any of a page's first six lines that began with deprecated, archived, retired… marked it so, headings and sentences too ("# Deprecated API removal standard", "Archived ledgers are kept for seven years."); now only a banner counts: a blockquote or admonition, the word followed by `:`, a dash, a full stop or "by …" ("Superseded by …"), "Status: Deprecated", or "This page is deprecated…". A fix to the C6 helper `pageStanding`, as the knowledge guide documents it; its schema is unchanged.
  - **A page superseded by one that didn't change is superseded.** A rescan reads only the changed pages, and `superseded-by` (and the AI's superseded page) was resolved among them only, so the old page's items stayed current; the newer page is now found among every page of the snapshot.
  - **A mirrored page is read by a copy that can be read.** The copy to read of a page mirrored in two wikis was picked before the restricted, classification and front-matter checks: a restricted standard's copy was skipped and the team's readable copy skipped as its mirror, so neither was read. The copy is now picked among those that pass the checks.
  - **A correct quote of a formatted page is taken.** Bold, emphasis, `code`, links, inline HTML (`<b>`), entities (`&amp;`, `&nbsp;`), zero-width spaces, en and em dashes for "-", "…" for "..." and setext headings made a quote of the visible words fail; the page and the quote are folded the same way.
- Review round 50 (getting started):
  - **`atticus repos discover` keeps a manifest's entries as they are.** Its proposal wrote each existing entry again with some of its fields (hosts, basePath, description, dialects, extractors and sparse were dropped) and at schema version 1; it now appends to the file as `atticus repo add` does, keeping every entry, comment and the file's version, and writes every field a new entry has. A new manifest is written at the current version.
  - **`atticus repos discover` reads a clone's origin as Git does** (`git config`, local, no network): an Azure DevOps URL with spaces (`https://dev.azure.com/forgeorg/Forge Payments/_git/forge ledger`) is no longer cut at the first space, `pushurl` is never taken for `url`, an ssh URL keeps its user (`ssh://git@host:22/...`) and a Windows path's backslashes aren't doubled. Only an http(s) URL's credentials are dropped. `workspace sync --from` then points each base clone at the whole URL.
  - **A new metarepo or adopted repo has nothing to upgrade.** init and adopt wrote repos.manifest.yaml at version 1, so doctor said "2 upgrade steps to run" right after the documented setup; adopt also left `.atticus/metarepo.yaml` unstamped and `.atticus/.gitignore` without this machine's files. Both write the current versions, adopt writes the whole local-only ignore list and records the Atticus that adopted the repo (only when no step would touch the repo's own files).
  - **`atticus doctor` runs on a runtime.yaml that isn't YAML.** The embedding check threw on the parse error and doctor stopped; the file now reads as unconfigured there, and the Org pack check fails naming the first problem (`runtime.yaml: can't read YAML: ...`).
  - **`atticus repo add` to the `repos: []` init writes adds one entry per block**, not the whole list rewritten on one line (a team added to `teams: []` from the app's owner screen too).
  - **`atticus repos discover` with two or more platform profiles asks for `--platform`**, as `repo add` does, instead of making `org-pack/profiles/default/`; a profile folder it makes gets its `profile.yaml`, and `atticus orgpack validate` notes a profile folder that has none.
  - **A found repo whose id the manifest lists for another repo is added under its own id** (`orders-api-2`) and said, instead of left out without a word; the same repo (the same clone URL, written another way) is still left out. The repo-id helpers moved from the CLI to `@maycombhq/workspace` so discovery and `repo add` share them.
  - **Files keep their line endings.** `atticus skills write` leaves CLAUDE.md alone when there is no team-instructions block to write or remove (it rewrote the trailing newline and said "wrote CLAUDE.md"), and writes the block in the file's own endings; init and `workspace sync` add their `.gitignore` lines to a CRLF file in CRLF.
  - **`atticus adopt` names `atticus assistant-files` before the line about `/atticus-map`**, since adopt writes no slash commands.
  - **runtime.yaml's `modes.default` defaults to the first mode `modes.allowed` lists** (the admin guide's `modes: { allowed: [hand-off, copilot-acp] }` failed validation). A loosening: every file that validated reads as before (C6 spec page regenerated).
  - `atticus plan snow` without a change id gives an `atticus plan snow ...` example (it named `plan stories`), and a repo link pasted from the browser (`.../_git/forge-ledger?path=/src`) gets the id `forge-ledger` in `repo add`, not `src`.
- Review round 50 (planner):
  - **A story or task keeps its number when the stories are drafted again.** They were numbered afresh each run, so a repo the architecture stage added (sorting before the others) moved every story and task after it: a task run under `tasks/<label>/` belonged to another task, a story file was replaced under a new name (a person's edit lost), and tasks.md's ticks and corrections landed on other tasks. The numbers are kept in `stage/stories-numbers.yaml` (it rides with every hand-off): a story is the same one by repo and flow; a step by today's step it changes or removes, or, added, by its kind, the step it goes after, the component it calls or table it stores in, and its ordinal; a task by its first step, else a step it covered. New ones get the next free number, a removed one leaves a gap, never reused. A removed story's file a person edited is kept and said, and a task run whose label now names another task (a change drafted before the numbers were kept) is said, never silently reused. Stage messages name a task by the label a person types, with its id when they differ ("Task 2.2 (id 2.3) has no acceptance criteria").
  - **proposal.md has one owner per section.** The feature stage writes it and the stories stage only its Capabilities, each keeping the other's; a file another stage wrote as it is now is no person's edit, so the feature stage no longer takes proposal.md for a person's draft and `atticus critic act … edit-and-apply` no longer refuses every edit as one to a rendered file.
  - **A tick in tasks.md isn't an edit of the draft.** Every edit check (the stage loop, stage/drafted.yaml, the critic's edit-and-apply) hashes tasks.md with its boxes aside, one helper (`editSha`); a hash kept before still matches.
  - **A step's words on two lines stay on one task line.** A multi-line or CRLF `what` broke tasks.md's checkbox line (the story went to the next line, and its ticks were lost); a task's title and words are collapsed to one line.
  - **Two repos' migrations of one name are two.** The deploy order keyed migrations by file name, so two repos' `V2__init.sql` were one; they are keyed by repo and file, and a missing one is named with its repo.
  - **Two flows' requirements in one capability are told apart.** A merged spec delta gave both the story's name and `openspec validate --strict` refused the duplicate; the later one is named with its flow.
- Review round 50 (confirmations):
  - **Done never runs a command the assistant wrote.** The app kept a prepared step's resume as `resume.json` in `.runs/<change>/<run>/`, which the assistant writes, and Done ran whatever it named through the command line: `change handoff … --approve`, `change sync --approve`, `digest --send`, `task run --level pr --approve`, all on the session token alone. The resume is now kept in the person's own state folder (`$XDG_STATE_HOME/atticus`, `%LOCALAPPDATA%\atticus\state` or `~/.local/state/atticus`, per metarepo), made from the words the step was started with, used once, and checked before it runs: a step that takes `--handoff`, its arguments, and no option but `--handoff`, `--resume <this run>` and `--source <wiki>`. Anything else runs nothing and says so. A `resume.json` in the run's folder is never read. (`atticus handoff done` runs no resume, so it had nothing to change.)
  - **A model run the app starts sends the key only where you confirmed it.** `POST /api/runs`, a task run or the story writer in model mode and Run all ready read `org-pack/runtime.yaml` from the working tree, so an edited `baseUrl` got your key; Test trusted settings committed to HEAD, which the assistant can commit. The address you last confirmed (a save or Test in AI settings with your confirmation, or a step you ran in model mode in your own terminal) is kept outside the metarepo; a run the app or a Copilot session starts somewhere else is refused before any provider is made (403 in the app, exit 2 in a session) and says to press Test in AI settings. With nothing confirmed yet, only a built-in provider's own address (Anthropic's or Bedrock's, no `baseUrl` or `caFile`) is taken. Scripts and CI run as before.
  - **The VS Code panel's buttons keep working after the app restarts.** A restarted `atticus ui` has a new page key, and an open bundled panel kept the old one, so every write button was 403 until the tab was closed; the extension now asks for each confirmation with the running app's key.
  - **A sync another process runs is waited for, not taken over, and the app doesn't hang on it.** A base clone's lock held by a live process on this machine is never taken over for its age (a long clone outlasted the 30 minutes and a second git ran in the folder); another machine's still goes by age. Open the workspace and Run with AI wait two seconds for it, then answer 409 "A sync of <repo> is running; try again when it finishes." instead of waiting up to 30 minutes.
  - **`atticus doctor` sees a key kept with `atticus secret set`.** The model check looked only at the environment and warned that the key wasn't set; it now asks the credential store too, as the providers and the AI tab do, by name only.
  - **Send it to me names where it posts, and your yes is for that channel.** The button says "Send it to <channel>", and its confirmation names the channel; the server reads the channel again when it sends and refuses a yes for any other one, so a channel changed in `.atticus/me.yaml` after you looked posts nothing. Saving the channel still needs no confirmation: it sends nothing, and the file it writes is one the assistant can edit anyway. Local API 3.1.0.

### Added

- Roles and the BA (ADR 0191, local API 3.8.0, question file version 6, roles.yaml version 3): a change goes product owner → architect → business analyst → tech lead, each answering at their level.
  - **A question goes to whoever answers what it asks.** The AI tags each question business, design or delivery when it raises it; business goes to the product owner (the business analyst from the stories on), design to the architect, delivery to the tech lead. The feature stage's "What happens when the account has too little money on the day?" is the product owner's, not the architect's. _Send it to_ on a question (`POST /api/questions/{id}/route`, `atticus questions route <id> --to <role>`) moves it, saying from whom, by whom and when.
  - **A design finding never holds the brief.** Each finding says what it asks; Home shows the product owner and the business analyst only the business ones (and how many design ones wait for the architect), and in Define only a business finding makes a change BLOCKED, so Home and the brief's gate agree.
  - **The business analyst shapes the stories in words.** On the Stories tab: rename, split, merge (stories of one repo) or add a story. A rename and a merge keep the number; a split's new part and an added story take the next free one, reserved at once in `stage/stories-numbers.yaml`. The next draft maps each task to its story (kept in `stage/stories-shape.yaml`), a moved task takes its new story's next number, and the stage refuses a story left with no task; the critic's tasks-cover-stories still checks the plan.
  - **Owners suggested from roles.yaml.** roles.yaml version 3 says who takes each role (a team's `roles`, the file's `defaults`); a new change's page suggests them, each accepted with one click. The owners form opens on _Change who is on it_, so the page has one Save at a time.
  - **Hand-offs name their reviewers and tell them.** The next role's owners review each hand-off (the architect the brief, the business analyst the design, the tech lead the plan), named with their role in the PR body (it said "None"), and told through the notify adapter (manual: a step). At a glance says "Waits on: @ari (architect)", or that nobody is named.
  - **Owner questions are sent.** _Send to <owner>_ posts an owner question through the notify adapter on your confirmation (a message, ADR 0186); manual notifications make it a step; with none, it says to copy the text, as before.
  - **The stories reach the work tracker.** The Stories tab lists the feature, each story and its tasks as work items and puts them in the tracker on a click: with a manual tracker (ADO) a step each with the title, description, acceptance criteria and parent to copy and the id to record; a live one on your confirmation. The ids are kept, so a later update changes the same items (the `atticus ado sync` code path).
  - **Knowledge answers, and opens in place.** Ask Atticus answers "How do I request a firewall rule?" from the knowledge items and the wikis' and inputs/ pages, each quoted with its page (no AI wording; `atticus knowledge ask` is offered). A change's suggested knowledge is named by its title or heading and opens in place (Read it here), the path under Details. Knowledge › Search reads inputs/ when it has no snapshot yet.
  - **Plain words for the product owner and the business analyst.** The brief is a tab of a change and a form, one box per section with its hint, writing the same brief.md; file paths are under Details; findings name the review question ("Is it complete?"), not the lens or rule id; "AI lens", "story bar", "knowledge gap" and "Apply puts it in the map" are gone from their screens; the Questions page's empty text no longer talks of scans.
  - **Home by role.** A product owner or business analyst sees Your changes with whom each waits on, their questions and their findings; how steps run, the set-up checklist, upgrades and the estate's checks fold under _Setting up Atticus_.
  - Small: "Write the brief on Feature brief" and "Name its repos on its brief" pointed nowhere (a change's repos now include its target flows'); the Questions page's repo filter no longer lists the change's id.
- **Draft the stories again works in VS Code's bundled panel too**: the extension passes `--force` on that one step, once, as the app's server does.
- Every daily step has a button (ADR 0188, local API 3.5.0): a person never types a command for a step of the day; where the app takes the step, its command is folded under "In a terminal".
  - **Read a repo with your assistant from the app.** Map › Trust, Home's Run next, Onboarding and Add to the picture offer Read with my assistant (or Run with the server's model, or Copilot): Atticus writes the prompt, and Done checks the answer, takes each batch and, after the last, puts the kept facts on the map (`verify --write`), saying what was kept and rejected. Read again and the rejected and unknown files' lists have the same button.
  - **The fast path's step** (`change small`) is Prepare or Run on Home and At a glance.
  - **A correction on the canvas ends with Apply and Propose for review.** Declare a link, Add to the picture, Owner and system, Connect's pairings and their Undo said "Written. Run this to redraw the map:" with `atticus verify … --write` and a git commit; Apply redraws the map and Propose opens the PR, the commands folded. The Change diagram's Draw it, Change › Knowledge and a stage review's comment offer Propose for review instead of "Copy the git command".
  - **An answer is applied, not read again.** An answer to a question a read raised offers Apply (`verify <repo> --write`), and Propose stays on the question, after a reload too, while its file isn't proposed.
  - **Apply, Sync and Join are buttons wherever they were commands:** Onboarding's apply, sync and Sync all wikis; Home's setup (Sync the repos, Read a repo on Map › Trust); Map › Flows' Join the estate's flows; Set up › Scans' Clean up finished worktrees (uncommitted work kept, as the command line keeps it).
  - **Copy task context for my assistant** beside a task's next step, instead of `atticus task context`.
  - **Draft the stories again** when the stories stage refuses over stories a person wrote, instead of "run `atticus plan stories … --force` in a terminal".
  - **Prompt history's recording is a toggle** (Turn recording off / on), instead of `atticus history off`.
  - **An empty catalog offers Map › Trust** (Canvas, Explore, Home, Ecosystem map, Design) instead of "Read a repo first: atticus map <repo>…".
  - **A stage that hasn't run points to its tab** (Feature design said "run atticus change architecture … In a terminal… No slash command does this step yet"); a finding that holds the gate goes to the change's page; reasons that named `atticus finding …`, `atticus todo` or `pass --repo` are in the app's words.
  - **Manual steps, Release, Scans and Prompt history are tabs of Home, Change and Set up** by default, not only under More pages.
- A stage is one conversation (ADR 0189, local API 3.6.0):
  - **Each stage of a change is one thread on its tab.** The proposal (and a small change's one step) on At a glance, the feature stage on Feature design, architecture on Architecture, stories on Stories: the prompt for your assistant with Copy, its answer and what the checks said, what the stage wrote, what the critic found (Reply, Make the fix, Ask the owner, Not relevant), the questions answered in place, your replies and corrections, in order. **Tell the assistant…** at the bottom sends your words and starts the next round; they reach the next prompt as your answer. No trip to Set up › Model runs (it stays the administrator's view); prompt sizes, models and file paths are under Details. `GET` and `POST /api/changes/{changeId}/stages/{stage}/thread`.
  - **The answer arrives by itself.** The app notices your assistant's answer file once it is complete (unchanged for a few seconds, and reads as an answer) and checks it as Done does, once, then the step goes on; Done stays. The prompt's last line asks only for the file: "Atticus notices the file and checks it. There is nothing to run."
  - **One paste per round.** A hand-off round has no separate context-expander (or rerank) prompt: the estate's names the input uses are read without a model.
  - **No question dropped in silence.** A question the assistant raised whose line didn't match is shown ("1 question couldn't be kept") with Keep it anyway, citing the nearest line.
  - **Make the fix where the assistant can't.** A finding about a file the stage's assistant doesn't write opens that file in the app's editor at its line; saving records the fix.
  - **The proposal drafter reads the attached files**, as the later stages do.
  - **Planned components are known.** A component the change's diagram draws as new is "planned in this change" to the architecture and stories checks and the critic, and the deploy order may name it, marked planned. The canvas gains **A component retired**, and resets "The API doesn't exist yet" after each stroke.
  - Fixed on the way: Done's lines no longer tell you to run a command with `--resume`; the critic's note says an AI lens waits only when one does.

- Set up from the app (ADR 0190, local API 3.7.0): "Edit, then Propose". Each edit writes its file through the code its command uses (comments, order and line endings kept), shows the lines it changes before Write it, and offers Propose for review after.
  - **Create the metarepo here.** `atticus ui` in a folder that isn't a metarepo shows Create the metarepo here (name, platform, owner, the metarepo's repo on the Git host, read from the clone's origin), which runs init's own code, or Open another folder (the app for that folder at its own address). Home then continues with Onboarding.
  - **Add repos in the app.** Set up › Repos › Add repos takes pasted clone URLs (one a line, up to 500) or a folder of clones (repos discover's code), shows each URL normalised (credentials and a browser link's `?path=` taken out), what the manifest lists already, and an id clash given `-2` with why; tick, say what each is if you like, preview and write the manifest. Each repo has Detect the type (the org pack's repo types, or what its files say) and its type set from there, `prototype` included.
  - **The outside systems.** An Adapters panel on Set up › AI and Settings sets the Git host (manual, GitHub, Azure Repos, GitLab, Bitbucket: the types runtime.yaml's schema takes), the metarepo's repo and the protected branches, the work tracker and service desk (manual by default, for an ADO or ServiceNow with no API access), notifications, the wiki and the design tool. It never takes a secret: a setting named like one, or a value that looks like a token, is refused; a token is kept with `atticus secret set` in a terminal.
  - **Settings edited in the app.** Settings says "Edit, then Propose": runtime.yaml and gates.yaml have Edit in app (the raw editor, checked with the file's schema, onto the file as opened), and Set up › Org pack adds an integration pattern and a wiki through forms, and any org-pack file in the raw editor. A raw edit that moves where the model's key goes needs your confirmation (ADR 0186).
  - **AI decides in a fresh metarepo.** `atticus init` writes runtime.yaml at version 4 and the Git host as `manual` (with the metarepo's id when known), so Propose for review and the first hand-off work with no YAML edit. Turning AI decides on lifts an older runtime.yaml the way `atticus upgrade`'s runtime-v4 step does; before, a version 2 file was sent to an upgrade `atticus upgrade --check` never listed. The AI page offers an upgrade step runtime.yaml waits on, from the same check.
  - `GET/POST /api/setup/metarepo`, `POST /api/setup/open`, `POST /api/setup/repos/plan`, `POST /api/setup/repos`, `POST /api/repos/{repoId}/type/detect`, `PUT /api/repos/{repoId}/type`, `GET /api/setup/adapters`, `PUT /api/setup/adapters/{kind}`, `GET/PUT /api/setup/file`, `POST /api/setup/patterns`, `POST /api/setup/sources`, `metarepo` in `/api/health`. Guides: install ("Minute 5"), admin ("The setup checklist", "Outside systems").
- **One Console for everything Atticus runs (ADR 0187, local API 3.1.0).** The Console at the foot of the app is always there (Open the Console): everything Atticus is doing or did lately on this metarepo, newest first, wherever it was started, with its kind, title, where it was started (this app, a terminal, VS Code, IntelliJ, a Copilot session, your assistant), state and time, and what it printed, live, with follow, search and copy. A Copilot run keeps its tab per session inside it. The strip under the top bar shows whenever anything runs (not only a Copilot run), how far, with Show in the Console and Stop (a terminal's run: stop it in its terminal, Ctrl+C).
  - The app's jobs stream their lines as they print them (a scan, a sync, an AI step, Apply, Propose, Bring in, upgrade, conformance, a Match, Run all ready tasks); the critic, a wiki's sync, a team's tool and a run's Done run as jobs so they show too, with their answers unchanged. Scans, Repos' branch scan and a Match link to their Console entry.
  - A long command run in a terminal, by an editor or by a Copilot session (`atticus map`, `map --all`, `verify`, `scan`, `workspace sync`, `task run`, `task run-ready`, `knowledge scan`, `upgrade`, `handoff done`, `index rebuild`, `reconcile`) keeps what it prints, redacted as the log is, in `.atticus/logs/runs/<run>.log`, and its command words, ids and flag names (never their values) and how it ended in `.atticus/logs/running/<run>.json`; the last 50 runs, about 2 MB a log. Quick commands keep nothing; `ATTICUS_RUN_LOG=off` turns it off. The app follows them live; one whose process is gone shows as ended without saying. VS Code and IntelliJ tell the commands they run who started them.
  - A hand-off run waiting for your assistant is an entry saying where its prompt is and how to finish it; once Done runs (in the app or `atticus handoff done`), what it printed is in the same entry.
  - `GET /api/activity`, `GET /api/activity/{id}/lines?from=n`, the live events `activity.changed` and `activity.lines`, `title` on a job and the job kind `work`. Guide: troubleshooting, "Where to see what Atticus is doing".
- Home loads fast: on a 40-repo estate (about 2,500 records, 12,000 facts, 200 open questions) the first Home after `atticus ui` starts took 25 s, a Home with nothing changed 4 s and a Home after one file changed 3 to 6 s; with the bundled embedding model installed, the first two Homes took 55 s and 27 s. They now take about 5 s, 0.3 s and 1 s (test: `packages/cli/test/home-speed.test.ts`, budgets 15 s, 1 s and 2 s).
- Home loads fast: **Home never waits for the machine's checks.** GET /api/setup ran `atticus doctor` (Git, the OpenSpec CLI, the Copilot language server and its ACP handshake, the embedding model), seconds on Windows; the checklist's machine step now says it is checking, and fills in when GET /api/doctor answers. `?machine=wait` waits as before (local API 3.1.0).
- Home loads fast: **no page embeds while you wait.** Every catalog request (the counts, the graph, search) embedded up to 500 nodes with the search model before answering, loading the model again each time; the server now embeds in the background, a few nodes at a time between requests, with one model per process.
- Home loads fast: **the index isn't checked against every file on every request.** While the server follows the metarepo's files it checks them after a change it hears (or a write through the API, or a job that finished), and at least every 30 s.
- Home loads fast: **files are read once.** Each question file, the org pack's files, every record's schema version (the upgrade check) and each repo's facts are read and parsed once and kept until the file changes; while nothing in the app is writing, a file isn't even checked again until the watcher says it changed (and then only that file or folder) or 30 s have passed. Asks of one file at once read it once.
- Home loads fast: **answers kept until something changes.** The upgrade check, the questions, the owner questions, the triage list, Map › Trust and a change's cockpit are worked out once and kept until a change is heard (Trust and the cockpit also until a base clone's commit moves), so twenty requests at once no longer read the same thousands of files twenty times.
- Home loads fast: **Propose for review's list asks Git a few times, not twice per file.** With answers proposed and not merged yet it ran `git rev-parse` and `git hash-object` for each file on every Home (hundreds of git processes, seconds each on Windows); now once per branch and once for all the files.
- Home loads fast: **doctor's probe no longer reloads Home.** The writability check's `.atticus/.doctor-<pid>` file was sent as a file change, so every machine check made Home read everything again.
- Home loads fast: **a burst of file changes reloads Home once.** A scan or an assistant writing many files sent one event per path and Home asked for everything again for each; Home (and every panel that follows the files) now waits for a quiet moment, and asks for the counts, the graph and the map only when the catalog may have changed. The map is asked for once what needs you has loaded.
- Home loads fast: Map › Decisions reads the catalog through the record cache, as every other page does.

## 2.0.0-beta.22 (2026-11-09)

### Fixed

- Review round 46 (Windows and editors):
  - **A secret's file can't be cited through an NTFS stream or a trailing dot.** The verifier refuses a cited path with a `:` anywhere (`.env::$DATA`, `certs/server.key::$DATA`) or a part ending in a dot or a space, reading `\` as `/` first (so `..\x` is outside the repo too); the secret-file rule reads each part as NTFS opens it, as a defence.
  - **Ticking a task in a CRLF tasks.md ticks it.** The line was matched without its `\r` but rewritten with it, so nothing changed while hand-up said "marked done"; the box is ticked and the `\r` kept. (A `.gitattributes` for new metarepos is left for a later change: creating one changes what `atticus init` writes.)
  - **A cited path is stored as the repo lists it.** On Windows `SRC\App.ts` opened `src/App.ts` and was accepted as written; each cited path (cite, parts, auth, scopes, callers, steps, error paths) is mapped to the one listed file it names ignoring case and `\`, before the carry's changed-file check and coverage, and a path naming no listed file is `no-file`.
  - **Branches differing only in case get folders of their own.** `Feature/Login` and `feature/login` shared `.branches/<repo>/Feature-Login` on NTFS; a name with capitals now has a lower-case folder with a short hash of the exact name (`feature-login-1a2b3c4d`), and a lower-case name keeps its folder. A checkout under the old name moves (`git worktree move`) on the next checkout, and a branch scan under `catalog/branches/<old name>/` is still compared until the next scan of the branch moves it.
  - **No id is a Windows device name.** `slugify` and the fact-id slug suffix `con`, `aux`, `nul`, `prn`, `com1`-`com9` and `lpt1`-`lpt9` (`aux` is `aux-1`, `con.v2` is `con-1.v2`), as Windows can't create `aux.yaml`. C1's RecordId is unchanged, so a record already named so still reads.
  - **`atticus onboard accept` retries the move into the org pack** (dialects and frameworks) when Windows holds the file for a moment, as Atticus's other moves do.
  - **Citations are found in a task run's worktree** (`workspace/.worktrees/<change>/.tasks/<task>/<repo>/`): the editors' gutter and `atticus cites --file` named no repo there.
  - **`atticus task run-ready <change>` runs.** The `task <change> <task>` shorthand read `run-ready` as a change and ran `task run run-ready <change>`.
  - **A Copilot session can't act as you through `atticus legacy`.** `legacy questions answer`, `legacy finding`, `legacy baseline`, `legacy knowledge verify` (and `legacy ui`, `legacy map --all`) are refused in the session's command check as the commands they name; the CLI already refused them.
  - **VS Code types the atticus you set.** A command typed into a terminal starts with the atticus `atticus.path` names (`node "<bin.js>" …` or the program's path, after `&` in PowerShell), not a bare `atticus`.
  - **The VS Code Atticus tab frames the app in a remote window.** The page's frame-src is the origin of the address VS Code gave (localhost, or the forwarded https host), not only `http://127.0.0.1:*`.
  - **Prerelease versions are ordered alpha, beta, rc, then the release** in VS Code and IntelliJ: 2.0.0-rc.1 counted older than 2.0.0-beta.21.
  - **VS Code starts the app again when `atticus.path` or the metarepo changes**, stopping the old one, and an open Atticus tab opens again on it.
- **The IntelliJ plugin names the brief step** ("Write the brief of <change>") as VS Code does. Its words check failed the beta.21 editor-plugins build, so beta.21 has no IntelliJ or JetBrains files attached; beta.22 carries them.
- Review round 46 (app):
  - Map › Decided by AI keeps what Undo did (removed and put back, the lines it left, and the Apply or Propose next steps) after the reload marks the batch undone, until you close it.
  - Opening a question on Questions keeps the role filter: the question's link carries the role, and only a new change or role in the address resets it.
  - Answering several questions at once keeps its result ("Answered N" and why each other failed) after the answered ones leave an open list, and lets the answered ones go from the picks.
  - The canvas drops a focus answer that is older than the last one asked, or from before the lens changed.
  - The AI's decisions load one at a time: a burst of file changes asks once more when the load out lands, so an older answer never lands last.
  - Questions' counts follow the role filter (`GET /api/questions?role=` counts that role's questions, as it lists them; `counts.byRole` stays every role's).
  - The artifact editor shows the saved text right after Save and saves next against the file as saved, so a quick second edit no longer gets a spurious "changed meanwhile".
  - The Task board keeps each task's message until that task's own state changes, not on any file change.
- Review round 46 (storage):
  - **A repo's corrections are written one process at a time.** A line the app appended (Connect, Correct this) while a CLI command rewrote `<repo>.corrections.jsonl` (`atticus decide`, Undo of a batch, the `corrections-v2` upgrade step) could be lost. Every append and rewrite now takes a lock file beside it (`<repo>.corrections.jsonl.lock`, created only when free, retried briefly; one whose process is gone, or older than 10 s, is taken over), as well as the queue within one process. It works the same on Windows.
  - **`atticus verify --write` writes whole files.** The facts file, `<repo>.rejected.jsonl`, `<repo>.verify.yaml` and the sample audit's `<repo>.audit.yaml` are written beside and renamed into place, so the app or another command never reads one empty or cut short.
  - **Two commands' model runs in the same second get two run ids.** Each command counted from 0001, so the second run's folder overwrote the first's. A run id now ends in a random tail (still counted on a test's clock), and the run's folder is claimed with its id; a hand-off run claims its folder the same way.
  - **One `change.yaml` that doesn't read no longer fails the app's Changes, Findings and record pages.** The change list skips it; `atticus change list` names it on standard error.
  - **A facts file cut short in the middle of a JSON array** is refused as not JSON (the repo shows as read incompletely) instead of failing the read.
  - **An answer never overwrites one given meanwhile.** Answering, retiring or reopening a question, and refreshing its options, write only over the file as they read it; otherwise nothing is written (409 in the app). The AI's batch leaves such a question to the person ("a person changed it meanwhile") and goes on with the rest.
  - **A history record after a hand-edited line** goes on a line of its own when the file's last line has no newline.
- Review round 47 (outside content):
  - The task context (`atticus task context --markdown`, the task_context MCP tool, `/atticus-task`) puts the story, the acceptance criteria, the flow's words and each knowledge item in `<atticus-data>` blocks, screened with suspicious lines marked, and leaves a high-risk item out for a person; titles, steps and page titles stay on one line, and the prompt's code fence is longer than any backticks in it, so wiki text can't forge a "How to work" section. `atticus task run` keeps wrapping the whole file as one source.
  - An API wiki's restriction and labels are merged into a page's own front matter, never dropped by it: a restricted page with front matter of its own is skipped, not snapshotted.
  - The knowledge scan's instructions no longer carry wiki page paths: the paths shared by two sources and where text was cut out go to the model JSON-quoted in data blocks.
  - Secrets are redacted anywhere on a line: a secret-looking key's quoted value in declarations, JSON and arguments, setters, shell exports, flags and connection strings, a URL's password, bearer and basic tokens, and GitHub, Stripe, AWS, Slack, Google and JWT token shapes; code that only names a secret word (`if (password == null)`, `tokenizer.next()`) is unchanged. The verifier drops a stored quote that still holds an opaque literal beside a secret word.
  - A component type's name and notes file are JSON-quoted in the data block, and `<` is escaped in every quoted name, description and signal, so none can close the block.
  - A hand-off PR body gives each open finding's summary in inline code, on one line, with its secrets redacted, so it can't mention people, draw an image, link or start a section.
- Review round 47 (catalog and rules). Ids are derived differently for the routes, deployments, settings and names below (no schema change), so stored catalogs for the repos that have them change on the next scan:
  - A route parameter with a constraint or an optional marker (`{brandId:int?}`, `{slug:regex(^[a-z]+$)}`, `{code:regex(^\d{3}$)}`) is cut to its name before the query string is dropped, so its Interface is no longer refused by C1 and dropped, and two such routes are no longer one Endpoint. Interface ids and Endpoint paths follow the same rule as a call's path.
  - Catalog, projection, merge, file-listing and dialect-rule order no longer depend on the machine's locale: `localeCompare` is replaced by a code-unit comparator (`cmpText`, and `cmpNatural` where line numbers sort as numbers) in core, scanner and extractors, and ESLint refuses `localeCompare` in core, scanner, verify, extractors and release.
  - A critic round writes `state: in-progress` to its `meta/round.yaml` first and the finished record last (an additive, optional field); the gate stops on a newest round that is still in progress, or has neither its record nor a finding ("round N didn't finish: run the critic again", reason `round-unfinished`), instead of passing on what a crashed round got to write.
  - Two deployments of one component for the same env on different platforms are two Deployments (`<component>.<env>.<platform>`), and a setting whose `<env>.<key>` meets another's dotted key (or a key that slugs the same) gets a short hash after its id; projection now reports any remaining records of one kind and id that say different things (`duplicate-record`).
  - A call's `authCite` and `tlsCite`, and their `with` lines, are files the call rests on: a change to one reads the call again (incremental scans and estate freshness).
  - A release plan breaks a dependency cycle inside the cycle (its strongly connected units), so a caller of a cycle still deploys after it, and the gap names the cycle's own repos.
  - A dialect that extends two dialects sharing a base applies the base once, so a parent's override of a base rule is kept.
  - A name with no Latin letter or digit (`注文`, `...`) gets `unnamed-<short hash>` instead of a shared `unnamed`, so such components no longer merge.
- Review round 47 (command line):
  - **A Copilot session can't hide a person's decision behind an option's value.** `questions --option 1 answer q1`, `questions --text hi answer q1`, `questions --state open sheet --apply f.csv` and `verify --write audit <repo> --answers a.yaml` reached their commands inside a session: the command line read the questions, decisions and verify commands' own flags as switches, so their values became the command's words, and the session's check stopped at the first flag. Every option is now in one list with its type (`@maycombhq/ai/cli-options`), which the command line, its own session refusal and the session's command check all read, each taking a flag's value as its value; `questions --option 1 correct q1` and `questions --text x owner q1` run instead of "isn't a command".
  - **`rules add … --draft=false` in a Copilot session is refused**, as any rule set active is: the session's check counted any `--draft=…` as a draft, and the command line read `--draft=false` as no draft.
  - **A switch written `--flag=true` or `--flag=false` means what it says.** `--dry-run=true` wrote (`repos discover <folder> --dry-run=true` rewrote the manifest), `--approve=true` dry-ran and `--handoff=true` ran directly, since each switch was read only when bare. Before any command runs, `--<switch>=true` is the switch and `--<switch>=false` none (any case); another value is refused with exit 2, naming the switch.
  - **Editors set only the options a command's usage requires.** `atticus commands --json` read flags from a summary's words too and marked them required, so picking `map <repo>` in VS Code added `--all --force`. Flags are read from the usage, the options listed at a summary's end and the option lines (an approve flag a summary names is still the command's), and of alternatives (`--option <n> | --text "..."`) none is required alone; VS Code also treats a required flag its usage doesn't show as optional, for an older command line. `rules add` and `change new` now show their required flags in the usage; `questions correct`, `stub serve`, `change similar` and `cites` list the options their summaries named.
  - **An option's value is in the catalogue as the help names it** (`--resume run`, `--dir path`, `--commit sha`, `--idle minutes`, `--right ids`, …), checked against the command line's own types, so VS Code asks for it instead of sending the flag bare; `glossary list|scan`, `plugin test|pin`, `secret set|check|forget` and `estate shared import|refresh|list` are a command each (`glossary list`, `glossary scan`, …).
  - **`critic <change> --resume --gate design` keeps the gate**, and `join --write --json` prints JSON: the first reading took `--gate` and `--json` as `--resume`'s and `--write`'s values. `--write` is a switch everywhere but `ci snippet --write <dir>`, critic's `--resume` a switch, and `bench stories --draft <file>` takes its file.
  - **`atticus watch <change> --json` and `watch <change> --off --json` print JSON** (`change`, `watched`, `watching` and, when watching, its line).
  - **`atticus skills write --check` passes right after `atticus init`**: it read `org-pack/agents/README.md` as an agent. A README.md in `agents/` or `instructions/` is skipped.
- Review round 48 (tasks and Git host):
  - **The archive waits for a release PR the change branch moved past** after it merged (code `release-pr-stale`), as go/no-go does.
  - **A task run again after its PR was closed or merged never raises the old commits again.** While `task/<change>/<task>` still has commits that neither the change branch here nor the Git host's has (the closed PR's work, or what a squash merge took in another form), the run (and its dry run) is refused, saying how to start the task afresh: remove its worktree and branch here and delete the branch on the host. Atticus deletes and force-pushes nothing. A task branch with nothing of its own starts again from the change branch as it caught up with the host (fast-forward only).
  - **Raising a task's PR records the PR the host opened when its answer was lost.** On the host's "already exists", the open PR from the task branch into the change branch is recorded and the task is `pr-open`, as release PRs do; one into another base is left alone.
  - **The manual Git host gives each repo its own default branch.** `getRepo` takes the repo's `defaultBranch` from the repos manifest (ADR 0123), with `defaultBranch.<repo>` and `protectedBranches.<repo>` settings to override it and the adapter-wide settings after that, and the default branch is always protected; release PRs no longer go into `main` for a repo whose baseline is `develop`.
  - **Raising a hand-off again is safe.** A hand-off PR the host opened without answering is recorded; a resubmit after Return to PO is refused while the returned PR is still open on the host (close it there first); and a re-run that changed nothing (the publisher says the branch didn't move, or the same files) posts no "updated the hand-off" comment.
  - **A merged release PR stands only while the change branch is where it merged.** Sync records the change branch's commit when it first reads the PR merged (in `release-prs.yaml`, `merged`); once the branch here or on the host has commits beyond it, go/no-go waits (`release-pr-stale`) and Raise the release PRs raises a new one. A local branch that is only behind the host's keeps the merged PR.
  - **Publishing a hand-off never moves your local hand-off branch from under you.** The commit is pushed by itself; the local branch follows only when no worktree has it checked out and it has no commits of its own, by compare-and-swap.
  - **A task id used by more than one repo in tasks.md gets a work item each.** Such a task is keyed and tagged `task:<repo>/<id>` (`atticus:<change>:task:<repo>/<id>`); an id used once keeps `task:<id>`, so items synced before still match, and an item synced under the shared key moves to its repo's key and is updated with the new tag.
  - **What applies to a change is safe in the hand-off PR body.** Each ingested title, reason, path and id is on one line in inline code with its secrets redacted, as the open findings are.
  - **A step that adds itself to an existing question never writes over a person's answer.** The question is read again and written only if unchanged; changed meanwhile, it is left as it is.
  - **copilot-acp runs claim their folder.** `atticus map --all` steps and the copilot-acp runtime take a new run id when the folder is there, so two runs in one second never share one.
- Review round 48 (app server):
  - **The session token no longer approves a write to a shared system** (ADR 0186). `atticus ui --show-token` prints the token for assistants and scripts, and with it alone an assistant could raise a hand-off, the release PRs or a task's PR, merge a ready PR through Sync, Propose for review, run a step with a team's tool or send the digest as you. Each now needs your confirmation, which only the app's page can ask for, on your click: single use, for that write and that change, task, step or scope, for two minutes. With the token alone each is 403 and says to use the app's button or the command with `--approve`; Sync without one only reads. The VS Code panel built from the extension's files gets its page key from the extension; IntelliJ and the framed panel get it from the page.
  - **The AI tab can't send another secret to a model's address.** `keyEnv` must be a variable starting `ATTICUS_`, a model provider's own (`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `OPENAI_API_KEY` and the like) or a name in this machine's credential store, and the providers read nothing else from the environment, so `keyEnv: GITHUB_TOKEN` with an address of a caller's choosing no longer sends your Git token there, from the app or a hand-edited runtime.yaml. Changing the provider, `keyEnv`, an address or `caFile` in the app needs your click, and Test of model settings that differ from the committed runtime.yaml does too. `atticus doctor` says when keyEnv names a variable the providers won't read.
  - **One workspace sync and one reconcile at a time.** `POST /api/workspace/sync` and `/api/reconcile` answer 409 while one of their kind runs on the server, and git work on a base clone (sync, a branch checkout, a change's worktree) holds `<base clone>.lock`, so the app and a terminal never run git in one base clone together.
  - **A change id from the address is checked first.** `GET /api/past-changes?change=../../x` read a `change.yaml` outside `openspec/changes`; it and `GET /api/changes/{changeId}/cockpit` (a 500 before) now answer 400 for an id that isn't one.
  - **`/api/view/toString` and `/api/view/__proto__` are 404**, not the object's prototype run as a view; a mapping field named `toString` or `constructor` is a field too.
- Review round 48 (AI decides):
  - **A person's answer to a targets question stands over an AI pairing, "None of these" too.** The AI no longer pairs a call whose targets question a person answered (its own, its group's or a rule's; checked under the corrections file's lock), a correction's spread skips such a call, and the join ranks the person's answer above an AI pairing.
  - **An AI answer never re-links a call a person declined.** A targets proposal stays a proposal when one of its calls has a person's pairing or "none" on the canvas, or a person's link rule names another box or none; and the join reads a person's "none" (`unlink`), so an AI targets answer no longer joins that call.
  - **A correction's rule no longer reaches calls whose path starts with a placeholder.** The spread, the rule checks of the AI's pairings and answers and the targets rules the AI is checked against read a rule as core's `matchCovers` does: only the rule's placeholders match any segment, so `/charges/**` doesn't cover `/{base}/accounts/{id}` in another folder. The join's reading of a targets answer's rule (ADR 0153) is unchanged.
  - **A superseded hand-off run is cancelled, not left open.** A new propose hand-off or Match brief, or a Copilot session's propose or Match, ends the open hand-off runs as `cancelled`; their Done says so and reads nothing, and Questions and Connect no longer find them as the open run.
  - **Undo of an AI questions batch puts the AI's proposals back** at `proposals/questions/<id>.yaml`, as they were (the batch keeps each proposal's text; an older batch's is rebuilt from the answer's option and lines), never over a newer proposal.
  - **A correction's derived rule no longer widens because of one distant sibling.** It is the longest prefix the item shares with at least one sibling (`/api/v1/limits/**`, not `/api/v1/**`); a shared prefix of two segments or fewer that is wider than the item's own folder gives way to the folder. Host and topic rules are derived the same way.
- Review round 49 (upgrade):
  - **An older Atticus never overwrites a stamp it can't read.** A `.atticus/metarepo.yaml` from a newer Atticus (a field this one doesn't know, or a higher schema version) was taken as no stamp, so `atticus upgrade` ran and wrote its own over it. The Atticus it names is now read on its own: a newer one is refused as before, and a stamp that names none, or an older one, is refused as written by a newer or unknown Atticus, with nothing changed; doctor fails, and the app's upgrade answers 409.
  - **Upgrade finds the catalog where the layout keeps it.** The catalog steps named `catalog/…` whatever `layout.catalog` said (`kb/catalog`, `.atticus/catalog`), so the backup skipped the files, `--dry-run` and `--json` listed the wrong paths, and records were rewritten with no copy. They name the layout's folder; and a file a step would rewrite that the backup can't find stops the upgrade before anything changes (only a step that makes its file may list one that isn't there yet).
  - **A question is written back to the file it was read from.** A stale `q-… copy.yaml` at an older version was written over the live question with no backup, and a renamed file was left at its version beside a new duplicate each run. A file not named `<id>.yaml` is left as it is and said (`atticus upgrade` and `--check` print "Left as it is"), and the rewrite only replaces the text it read, so an answer that lands meanwhile is read again, never lost.
  - **sources.yaml takes the wiki refs beta.18 took.** Round 13 narrowed a git wiki's `ref` to letters, digits and `._/-` without a new schema version, so a beta.18 file with `ref: v2.1+hotfix` (or `@`, `_`) stopped parsing; this loosens it back to the names git takes (refused: empty, a leading `-`, spaces or control characters, `..`, `@{`, `~ ^ : ? * [ \`, and git's other ref-format rules), still never readable as an option. And the layout is read on its own, each folder by itself: a wrong field elsewhere in sources.yaml no longer moves every folder back to its default; `atticus orgpack validate` names the problem.
  - **The app's upgrade records this Atticus when no step waits.** POST `/api/upgrade` answered 409 "Nothing to upgrade" where `atticus upgrade` writes the stamp with the new version; it now runs when the stamp is older than this build or missing, and 409 only when the stamp is this build's.
  - **A question or record from a newer Atticus is said as one.** Reading a question at a version above this one's printed the schema's raw issues, and `atticus questions show` said "No question"; both now say `written by a newer Atticus (question version N); install it`. A catalog record above this one's version says the same (`record version N`).
  - **Migrations keep long lines as they are.** change-types.yaml, gates.yaml, runtime.yaml and roles.yaml rewritten by `atticus upgrade` no longer fold long strings at 80 characters; and `atticus init --by <you>` records that person in the stamp (it recorded the Git user).
- Review round 49 (scan pipeline):
  - **The verifier never follows a symbolic link.** It walked links as folders and files: a loop (`src/loop -> ..`) hung `map`, `verify` and the facts behind a scan; a link to `/etc/passwd` put that file in a map prompt and let a fact cite it; a link to `../.env` under another name got past the secret-path check. It now lists a checkout as the scan and the assistant's code tools do (links and `.git` skipped), and a read refuses a link and any path whose real path leaves the repo.
  - **A scan setting's exclusion changes the stored scan's key.** Results were stored by repo, commit, extractor, version, dialects and the dialect rules only, so after `gen/**` was excluded the next scan reused the result that still had `gen/`'s records. The key now carries how the files were listed: the exclusions, the folders a scan skips and the size limit. **Stored scan results are recomputed once** (the key is new).
  - **One file with injection text no longer leaves a whole map batch out, silently.** A batch's files went to the model as one source, so a test resource holding prompt-injection text on purpose quarantined every file of the batch, and nothing said so without `--max-tokens`. Each file is now a source of its own: only that file is left out, and `map` says `left out <file>#L<a>-<b>: <reason>` (a batch whose every file is left out is skipped). A source a step can't go without that is left out for any reason (its classification, the budget, the defences) now stops the step with a clear error (`RequiredSourceLeftOut`), with or without a model limit; this holds on `map`, `map --all` and a rules top-up.
  - **A long line can't make a map prompt without end.** A line over 1,000 characters (a minified bundle, an inlined blob) went whole into its slice, so one line could exceed any limit. A prompt now shows its first 1,000 characters and `… [line cut: N characters]`; a quote of what is shown still matches the line.
  - **A submodule bump re-reads the facts that cite its files.** The diff named only the submodule's folder, so a fact citing `lib/sm/api.ts` was carried as unchanged. Changes are read with `git diff --raw`, a gitlink (mode 160000) is marked, and every cited file under it is read again (deleted with it, when it goes).
  - **Coverage, the scan and the code tools list the same files.** The verifier's coverage counted what a .gitignore ignores (`.terraform/`, generated output) and the scan read it too, though neither is in the commit. One walker, in @maycombhq/core, honours the repo's .gitignore files and skips links for the verifier, its coverage, the scan's `fsRepo` and the assistant's grep, files and outline.
  - **Files that aren't UTF-8 are read.** A `.sql` saved by SQL Server Management Studio (UTF-16 with its byte-order mark) or a Latin-1 `.java` never verified: every reader decoded UTF-8 only. One decoder now reads UTF-16 LE and BE with their mark, UTF-8 without one, and windows-1252 when the bytes aren't UTF-8, for the verifier, the scan and the code tools. A file with a NUL and no UTF-16 mark is binary, never source.
  - **Tokens outside ASCII are counted.** The estimate was characters / 4, so a Japanese or Cyrillic file was planned at a quarter of its size and its batches overflowed the model. One estimate (`estimateTokens`, @maycombhq/core) counts four ASCII characters a token and each other character one, for every budget; a tool's result cut to its room is cut by the same count. One map test's limit moved from 8800 to 9100 tokens so its flow still shares a batch with its entry.
  - **A file is judged by its name and size before it is read.** The map's planner and coverage read every listed file whole (a 2 GB dump included) only to pass it over, as grep and the name search did. A binary by its extension, a lock file, or a file over 5 MB (the scan's limit) is now passed over unread; the code tools stat a file before reading it.
  - **An un-smudged Git LFS pointer isn't source.** A checkout without `git lfs pull` holds three-line pointers (`version https://git-lfs.github.com/spec/v1`); they counted in coverage and went into prompts. They are left out of both.
- Review round 49 (end to end):
  - **`atticus change archive` rescans after OpenSpec archives the change.** It printed "Archived" then failed "No change …: change.yaml doesn't exist": the rescan read the change after `openspec archive` had moved its folder to `openspec/changes/archive/<date>-<id>/`. The repos to rescan are worked out before archiving.
  - **An Apigee proxy with no conditional flow is recorded, with its hop to the target.** The map prompt asks for one `ANY` entry at `<BasePath>/**` cited on the `<BasePath>` line and a forwarding call, and the verifier refused both, so the gateway's call to the service was never kept. It accepts `<BasePath>/**` on the `<BasePath>` line, and a pass-through `/**` (or `/`, after the target's own path) for a forwarding call cited on the target's `<URL>` or `<Path>` line or the `<RouteRule>` line; the prompt says where to cite the call. Golden: `fixtures/gateway-facts/passthrough/`.
  - **Two settings whose keys end the same are two Settings.** `gateway.url` and `audit.url` of one file, read as `url` each (the prompt said the last part was enough), projected to one Setting and the gateway's URL was lost. The prompt asks for the key's full dotted path; facts of one key and env that say different things each get a short hash of their file and line after the id (stable), and the clash is still reported as `duplicate-record`.
  - **`atticus todo done` of a manual hand-off PR's step syncs the change, as the app does.** After recording the PR's link and `merged=yes` on its reviews step, `next` still said the PR wasn't open yet and only an unnamed `atticus change sync` moved on. The CLI now syncs the change's hand-off when such a step is recorded (one shared path with Home › Manual steps), and when a step was recorded without a sync, `next` names `atticus change sync <id>` (with `--repo` when runtime.yaml doesn't name the metarepo).
  - **A task drafted at the draft level can be raised at the pr level.** Run again at pr, the same answer staged nothing and the run stopped on "nothing to commit"; the task branch's own commit, ahead of the change branch, is used and the run goes on to the build and the PR. With no build command, the run says to set one (`build:` in repos.manifest.yaml) rather than only "No PR".
  - **A topic owner question retires once its topic is no longer a loose end.** Asked when no repo of the estate served the topic's other side, it stayed open after that side was read and joined; it retires as a target question does, saying why, or names the question that asks about the topic now.
  - **`next` checks the gate before offering a hand-off.** With the stage done but a blocking finding or an open gap on the gate, it offered `atticus change handoff <id> --approve`, which then refused. It names the finding (`atticus finding <change> <id> edit-and-apply`, or `ask` for a gap), or the owner question a gap waits on, with the gate's own words.
  - **`next` takes a released change to its archive.** `released` counted as done, so the archive was never offered. With the archive gate's own checks, it names the released repos to read again (`atticus map <repo>`, `atticus verify <repo> --write`) where their facts aren't of the merged commit, what else stops the gate, then `atticus change archive <id>`.
  - **`adapters: { gitHost: manual }` is accepted**, as the admin guide words it: an adapter named by its type alone is short for `{ type: <type> }` (C6 loosened, every file valid before reads the same; no schema version bump). A runtime.yaml whose adapters don't validate is said with the validation error, not as "No Git host is configured".
  - **The critic's closed-gate line names the change**: `atticus finding <change> <id> <action>`, not `atticus finding <id> <action>`, which the command refuses.
- C4: the local API is at 3.0.0 (ADR 0186): `POST /api/confirmations`, and `confirmation` on the hand-off, release PRs, sync (merge), task PR, propose, step run, digest send and AI settings and Test; the session token alone gets 403 on those writes. Also 409 on `/api/workspace/sync` and `/api/reconcile`, and 400 for a bad change id on `/api/past-changes` and the cockpit.

## 2.0.0-beta.21 (2026-11-08)

### AI decides first, people correct, and a correction spreads (ADR 0181)

- ADR 0181 (AI decides first): with `decide: { links: auto, questions: auto }` in runtime.yaml (version 4; `propose`, today's flow, by default; `atticus upgrade` writes the number), Atticus applies what a Copilot session it ran decided, as the AI's and never as yours: a Match with Copilot pairing (a `link` line by `atticus` with the run, joined `joined-by-ai`) and a `questions propose` answer to an option question about a target (`by: { kind: ai, runtime: copilot-acp, run }`), only when the citation check passes and the shapes agree (ADR 0178's confident pair, or their first pick) or a person's rule says the same. Never words, a scope wider than the repo, a hosts or topics answer (they write the manifest or the org pack), an outside system's pairing, a gate or anything person-only. Each application is one batch under `decisions/`. `atticus questions correct <item> --option <n> | --api <entry> --to <repo> | --none` records your correction as yours; Apply to similar (on; `--no-similar` off, `--match` to give the rule, `--preview` first) derives a path, host or topic rule and re-checks every AI decision and open item it covers, applied as your rule or opened again; a shape corrected three times offers a reading rule (ADR 0176). `atticus questions undo <batch>` removes exactly a batch's lines and answers. `atticus questions decisions` lists what stands, counts from code / by a person / by AI, and the batches. The sample audit draws the AI's decisions as their own kind (`atticus verify audit`), and below `decide.minPrecision` (0.95) deciding pauses back to propose and says why. The critic asks the caller's owner to confirm a link the AI decided, a change's impact marks a caller whose links are all the AI's (`decidedByAi`, `byAi`), and the stages read an AI answer as the AI's. Contracts: catalog schema 5 (evidence `source: ai` with `run`), question schema 5 (shared with role-owned questions), corrections lines version 2 (`ai`, `batch`, `rule`, `match`, `unlink`), runtime.yaml 4 (`decide`); upgrade steps `catalog-schema-v5`, `runtime-v4`, `corrections-v2`. Local API 2.80.0: `GET /api/decisions`, `POST /api/decisions/correct`, `POST /api/decisions/batches/{batchId}/undo`, `MatchLinksView.decided`, additive. The screens ("Detected by AI" chips, Correct, Undo, Home's pause) follow.
- ADR 0181 (screens: Detected by AI, Correct, Undo):
  - **"Detected by AI" wherever the AI decided.** A chip in words with a shape (never colour alone), its meaning spoken to a screen reader, and the run linked to Set up › Model runs: on the Connect panel (what the AI already paired between the two boxes), Match with Copilot (what Atticus applied as the AI's, with the bar it met, and what it kept as proposals and why), Why is this linked? (the call's `ai` evidence, in place of "unknown"), a box's Corrections and declarations, Questions (the answer says "Answered by AI", in the list and on the question, never as a person), Contracts (the list and a record's evidence), a change's impact (the breaks linked only by AI, counted, and each such caller marked) and a change's knowledge. Questions and Contracts show how the questions and links were settled: from code, by a person, by AI.
  - **Correct, and Apply to similar.** Any standing AI decision has **Correct**: the right option, the right API with its repo (from that repo's APIs), or none of these. Apply to similar is on; **Preview** shows your answer, the rule Atticus derived (editable) and every item it re-checks (applied, confirmed, replaced, reverted to open) before anything is saved, and a change after the preview asks for a fresh one. **Save my correction** is your approval and writes your word; the result takes the focus, says what spread with the commands to run, and offers **Make this a reading rule** when the shape has been corrected three times (`POST /api/reading-rules/from-pairing`, or the `rules draft` command for a question).
  - **Map › Decided by AI** (a new Map tab, beside Trust; in Ctrl+K as Detected by AI and Batches): what the AI decided that still stands, filtered by repo and kind, each with its bar, lines and Correct; the counts split three ways; and every batch, newest first, with **Undo**, which asks first and then says what it removed, put back and left because it changed since. Empty, loading and error states throughout.
  - **The pause.** When the sample audit pauses AI deciding, Home says why with a link to Map › Trust; Map › Trust shows the AI decisions' precision across the estate against `decide.minPrecision`, and each repo's AI audit beside its facts' and links'.
- ADR 0181 (canvas: Detected by AI layer):
  - **The AI's links on the canvas.** A link the AI paired is drawn dotted, apart from a person's dashes, with "◆ Detected by AI" in its label (or how many of its calls, when only some are the AI's): a shape and words, never colour alone, in a violet that keeps 3:1 against the canvas in both themes. It sits on its own **Detected by AI** layer in Layers, filters and tools, on by default, with a legend entry, **Only links detected by AI** (pressed again, every link layer is back; a kept view keeps it as its layers) and each AI link listed as a button, so a keyboard reaches them all. Above the picture, its links are counted from code, by a person and by AI, with how each kind is drawn.
  - **Pick one, correct it.** Click an AI link on the picture, or pick it from the list, and the inspector opens on it (its heading takes the focus from the keyboard): each call with Why is this linked?, the "Detected by AI" chip with its run, and **Correct** with Apply to similar, as on Map › Decided by AI. Once a correction is saved the decisions and the picture are read again and a screen reader hears so; the link moves once `atticus verify <repo> --write` has joined the caller's repo again. Loading and error states for the decisions, and a word when the link has left the picture.
  - **Exports.** Mermaid, draw.io and SVG draw an AI link dotted (draw.io `dashPattern=1 4`, SVG `stroke-dasharray="1 4"`) with its words in the label, the same output every time.
  - Local API 2.82.0, additive: a graph join's `how` may be `ai` (its Interface flagged `joined-by-ai`), with the deciding `run`; it was `unknown` before.

### Component types your organisation owns (ADR 0180)

- `org-pack/component-types/<id>/type.yaml` (with `notes.md` beside it): what a kind of component is, how a read recognises one (signals), where it runs, how the canvas draws it (shape, colour, badge), and what it must and must not do, in your words. Atticus's thirteen types are the default set; a file under a built-in's id rewords it, `off: true` takes it out. `atticus types list | add | notes`. Reads write one `component` fact per component (the type, cited on the line that shows a signal; facts file version 4, additive), the verifier checks it and flags a type the set doesn't have, the Component record takes it; the canvas draws each component's badge and the tools panel lists the types as filters; a new box drawn for a change can be of a type; the architecture stage is handed what each type must and must not do. Local API 2.60.0 (`GET /api/component-types`), additive. Guide: docs/guide/component-types.md.

### Draw a change on the canvas (ADR 0179)

- Under a change's diagram, **Draw the change**: say a stroke (a call one component makes to another's API, a topic it publishes and who listens, a table it reads or writes, a step of today's flow taken out or sent elsewhere, or a new flow of a new component with where it runs), Preview the lines it adds to the change's target flows, and Draw it on your yes; the picture redraws with the stroke marked + new or ✕ retired, and Undo takes any stroke back. The strokes are ordinary target-flow files, reviewed in the pull request; `atticus change architecture <change>` then fills in the rest around them and never removes or moves a drawn step. A stroke is a plan, never a fact. `atticus change sketch <change> --from <file.drawio|.mmd> [--save]` reads a drawing made elsewhere as the same strokes. VS Code's **Open the Change diagram** and IntelliJ's Change diagram action open the page the panel lives on. Local API 2.58.0 (`GET`/`POST /api/changes/{changeId}/sketch`), additive.

### Connect: matching calls to APIs at scale (ADR 0178)

- Connect to… finds more: a call whose path is a setting (`${refunds.base}/request`) is matched with the setting filled in from the repo's config facts; `/v1`, `/v2` prefixes don't count against a match; a spec operation's `operationId` and a call site's constant name are compared as words; a gateway's route is matched by the path it forwards to. Each call gets its three likeliest APIs (the select lists them first), and a **confident** mark when the best is a sure match well ahead of the rest. The panel counts confident, suggested and unsuggested calls, and **Tick the N confident** / **Tick all suggested** pair many at once (one Preview, one write); only confident ones start ticked. With many APIs, a search box narrows the list. Local API 2.57.0 (`suggestions`, `confident`, `words`, `forwards`), additive.
- **Match with Copilot** in the same panel: when the paths aren't enough, one Copilot session reads the repo's call sites (the constants and settings the paths are built from) against the other box's APIs and proposes which API each call reaches, naming the lines; the answer is checked (every call one of the repo's, every API one of the box's) and shown as proposals you tick, preview and confirm like any pairing. The session writes only its answer, under `proposals/matching/`; where copilot-acp isn't allowed the brief is written for your assistant and **Read the answer** reads it back. `atticus connect match <repo> --to <repo> | --system <id> [--mode copilot-acp|hand-off]` does the same from a terminal. Local API 2.59.0 (`GET`/`POST /api/links/match`), additive.

### Skills and agents from other teams, for Claude and Copilot (G5)

- `atticus skills import <their repo> --team <team> --at <tag>` takes another team's skills, agents and instructions as a pinned copy into `org-pack/{skills,agents,instructions}/imported/<team>/`, with where they came from, and, after review, `atticus skills write` writes them for both assistants: `.claude/skills/` and `.github/prompts/`, `.claude/agents/` and `.github/agents/`, `.github/instructions/` and an `@` import in CLAUDE.md. Your own go in the same folders. A file edited here is kept; a skill with scripts, or one that states estate facts with no atticus command to check them, is flagged for review; imported text never reaches Atticus's own prompts. `atticus skills list | write [--check]`. ADR 0177; guide: docs/guide/skills-and-agents.md.

### Reading rules your organisation owns (G4)

- `org-pack/reading-rules/`: rules for how your repos are read, in layers (another team's imported and pinned, the organisation's, a repo type's, one repo's), each with its key, words, files, an example line and who wrote it; a later layer can switch an earlier rule (or one of Atticus's sets, `builtin:<set>`) off, saying why. Reads are given them after Atticus's own; a read records each rule's id, so `atticus map --all --stale` tops up only the files a new or changed rule names. `atticus rules list | add | activate | off | draft | try | import | notes`: Copilot drafts rules for a type from what the reads keep missing, `try` shows what a rule would change and writes nothing, `import` takes another team's rules pinned at a tag, each a draft until reviewed. On the canvas, a pairing can become a draft rule for every repo of its type. ADR 0176; guide: docs/guide/reading-rules.md.

### A canvas you work in (G6)

- Above the canvas, **Layers, filters and tools**: layers (read and cited, guesses and gaps, declared, paired, outside systems, topics, only mismatches); a filter (`team:`, `system:`, `type:`, `path:/accounts/**`, words); colour boxes by gaps or open questions; the blast radius of the selected box (upstream, downstream or both, depth 1–5); a trace hop by hop; the picture's open problems; compare with a snapshot (added links marked new, those gone listed); saved views kept as `diagrams/views/<name>.yaml`; export as Mermaid, draw.io, SVG, PNG or PDF. A citation opens its file at the line in VS Code or IntelliJ. ADR 0175.

### Add a box, declare a link, and Undo (G3)

- **Add to the picture…** lists the manifest's repos not read yet (each with Read with Copilot and its command) and adds a new vendor or another team's system to the profile, for review. On a system's box, **Declare a link…** writes a topic it publishes to or listens on, or a gateway route it calls, under the system in the profile with who and when as the line's comment; declared and person-paired links are drawn dashed. Every box lists its corrections or declarations with **Undo**, which removes exactly that line. The guide: docs/guide/correcting-the-map.md.

### A repo's owner and system from its box (G2)

- A repo's box on the canvas has **Owner and system…**: pick a team from roles.yaml (or **Add a team…**, written to roles.yaml for review) and a system of the profiles; Preview shows the lines that change in each file, and Write it edits the manifest in place, its comments kept. The map shows it after `atticus verify --all --write`. Local API 2.53.0 (`GET`/`PUT /api/repos/{repo}/owner`).

### Connect two boxes on the canvas (G1)

- On the canvas, a repo's box has **Connect to…** (or draw a line from one box to another): a panel lists this repo's calls that join nothing, the other box's APIs (a service's entries, a gateway's routes, an outside system's operations from its spec), and the API each call most likely is, picked already (the same path, the same but for a prefix, a parameter or a setting, or names alike: `captureChargePath` and `/partners/northwind/charges/{chargeId}/capture`). Preview says what the next `atticus verify <repo> --write` changes; each pair you confirm is one `link` line in the repo's corrections file, the call's fact left as read; the link is drawn flagged `joined-by-person`, found again by method and path when a read renames the entry, and Undo removes exactly the line. "All" answers the repo's open target question with `/**`. When a side has nothing on record, the panel gives the command that reads it or adds the spec. ADR 0174; local API 2.53.0.

### Copilot runs from the app

- Map › Trust can start an unattended Copilot run (copilot-acp) after your yes: read the stale repos, fill the gaps, read the repos not read yet, or read one repo again. A dialog names what it reads, the model and what a session may do; a strip under the top bar shows the repo of how many, the session, facts kept and rejected and tokens, with Stop; and a collapsible Console at the foot of the app shows the lines, one tab per session (follow the end, search, copy). A reload, or the app in VS Code or IntelliJ, picks the run up. ADR 0173; local API 2.52.0 (`/api/copilot-runs`).

### Faster pages, and a bar for anything loading

- A thin loading bar runs across the top of the app whenever any request is in flight, however short, and every panel still waiting shows its own; both are said to screen readers and move only when motion is allowed.
- The Questions page is one request: the list carries the counts by state (and per repo) for the top of the page (`counts`, local API 2.51.0), every question is read once, and the repos' facts the rule offers need are parsed once per version of each file, not on every load.
- A repo's facts view (the evidence on the canvas and Know) is worked out once per version of the files it reads (its facts, corrections, audit and the questions) and kept, so 40 repos' evidence comes back at once on a second look and after unrelated changes.
- The canvas builds the estate once per state of the catalog, not on every box opened: the app starts building it as it opens, boxes opened together share one check of the catalog files, and a catalog file the app sees change is read on the next open. At 40 systems and about 4,900 records, opening a box after the first went from seconds to well under a second (a test at that size keeps it so).

### Fixed before release (review)

- Final review (safety):
