  - **A Copilot session can't write a person's facts record under a name Windows opens as it** (a trailing dot or space, or a `:stream`); such names are refused for any file.
  - **Undoing both of two batches, in either order, never leaves the AI's answers standing**, and Undo never puts back an answer an undone batch wrote; when nothing of a batch stands, Undo says so.
  - **Done on a decide hand-off is a person's**: inside a Copilot session it is refused and the run stays open for the person's Done.
- Final review (git and tasks):
  - Hand-down commits only the files it wrote, with a pathspec commit: a person's uncommitted or staged edits elsewhere in the change worktree stay as they left them, and no longer go into the "Hand down task" commit the task branch is cut from and pushed with. A file Atticus merges into (a constitution section, the OpenSpec reference) that already held the person's uncommitted edits is left uncommitted, and the hand-down says so.
  - `workspace prune` gives the task worktrees under a change's `.tasks/` folder the same care as its change worktrees: one with uncommitted work is kept unless `--force`, the rest go by `git worktree remove`, and the change's folder is removed only once none is kept. Before, removing the folder deleted them, uncommitted work included.
  - Bring in keeps a reviewer's edit to a new change's `change.yaml` made on the hand-off branch. The merge base is the hand-off ref this copy published; with only the remote's branch, it is that branch's first commit after it left this copy (Atticus's published commit), never the remote's tip, which carries the reviewer's commits.
  - Catching the change branch up with the Git host's moves `change/<id>` only. When the person switched the change worktree to a branch of their own, that branch is left as it is and `change/<id>` moves by compare-and-swap; when `change/<id>` is checked out in another worktree, the run refuses, saying to switch back.
  - A "nothing to release" entry in `release-prs.yaml` holds only while the change branch, here and on the Git host, is at the commit the raise recorded. Once work lands on it, go/no-go and the archive wait for that repo's release PR again.
- Final review (API, scan and intake):
  - **A path glob can't stall a scan or a code search.** The globs of grep's paths, files and every .gitignore line of a scanned repo were run as backtracking regular expressions on the main thread with no time limit, so a glob such as `*a*a*a…!` took seconds and grew five-fold with each `*a`. They are now matched in linear time, with the same results.
  - **A gateway hop that guessed makes the walked link a guess.** A forwarding call joined by path, by a host's first label or by a person's pairing was dropped from the caller's link through the gateway, so that link came out verified and flagged joined-by-host. It is now inferred with the hop's flag (joined-by-path, joined-by-label), or that person's pairing (joined-by-person) citing the hop's forwarding call, as a hop the AI joined already made it the AI's.
  - **Apply refuses a repo that isn't a repo name.** `POST /api/facts/{repo}/apply` passed the repo straight to verify, and the router decodes %2F and %5C, so an encoded `../` could read a facts file outside the metarepo. It is checked as the other /api/facts/{repo} routes check it (400), and must be a repo the workspace lists (404), before a job starts.
  - **Attached and inbox files never differ from another only in case.** Attaching (the upload and `atticus change attach`) and adding to the inbox (promote, `atticus intake add`) compared names case-sensitively, so on Windows SPEC.pdf overwrote spec.pdf, and INDEX.yaml a change's inputs index. Names are now compared without case: SPEC.pdf is kept as SPEC-2.pdf, INDEX.yaml as INDEX-2.yaml.
- Ninth UX walk (no terminal in the daily path, wording):
  - **Questions opened for a change says that change has no question** when its list is empty, not that a scan adds one.
  - **A saved stage correction says where the file goes**: stage/corrections.yaml goes with the stage's hand-off; outside one, Propose for review; the git command is under "Or in a terminal".
  - **A knowledge gap or a blocking finding on a stage's gate has an in-app step** on Run next: answer it on the change's page (Apply fix, Ask, Not relevant), not "In a terminal… No slash command does this step yet".
  - **The app's lines say the app's actions**: a failed answer says "fix the answer, then press Done again", and a merge not pulled yet says "Bring in what merged on Home" (the command line keeps its commands).
  - **A failed hand-off's page says one state and each error once**; a task's run goes on to the Task board after Done, and the keyboard comes to that link; the AI task runs count a task with the assistant.
  - **At a glance**: "done 2 drafts" no longer runs together, a manual Git host's PR is said once (not once per repo), and the workspace command is only under "Or in a terminal".
  - **Words and layout**: no "2 edges ()" in the Impact explorer; Feature design chips feature.md as drafted by AI once; paths and cites keep their case; the build hand-off is "Build ready" (not 'raise "Raise PR"'); a manual host's open-PR step names its repo; the Tasks page follows the change's step once the stories are done; the Release plan's rollback label is apart from its words.
  - **Release PRs on a manual Git host** say they wait on you to open them (Home › Manual steps), never "#103 DRAFT".
  - **Set up › Repos**: Say what it is, Confirm it, Drop it and Edit branches name their repo; the Ask Atticus field draws its own focus ring.
- Ninth UX walk (Bring in and what reaches main):
  - **Bring in what merged no longer refuses after a hand-off merges.** Sync here and the hand-off branch's copy both edit a change's `change.yaml`, so the text merge clashed ("What merged also changes … change.yaml where your copy changed it") after the Define, Design, Plan and Build hand-offs. The record is now merged by its fields: history and hand-offs are a union (a hand-off both sides have keeps its further state), the state is where the merged history ends, who wrote each artifact is a union, and a field a person edits (title, owners role by role, systems, touches) takes the side that changed it. Bring in refuses, naming the field and changing nothing, only when both sides changed the same field differently. A file that isn't a change record Atticus can read is still merged as text. The result is canonical YAML.
  - **Everything a change writes reaches main.** A hand-off now also carries the files of earlier phases' stages (the feature stage often runs after the Define hand-off merged, so `feature.md`, `feature.yaml` and `stage/feature.yaml` now go with the Design hand-off), `stage/corrections.yaml` and `stage/drafted.yaml` with every hand-off, and each task's `tasks/<task>/report.md` with the Build hand-off (not its run records). Decision batches (`decisions/`, which Undo reads) are proposed with Home's Propose, the answers and the corrections, so another clone can undo a batch; Home's Propose also lists each change's `stage/corrections.yaml` and `stage/drafted.yaml`, so a correction saved in the app can be proposed before the next hand-off.
  - **A merged PR's manual steps close.** On a manual Git host, when Bring in finds that a hand-off's or a proposal's branch into the metarepo has been merged in, it closes that PR's open steps (open, reviews, merge) with the note "merged, seen by Bring in", so Sync reads the PR as merged. A PR into another repo, or a branch with no commits of its own, keeps its steps.
- Ninth UX walk (gates, owner questions, roles, tasks):
  - **A pattern rule with nothing to check against doesn't hold the gate.** While the org pack's pattern registry is empty, `constitution.interface-approved-pattern` (and the pattern-code checks) find nothing, instead of "Use a code from the registry: ." findings nobody could fix or waive. Home's setup checklist has a new step, "The integration pattern registry has patterns", saying how to add them under `org-pack/patterns/`. Once the registry has codes the rule stays unwaivable.
  - **A stage's "Three drafts in a row" question closes with its finding.** When a person dismisses, waives or fixes the critic finding a stage's question was about, or the gate's newest round no longer raises it, the question is retired, saying why, at once (`atticus finding`, the app's Not relevant) and when the stage runs again, and the stage carries on.
  - **Ask on a knowledge gap raises an owner question for that team.** It is listed in Questions, Owner questions, and counted on Home; the gate waits for its answer, and a person's answer there (or `atticus questions owner <id> --text`) closes the gap at the gate. Before, Ask unblocked the gate with nothing anybody could answer.
  - **Role-owned questions.** Home shows "Your role" only for the changes that don't name you among their owners (where one does, its owners decide), a question says its role on the Questions list and the question, the change page names the change's owners per role (product owner, architect, business analyst, tech lead) in `change.yaml`, and the feature stage's questions are the architect's, since it places the change on the estate (ADR 0181).
  - **Tasks in one repo run in order.** A task waits for the earlier not-done tasks of its own repo in `tasks.md` (unless both are marked `[P]`), so Run all ready tasks drafts each from a base with the code before it, and the Task board says "Waits for 1.1 (same repo)". Local API 2.83.0.
- Ninth UX walk (AI decides first):
  - **The AI decides in hand-off mode too.** Where copilot-acp isn't allowed, `atticus questions propose` (and Ask AI to propose on Questions, Hand it to my assistant) and Match with Copilot hand their brief to your assistant as an Atticus hand-off run (`.runs/questions-propose/…`, `.runs/match-<repo>/…`, listed on Model runs). Its Done (Done on Questions, Read the answer on Connect, Done on Model runs, `atticus handoff done <run>`, `atticus connect match … --read`) reads the answer back, and Atticus applies what passes the same citation check and bar as the AI's, recorded with runtime `hand-off` and the run, never as you. A proposal on file before the hand-off isn't the run's.
  - **Where deciding happens is said honestly.** Decided by AI says which runs decide here, as runtime.yaml's modes allow them (none, a Copilot session Atticus runs, a hand-off marked done), with a link to Set up › AI. Ask AI to propose says what happens: with decide auto, a proposal that meets the bar is applied as the AI's (Detected by AI); paused, why; otherwise nothing is answered until you accept it. The chip says "through an Atticus run", not "a run Atticus started".
  - **Set up › AI sets AI decides first**: pairings and option answers (propose or decide) and the audited precision that pauses it, written to runtime.yaml on Save (a version 3 file is lifted to 4; CRLF and comments kept; only onto the file as read), then Propose for review.
  - **Home offers the upgrade the metarepo waits on** (runtime.yaml at an older schema, say): each step, and Run the upgrade, which runs `atticus upgrade` as a local job on your click.
  - **A link through a gateway the AI joined is the AI's.** The walk through a gateway route kept neither the caller's own AI answer or pairing nor a hop's: the Interface read `joined-by-answer` (a person's) with no chip. It is now `joined-by-ai`, inferred, with evidence of source `ai` and the run (the hop's forwarding line when the hop was the AI's).
  - **The counts are honest.** Links say they are the map as last applied, and, when the corrections differ, how many AI and people's pairings stand there now (Apply brings the map up to them). Retired questions are counted as retired, not "from code".
  - **An AI answer reads as the AI's on its question**: Atticus applied the proposal from the run because it met the bar, and no person has confirmed it. Correct is the one way to change it (it spreads); the answer form isn't offered beside it.
  - **Correct on Decided by AI**: the rule's field shows whenever Apply to similar is on, so "give a rule with match" can be answered; a question has one "None of these" (its own option); the repo is picked from the estate's repos; the form takes the focus when it opens and Undo's result takes it after; a lone correction's batch is "A person's correction"; the batch rows' chip is "By AI" with the run, not the whole sentence. After Correct or Undo, Apply and Propose for review are buttons in place, the commands under Or in a terminal; a question's reading rule stays a command (there is no route for it yet).
  - **Map › Impact explorer marks an Interface the AI joined** with Detected by AI and its run, as a change's Impact page does (`/api/impact/{id}`'s affected items carry `byAi` and `run`).
  - C4 local API 2.83.0, additive: `POST /api/links/match/done`, `GET`/`POST /api/propose-answers`, `GET`/`POST /api/upgrade`, `MatchLinksView.run`, `DecideStateView.decideIn`, `DecisionCounts.retired`, `DecisionsView.links.lines`, Impact's `byAi` and `run`, and `decide` and `schemaVersion` on the AI settings. An AI decision's runtime may be `hand-off` (question file version 5 and catalog schema 5, unreleased, extended in place).
- Round 45 (review):
  - **The AI never overrides a person's word on a pairing.** Atticus adds an AI pairing only under the corrections file's lock, and never for a call a person has paired or said reaches none, whenever they wrote it (a person's "none" before the run, or a pairing made while the Copilot session ran).
  - **One Run all ready tasks at a time**: the server checks and starts the job in one tick, and the button is held from the click, so a double click starts one job. The panel says when it is reading the ready list and when it couldn't.
  - **A batched hand-off's section starts only at the start of a line**, so a `=== <lens>` quoted inside an answer stays that answer's text.
- ADR 0181 (stage drafts: AI-drafted, corrections carried):
  - **Every stage draft says who wrote it last.** On Feature design (feature.md, proposal.md), Architecture (the target flows, design.md, data.md), Stories (the stories, the specs) and Tasks (tasks.md), each draft shows "Drafted by AI" with the run that drafted it, linked, until a person saves it in the app ("Edited by <who> on <date>"); a draft changed outside the app says "Edited by a person". The stage loop records what each draft wrote, with its stage, run and hash, in one per-change file, `stage/drafted.yaml`; a save through the artifact or placement routes records who and when. A change drafted before falls back to the loop files' hashes.
  - **Correct any AI-drafted item, not only when the critic found something.** Each draft and each of its items (a target flow's step, a section of the proposal, design, a story or a spec, a task) has **Correct**: say what it should say or do instead. Corrections are kept per change in `stage/corrections.yaml` (the item, the person's words, who, when) and listed as waiting for the stage's next run, with **Run the stage again to apply them** (Prepare first). A waiting correction can be taken back.
  - **The next run follows the correction as the person's word.** The stage's brief lists every correction of its drafts in the hand-backs block, as the answered questions are, under an Atticus instruction that says to follow each one, that a later correction wins, and that a correction never overrides a fact verified from code (keep the code's fact and ask). A correction waiting makes a done stage draft again, starting from the current drafts. After the draft, a correction whose item the draft changed is marked applied (one whose item didn't change is marked read and says so); both stay on file.
  - **Apply to similar (on by default) corrects the rest like it.** The correction names what the similar items share (prefilled with the step's component, entry or table) and the run applies the same correction to every other step, story, section or task naming it; after the run the correction lists the other items the draft changed for it.
  - A stage with no corrections sends exactly the prompt it sent before, so recorded replays still match (tested by the prompt's hash). Local API 2.79.0: `GET /api/changes/{changeId}/drafts/{page}`, `GET` and `POST /api/changes/{changeId}/corrections`, `POST .../corrections/{correctionId}/remove`; additive. No contract file changes: both files are new, per change, at version 1.
- Eighth UX walk (next steps):
  - **Home's Run next follows the change, not the map's questions.** Onboarding is done once every repo is read and its facts verified; open map questions no longer hold Run next on "4 questions are waiting" for a whole change. Home shows the first change in flight's own step, with "N map questions open" and a link to Questions beneath it. The changes in flight are counted once (the second "Changes in flight" tile is gone), and each change's Run cell is its in-app action (Release, the Task board, Raise hand-off), or its At a glance, with the command under "Or in a terminal".
  - **Blocking findings come before Raise.** With every task ticked and blocking findings open, `atticus next` and At a glance say "resolve them" (on the change's page: Apply fix, Not relevant or Waive), and raising the hand-off comes after. Raise hand-off waits while what blocks the gate is read, rather than turning on for a moment, and when held it lists the blockers and says why beside the button, not only in a tooltip.
  - **A finished run goes on to its change.** The last run of a chain links to the run that waits next, else to the change's At a glance (the Task board for a task's run), beside "Back to every run". A step's rounds are numbered in order, so two runs that both recorded attempt 1 no longer both read "Round 1".
  - **Questions opened for a change shows that change's questions.** `#/questions?change=<id>` lists the questions its steps asked (the step that asked it, or the one its answer reruns, names the change: the hand-off's rule), says so in a change's words with a link to every question, drops the repo filter and the map's "Apply puts it in the map" opening.
  - Words: "1 question waits for a person"; "Or in a terminal" wherever a command is the other way; At a glance makes the change's branches with a button when no repo has one (the command under "Or in a terminal"); "start one with New change" instead of `atticus change new`; the breadcrumb reads "Change › <id> › …", not "Change › change <id>"; a change's run list says "PR merged" (or closed) from the PR's state on the Git host.
  - After Accept the catalog on Home, the count of files waiting to be proposed is read again at once.
- Eighth UX walk (code against design):
  - **A call the code makes whose target isn't settled yet is "couldn't confirm", not missing**: the check names the call and points to its map question.
  - **The code-against-design check rests on facts; a reader alone never says missing** (rule 4, ADR 0074, 0124). A task branch changes the files the repo's facts cite, so its scan carried none of them (or only an unrelated config fact) and nobody had read the new code; the check then called the gateway proxy's new Apigee `LockCard` flow and the teller app's `config.getUri("core-gateway") + path` call missing. Now the scan says what each repo's records rest on (`basis`: facts, with the changed source files no fact covers yet, or the readers), and only facts that cover the commit can make something missing. Elsewhere the step is under "Not checked by a scan" as "couldn't confirm ... from the code; check it by hand", naming the files to read again (`/atticus-map <repo>`, then `atticus verify <repo> --write`). A migration file is still checked from git. Fixture `fixtures/conform-gateway`.
  - **Check the code again is a button** where the check is shown, beside the command: `POST /api/changes/{changeId}/conformance` runs the check as a local job on the person's click (API 2.76.0); findings are the check's answer, not a failed job.
  - **The task prompt says the build command the manifest has now.** A story's "repos.manifest.yaml names no build command" line, written before a person set the command, is said again from the current manifest when a task runs.
- Eighth UX walk (stage pages and access):
  - **Feature design shows where the feature lands**: feature.md (the feature in a few sentences, the flows it touches, the gaps) with a "Drafted by AI" chip until a person saves it, and Edit opens feature.yaml, the source the stage's next run reads; Save checks it still reads as a placement and draws feature.md again (API 2.76.0, `GET`/`PUT /api/changes/{changeId}/placement`). The change's files list feature.md and feature.yaml.
  - **The in-app Prepare comes first** in a stage's critic panel (before it ran, and under "Then run the stage again"), the breaks panel and the target flows' empty state, with the command folded under "Or in a terminal"; the target flows' empty state has a button now, not only words for a terminal.
  - **One path to write stories**: the stories stage's Prepare. Breakdown links to the stage instead of its own "Write stories with AI", and a stage that is done offers "Run the stage again" only under a disclosure.
  - **The keyboard stays with the result** after Apply, Push and open the PR, saving a file, Mark done on a manual step, Not relevant or another action on a finding, Raise hand-off and Mark done or not done on a task: the result's words take focus when the button went or was disabled, a step marked done stays shown with what was recorded, and a finding that leaves the open list says what was recorded in the list's own status line.
  - **Each repo's build button names its repo** on Set up › Repos.
  - **The brief editor** shows the template's guidance beside the text, offers Cancel while there is unsaved text, and the gate reads again after a save (no more "still holds only the template" until a reload).
  - **A task's PR and tick, as they are**: "PR #n OPEN" once a PR is recorded, "PR TO OPEN" (waiting on you) only while a manual Git host's PR isn't opened; a task ticked or unticked by hand says who did (API 2.76.0, `taskRun.marked`), not that Atticus ticked it at the merge; Design vs plans vs code says "No code yet" before any task has run.
  - **A manual Git host's approval says who recorded it**, on the reviews step once done and on the merge step's "Approved by".
- ADR 0181 (roles and Run all ready tasks):
  - **Each role answers its own questions.** Every question a change's phase raises carries the role that owns it, from the stage that raised it unless the question says otherwise: the brief's (feature) the product owner's, the design's the architect's, the stories' the business analyst's, the tasks' and the build's the tech lead's. Questions are at schema version 5, with an optional `role` (`atticus upgrade` rewrites older files at version 5; an older file reads unchanged and its role is derived from the step that asked). The Questions page has a **Role** filter, and Home shows **Your role's questions** for each change in flight: your role comes from the change's owners (change.yaml, with roles.yaml's custom roles by the role each is based on) for the signed-in Git user, else from the role you pick, remembered in this browser. Local API 2.76.0: `GET /api/questions?role=&change=`, `about.role`, `counts.byRole`, `yourRoles`, and the cockpit's `openQuestionsByRole` and `yourRoles`. Additive.
  - **Run all ready tasks.** On a change's Task board, beside each task's Run with AI: the tech lead or architect runs every task whose dependencies are done (the tasks of the stories its story depends on), in cross-repo order (the deployment plan's order of the repos), one task run per task with its lock, as one server job that stops on the first task that needs a person. The click approves the drafts; each PR is raised on its own Raise click. `atticus task run-ready <change> [--level draft|pr] [--approve] [--dry-run]` does the same from a terminal; at the pr level it raises each PR from `task/<change>/<task>` into the change branch only with the person's `--approve` (a Copilot session is refused it), and without it is a dry run. Nothing is merged or pushed to a protected branch. Local API 2.76.0: `GET /api/changes/{changeId}/tasks/ready`, `POST /api/changes/{changeId}/tasks/run-ready`.
- Round 44 (review):
  - **Sync reads only a change's own release PR**, by its head branch, never another PR that shares its number on a manual Git host, and corrects a recorded number that named the wrong PR.
  - **A change's hand-off carries the questions its own steps asked**, matched by the step (`atticus change <step> <id>`, `atticus plan <step> <id>`, `atticus critic <id>`), never by the repo field: a stage's question about a repo rides with the change, and a repo's own map question never does, even when a change shares the repo's name.
  - **Setting the org pack's owner keeps pack.yaml's line endings** (Windows CRLF stays CRLF), in the app and on the command line.
  - **A regex grep whose call has used its whole time stops as cut** ("the search stopped at its cap of files, bytes or time"), not as a pattern that is too slow.
- Round 44 (review, manual host and task branches):
  - **A recorded PR number could pass another change's gate**, on a manual Git host: when the person recorded a PR's real number and it equalled the local number Atticus had given another PR in the same repo, a lookup by that number returned the recorded PR, so `change sync` for the other change rewrote its link to the wrong PR and could advance its gate on that PR's merge; a task's PR state (the Task board, and the run's "PR is open" check) read the same way. The manual Git host now goes by the number it gave a PR whenever the recorded one is another PR's own number (or was recorded for two PRs, which is then refused by that number), so no number names two PRs; and `change sync`, a hand-off update, the Task board's PR state and Run with AI check that the PR read is from the hand-off's or the task's branch, else look it up by that branch, and refuse without it. Tests in `packages/adapters/test/manual-pr-recorded.test.ts`, `packages/changes/test/pr-refresh.test.ts` and `packages/taskrun/test/task-pr-head.test.ts`.
  - **The third task in a repo was always refused with squash or rebase merges**: a task's hand-down is committed on the local change branch and reaches the Git host only inside the task's PR, so once squash- or rebase-merged the local commit never became part of the host's branch, and every later catch-up refused ("has commits the Git host's hasn't"). When the commits only the local change branch has are all this change's hand-downs and the host's branch has their files as they are, the local branch now moves to the host's (local bookkeeping; nothing is pushed or force-pushed); any other local commit is still never rewritten. Tests with real Git and a bare remote, squash-merging by hand, in `packages/taskrun/test/task-merged.test.ts`.
- Round 43 (review, release plan):
  - **The deployment plan orders a retired interface**, in release: a `planned-retire` hop in interfaces.md was ignored, so the plan could deploy the callee (removing the operation) before its caller stopped calling it, with no gap. The caller's step now says it stops calling the operation and the callee's that it retires it, and the caller deploys first (the reverse of a new or changed operation). When that order fights another dependency (a new operation the other way, or a story dependency), the plan says so with the "depend on each other … agree one and write it in design.md" gap. Golden `packages/release/test/fixtures/expected/plan-retire.yaml`, tests in `packages/release/test/release.test.ts`.
- Round 43 (review, grep and paths):
  - **A grep pattern that runs away no longer hangs the MCP server or a scan.** A regular expression the assistant or the model chose (`(a+)+$` on a run of a's) could backtrack for minutes, and the MCP server answers one call at a time, so every later call waited. grep now runs a regular expression in a `vm` context with a time limit (1 second a file, 5 a call) and answers "the pattern took too long; make it simpler"; the next call works as before. No new dependency.
  - **A manifest url written the Windows way (`.\forge-ledger`) is in place**, like `./forge-ledger`: sync no longer runs `git clone .\forge-ledger` into workspace/, and scans read the repo where it is, not a stale copy. One rule in @maycombhq/workspace (`isInPlace`, `inPlacePath`, `inPlaceDir`) now serves sync, scans, adopt, doctor and the CLI's map and repo-id checks.
- Hand-off mode: one paste for a stage's critic lenses:
  - **A stage round is two pastes: the draft, then all its lenses.** When a stage's critic round hands two or more AI lenses to your assistant, they are also asked in one prompt, and the draft's Done offers it as the next prompt. The answer has one `=== <lens>` section per lens; Done checks each section on its own with that lens's own check and reads in each lens that passes. A missing or bad section fails only its lens: fix it and press Done again (only the lenses still out are checked), or answer that lens alone from its own run. Each lens keeps its own run, prompt, pack and record, so Runs, evals and audits still see one run per lens. Feature design goes from 4 pastes to 2, architecture from 5 to 2, stories from 6 to 2. Model and Copilot modes are unchanged, and so is the critic run on its own. Runs shows the batch as one prompt with its lenses listed, and a lens's run links to it. ADR 0185; run record version 6 (`batch`, `parts`, a version 5 record reads without them); local API 2.76.0 (`parts`, `batch` on a run), additive.
- Seventh UX walk (answers and access):
  - **Answers to map questions are offered for Apply after you leave the page.** Propose for review's `applyFirst` (API 2.70.0) lists a repo whose answered map questions (asked by Apply for that repo) are newer than its last Apply, as it did for corrections; Home offers Apply for it, and an answered question on Questions shows Apply while its answer isn't in the map yet.
  - **A change's own answered questions go with its hand-off PR.** The decisions filed under a change id in `questions/` (the question's repo or scope is the change) ride along with every hand-off of the change; one already merged adds nothing to the diff. Open and retired ones don't ride, so they no longer stay "new" in Propose after release.
  - **Focus is seen and kept.** The header's Ask field shows a focus ring (WCAG 2.4.7); after Save the answer, focus moves to what was saved, and after Start the initiative to what was started (WCAG 2.4.3).
  - **Words, not commands.** A question says when it was asked in words ("Asked when Atticus checked forge-ledger-core's map"), and its options say what they change without the command in brackets. A change file's status says "Not written yet" for a missing file or one that holds only the template (API 2.70.0's `written`), not READY. Initiatives say their status in words, the form closes after Start, the plan's changes are listed under Changes to start, and "no plan yet" points at Plan it on the page.
  - **Pages in their section.** Initiatives and Manual steps sit with Home, Governance with Set up, instead of showing Map's or an empty change's tabs.
  - **Errors say what the server answered.** A failure with no reason of Atticus's own shows the status and the server's words; Home names runtime.yaml only when the problem does, and no longer tells you to check that atticus ui is running for every error.
  - **Count tiles on Questions.** Answered and Retired are counts, not disabled buttons; Open and All still pick the list's filter.
- Seventh UX walk (tasks and manual steps):
  - **A task whose PR merged is ticked, and a person can tick or untick one from the Task board.** Listing the tasks ticks a task in tasks.md once the Git host says its PR merged, once (`taskRun.ticked`; a person who unticks it keeps it unticked), and the board says Atticus ticked it. Mark done and Mark not done tick or untick a task on the person's click (`POST /api/changes/{changeId}/tasks/{taskId}/done`, local API 2.72.0). Only the box changes; the edit rides the change's build hand-off. A task on the Task list opens its row on the Task board (`?task=`), where it runs; its terminal commands stay below.
  - **A new task starts from the tasks merged before it.** Before a task's branch is cut, the local change branch fast-forwards to the Git host's, where earlier task PRs merged. A local branch with commits of its own while the host's has moved on is never rewritten: the run is refused, saying to merge the host's branch first.
  - **A manual Git host goes by the PR's recorded number and link.** Once the person records them, the task's record and the board show the real PR, not `manual://`, and the number Atticus gave it still finds it. Each PR's steps are named by its branch, so a task's steps no longer collide with the metarepo's hand-off steps.
  - **Merged is recorded on the reviews step.** A manual PR's reviews step asks whether it merged on the Git host, so a PR merged there needs no Merge step; a task's PR whose reviews were recorded without its merge is asked about again (a hand-off's waits for its Merge step, after the gate checks approvals), and a reviews step still open on a PR that reads merged or closed is skipped, saying why.
  - **Marking a hand-off PR's step done syncs the change.** `POST /api/todo/{stepId}/done` for a Git host step of a change's open hand-off syncs it, as Sync does, and returns what sync said (`synced`, local API 2.72.0), so the gate moves on with no second click. Sync stays read-only: it never merges.
- Round 43 (review, tasks):
  - **A task whose PR was merged or closed runs again from the app.** The app's Run with AI didn't give its Git host to the check that holds a task with an open PR, so the run was refused saying no Git host was connected. It now asks the Git host (read-only) whether the PR is still open, as `atticus task run` does. The Task board hears it too: listing the tasks asks the Git host for each raised PR's state (read-only, a short timeout, the recorded state when the host can't say; `taskRun.pr.state`, local API 2.70.0), says a PR merged or closed, and offers Run with AI again, where before it showed the PR open for ever.
  - **An answer that changes nothing needs a person, never a built task with nothing to raise.** A build with nothing to commit left the task built with no commit, so the Task board offered Raise, which refused it, and at the pr level an empty PR was opened. The task now needs a person, with a note that the AI's answer changed nothing (run it again or do it by hand); no PR is opened, and Raise is shown only for a task with a commit.
- Seventh UX walk (what's next):
  - **What's next follows the change's state and gate, never a later stage** (`atticus next`, At a glance, Home, the stage pages; ADR 0006 addendum 2026-11-08). It took the stage OpenSpec's status named, so at define it said "then run the architecture stage again" and at design "has an approved design: write its stories" before the design PR was raised. Now: the state's own stage's questions, then that stage again (said as "its questions are answered: run the stage again" once they are); with the stage done, Raise hand-off; with the hand-off raised, its merge (`merge`, carrying `pr`); with changes requested, hand off again; with every task ticked, the build or QA hand-off instead of `atticus change status`. Home's Run next reads the change's cockpit, so it no longer says "at the feature stage" while the PR waits. `atticus next` no longer runs `openspec status`.
  - **"What blocks it" says when a stage's questions are answered**: the gate said the stage "waits for a person to answer its questions" after every one was answered; it now says to run the stage again. Raise hand-off at a glance reads the gate again when files change.
  - **Run next puts the in-app action first**: New change on Home, Raise hand-off for a hand-off, the Task board for a task, Feature brief, Questions, Release, Prepare or Run for an AI step, the PR for a merge, or the page that does the step; the command stays copyable under "Or in a terminal". The same on At a glance, the stage pages and the no-change panel.
  - **A manual Git host's PR is a step waiting on a person, never a `manual://` link**: `change status`, At a glance, Release ("WAITS ON YOU", and Ask for go / no-go says the step is yours) and Across changes say so with a link to Home › Manual steps, instead of "Merge PR #4 once it is approved (manual://…)" and "Go / no-go PR OPEN".
  - C4 2.70.0: the cockpit's `next.pr`. Additive only.
- Seventh UX walk (setup and stories):
  - **A repo's build command is set on Set up › Repos, and the org pack's owner on Home**, each on a person's click, writing the same files the CLI writes.
  - **The story bar reads the stories stage's own format**, so a story the stage and its critic passed is ready, and the change id and generated lines are no longer "unknown names".
  - **The Stories tab shows the stories**, read-only with Edit.
  - **Flows with no spec yet offer the spec step**, and the change diagram's "a step taken out" says why and offers it too; Questions offers "Ask AI to propose answers" while questions are open.
  - **Wording**: a change in build with open tasks says how many are ticked, a drawn stroke reads "drawn on the canvas by pat", and a new flow drawn from a component names its repo.
- Seventh UX walk (release):
  - **What's next names the release PRs before go/no-go**: raise them, then merge each on the Git host; the step opens Release in the app.
  - **The code reaches the default branch (ADR 0184).** Task PRs merged into `change/<change>`, but nothing raised the PR from there into each code repo's default branch, so a released change left every repo's main untouched. **Raise the release PRs** on the Release page (`atticus change release-prs <id> --approve`, `POST /api/changes/{changeId}/release-prs`, local API 2.70.0) opens one PR per code repo from the change branch into its default branch, on the person's click. It records each PR in the change's repos.yaml. Atticus pushes the change branch only where the host lacks it, never forced, and never pushes the default branch. It refuses a protected change branch and any other branch. A person merges each PR on the Git host, and Sync reads them back, with the host's checks as the build. A manual Git host gets each PR as a step that records the real link. Go/no-go now waits for every repo's release PR to be merged (raised only, with `archive.requireMergedPrs: false`), and so does the archive gate. The Release page lists each repo's PR. A Copilot session can't run the command with `--approve`.
  - **No more false drift at the archive (ADR 0074).** The archive gate ran the built-in readers on the change's stale worktree and reported calls "no longer in the code" that the code still had. It now compares each repo with the facts read and verified at the merged commit, and the catalog written from them. If the facts are of another commit, it says to read the repo again (Map › Trust › Freshness), or to sync first when the clone hasn't moved. The readers' differences on such a repo are a note. A repo the assistant never read is still checked by the readers, on its clone of the default branch.
- Round 42 (review):
  - **One plural rule for endpoint ids and the glossary**, in core. Round 43 narrowed it so only wrong ids move. **Ids that change on upgrade:**
    - Endpoints whose last word is a known -us noun in the plural: `/payment-statuses` is `get-payment-status` in every version, and `/status` is `get-status` (it was `get-statu` in beta.20).
    - Endpoints ending in `responses`, `cases` or another -ses word that isn't a known -us noun: they lose only their s (`get-response`, not `get-respons`).
    - Glossary terms ending in such a noun ("Payment statuses" is `term.payment-status`, not `term.payment-statuse`), and in "aliases", "analyses", "crises", "diagnoses" or "theses".
    - A person's glossary definition or link aimed at an old id is reported as not in the catalog: aim it at the new id.
  - **A stage's write entries stay inside the change folder**, with a `*` only in the last part; an org pack that writes otherwise is refused at load, not followed.
  - **A stage's step with options (`--resume`) gets the app's words too**, and At a glance sends the brief step to Feature brief, not to a path to copy.
- Round 42 (review, task PRs):
  - **A second task's PR is raised after the first one is merged.** Raising a task's PR pushed this machine's change branch too, which is behind once a person merges a task PR on the Git host, so the push was refused and every later task PR failed. The change branch is now pushed only when the host hasn't got it (never over it), and then together with the task branch (`--atomic`), so one is never pushed without the other; the PR goes into the host's change branch.
  - **A task whose push or PR failed can be raised again.** It stays built with a note that starts "The PR wasn't raised:" and says why, instead of needing a person while Raise refused anything but built. The Task board shows that note and offers Raise the task's PR again; the app's Raise answers 422 with the reason (201 only when the PR is raised); `atticus task run --level pr` ends 1 when its PR wasn't raised.
  - **Raise pushes only what was built.** A task branch with commits after the build is refused, with nothing changed, saying to run the task again so its build checks them (or to push it and open its PR by hand), rather than pushing commits the PR's "Build passed" never covered. Pushing the built commit instead would leave the branch here ahead of the one on the host.
  - **A task with an open PR isn't run again over it.** `atticus task run` (and `--dry-run`) on a task whose PR is open is refused, saying to merge or close the PR first; once the Git host says it is merged or closed, the task runs again and notes what became of the earlier PR. Before, the run wrote over the record that named the PR, and the next raise collided with it.
- Sixth UX walk (what's left):
  - **What's next skips a task that is drafted, with the person's assistant or with its PR open**, and says that its PR is on the Task board.
  - **After a change's question is answered, Questions offers the stage's step from the app**, with the command after it ("Or on the command line"), and calls the answer's scope "This change only".
  - **A stage waiting on its questions says to answer them on Questions**, then run the stage again.
  - **A step's input carries the brief's title once**, not twice.
- Sixth UX walk (Bring in and ids):
  - **Bring in says a change's merged record is merged**, not one of "your own edits", and a long list of edits names five and counts the rest. "1 file were already the same" reads "1 file was".
  - **An endpoint's id keeps a word that only ends in s whole**: `PUT /accounts/{id}/status` is `put-status`, not `put-statu`; `/responses` and `/cases` lose only their s.
- Sixth UX walk (next steps):
  - **What's next no longer offers a needs step it can't prepare.** `atticus next` and a change's At a glance offered "what it needs" from the moment the feature was placed, and its Prepare failed with no knowledge items (knowledge is an optional setup step). It is offered only when there are knowledge items, and after the stage's own step, so the step that moves the change on is always the one shown: the questions its stage stopped on ("answer 2 questions on Questions, then run the feature stage again"), at Design the architecture stage, at Plan the stories stage. A change's questions count those its stages filed under the repos they are about, and they are asked for once.
  - **Before the brief is written, What's next says to write it** (the brief's file), not to run the feature stage.
  - **A step run from the app says what to do next in the app.** Prepare's and Done's lines said to run `atticus handoff done …` and the step again with `--resume`, `atticus questions answer`, or the stage's hand-off command; they now say the next prompt is ready on Runs, to answer on Questions, to prepare the stage again on its page, and to raise the stage's hand-off on the change page. The command line keeps its commands.
  - "Reviewers: none named" after a hand-off is raised ends with a full stop, so it no longer runs into the next sentence.
- Sixth UX walk (stages and tasks):
  - The plan hand-off carries everything the stories stage writes: the OpenSpec specs (`specs/`), the proposal naming them, `needs.yaml`, `needs.md` and the task prompts (`tasks/*.prompt.md`), beside `requests.md` and the answer. They used to stay local edits, so main never got the specs. The design hand-off also carries the architecture stage's deploy order and the flows it only reads, and the define hand-off the feature stage's `proposal.md`. A stage's write entry may now be a `*` pattern in its last part (ADR 0087 addendum); a task run's files under `tasks/<task>/` stay out.
  - A task run's prompt names one place to work: the task context inside it now says the run's own worktree (`.tasks/<task>/<repo>`) and branch, as the prompt's top does, not the change's worktree, which is on the change branch.
  - Spec Kit's plan.md takes its Summary from the repo's flows under design.md's Target flows when design.md has no Approach (the architecture stage's design.md has none), then from OpenSpec's Decisions, so "design.md has no Approach section" no longer stands in for a design that is there. design.md's Rollout and rollback is written for a change that writes no table too, saying a redeploy of each repo's previous version, in reverse order, undoes it, as the design artifact's instruction and the release plan ask.
- Sixth UX walk (screens):
  - **Build has a step after a task's Done.** The Task board says where each task's Run with AI stands, from its `task-run.yaml` (drafted, unverified, PR raised, with your assistant, needs a person), links its run, and offers **Raise the task's PR** on a drafted, built task: the person's click is the approval, as Raise hand-off's is, and it makes the push and PR the pr level makes without running the AI step again (`POST /api/changes/{changeId}/tasks/{taskId}/pr`, local API 2.69.0; tasks carry `taskRun` and `run`). A task drafted without a build (no build command) says why Atticus won't open its PR and what to do instead. A drafted task no longer shows Run with AI; one that stopped shows Run with AI again.
  - At a glance doesn't send the person back to a drafted task: when its next step names one, it says the task's PR is the step (with Raise the task's PR) and gives the first task not run yet beside it. A hand-off next step has Raise hand-off beside its command.
  - On a manual Git host, a hand-off or task PR nobody has opened yet is said as a step waiting on Home › Manual steps, with a link there, on the change page, the gate, At a glance and the Task board; no `manual://` link is shown as an open PR.
  - Home offers Apply before Propose only while a repo's corrections are newer than its last Apply (`applyFirst` in `GET /api/propose`), and after Push the lines about what waited go, leaving what the push said, and the list is read again.
  - The Task board's AI task runs counts and lists task runs only, not the critics' and stages' runs.
- Round 41 (review, continued):
  - One AI step and one local step at a time (local API 2.68.0): the server's job list tracks every running job of a kind, so a second job ending no longer hides the first, still running; `POST /api/runs` answers 409 while another AI step runs (two at once could each report the other's run), and Apply, Propose for review and Bring in answer 409 while another of them runs (Bring in moving the working tree while Apply writes facts). Each refusal names the job that runs, and the app's Apply says the server's words instead of "Verify found problems".
  - Org pack: two dialects or two checklist packs of one name (a copy in a subfolder, say), or a pack reusing another's rule id, are load problems naming both files; only the first is loaded, so the scanner no longer runs both and `extends` no longer resolves to the last. Problems about dialects, patterns, agents, service-desk templates and procedures name the file they are in, not one built from the record's id.
- Draw the change: the preview is the write (the flow's file, and design.md / data.md where Atticus wrote them); a write after the target flows changed is refused; Undo takes back a whole stroke (a retarget's two steps) and only strokes, never a step the stage wrote; a stroke lands on the component's own flow, never a sibling's; a topic's listener step goes nowhere (one line, publisher → listener); a delta file that doesn't read blocks strokes instead of being lost; the panel keeps its state when the first stroke or last Undo redraws the picture, and Draw it turns off after a failed write. `change sketch`: dotted Mermaid lines read, chains and `&` are reported rather than guessed, parameterised paths aren't marked new, a retired line removes the step from every flow that makes it.
- Safety and robustness (review): a Markdown folder or a cloned wiki never reads a link (a planted link to a key or a credentials file stays out of the knowledge store; wikis clone with `core.symlinks=false`); PDF bombs are read within one budget per file (each stream decoded once, 256 MB in all, a lying object count bounded, at most 10,000 pages); the events socket closes on an unmasked or oversized client frame; the server follows a folder removed and made again, and `.atticus/` made after it started; a build command's timeout stops everything it started (the whole tree on Windows) and the log keeps the last 4 MB of its output; a branch checkout with a person's edits is refused rather than overwritten; a change folder is named by an id only; drift merges with the organisation's component types (no false drift); the C6 page's component-type fields have their descriptions back.
- Round 41 (review, facts and Bring in):
  - **Facts read at a later commit, or on another branch, stay in the facts file when the clone is checked out behind them.** Round 40 kept only facts of a commit the clone hadn't got; a clone put back on an older commit still diffed backwards and dropped the facts that cite a changed file.
  - **Bring in never takes what merged as the base of a change's record.** It uses the hand-off branch, here or on the remote; with neither, a record both sides changed is refused, not merged so as to drop the reviewer's edit.
  - **Bring in puts the person's own record back when the fast-forward fails**, unstages a new record so the fast-forward can go ahead, and refuses (rather than failing) when the record was deleted here and changed in what merged.
- Round 41 (review):
  - **A SharePoint export page whose front matter can't be read is refused.** Before, a typo in its YAML dropped its classification and the page was read as if it had none. The sync now lists it as skipped, with the error. Front matter closed by `...`, or by `---` with a trailing space, is stripped from the body too.
  - **An Excel cell past a long run of blank columns is read.** Before, the cell itself was skipped. Now the blanks are left out (at most 1,000 kept), with a note once per sheet. The note that the workbook had too many cells also shows when the limit is reached at the end of a row.
  - **A folder or Git wiki page whose front matter doesn't read is skipped with why, too**, never read at the default classification.
- Round 40 (review):
  - **Facts read at a commit the clone doesn't have stay in the facts file, and are still refused.** Before, a verify (or the app's Apply) on the wrong checkout emptied `<repo>.jsonl`, and the next one wrote a catalog without that repo's records.
  - **What's next asks the gate whether the draft is missing.** On the stage path the define gate needs no separate draft, so a placed change is handed off, not sent to `change draft`, which refused.
  - **Bring in merges a change's `change.yaml` three ways.** Sync's newer state and a reviewer's edit on the hand-off are both kept, and a clash between them is refused with nothing changed.
- Round 39 (review) and the fifth UX walk:
  - **Bring in what merged works after a hand-off merges.** A change's `change.yaml`, which Sync rewrites with the gate's newer state, is carried across the merge, never called "your edit".
  - **What's next follows the gates.** A change placed on the estate but without its proposal or specs is drafted next, not handed off. A change in Design that isn't placed yet is placed first, before its architecture.
  - **On a manual Git host, Raise hand-off says the PR is a step to do on Home**, not that it is open.
  - **A failed Apply on a correction leaves a note on the page.** Done's note leaves out the terminal commands the app has already run.
  - **What a prepared step says always reaches the person**: its messages, the problems with an answer, the questions it raised and the pages left, never an empty "the step prepared no run". After Done finishes a step, the stale "run the step again with --resume" line is left out.
  - **What Done did, and the step's next prompt, stay on the page as a note** after the run reloads as validated.
- Round 38 (review) and the fourth UX walk:
  - **A step prepared in the app is finished in the app.** Done checks the answer, then runs the step's resume, which writes what the answer said (a new change's proposal and specs) or offers the next prompt with a link. A new change now goes from Define to hand-off without a terminal. At a glance also offers _Prepare it for your assistant_ for the draft. Prepare names a run that still waits for its answer. A run that fails again shows its new problems (API 2.67.0: Done gives `lines` and `next`).
  - **After a correction's Apply takes its panel away, a note stays on the page** saying it was applied and linking to Home. Home's pending list offers Apply for each repo with an unapplied correction before Propose.
  - **A plan item of a commitment recorded at risk or slipped says so too**, matching the commitment.
  - **The record of what was proposed lives in `.index/`, which Git ignores.** Before, it sat at the metarepo's root as an untracked file, and Bring in listed it as your own edit. A proposal whose branch is gone (merged and deleted, or closed) stops being marked, since Bring in prunes, and the label says "already proposed" rather than "in review".
  - **A SharePoint Markdown page saved with a BOM keeps its front matter out of its text.**
  - **An older beta refuses a metarepo a newer beta upgraded** (and `atticus doctor` says so), and no longer stamps it back to its own version. Versions now compare in semver order, with pre-releases: beta.30 is newer than beta.21.
- Round 37 (review) and the third UX walk:
  - **A failed hand-off run's page no longer crashes on reload**: the run list carries each run's errors.
  - **A new change can leave Define in the app.** A proposal not written yet opens empty with Edit, and Save writes it. Feature brief and At a glance offer _Prepare it for your assistant_ for the change's draft (proposal and specs).
  - **A commitment recorded at risk or slipped shows so**, until the dates say worse.
  - **After a correction, the app says Apply then Propose for review** (here or later from Home), not "it runs no command". Bring in what merged says when nothing new has merged, and shows once.
  - **An AI step on Knowledge shows its Run or Prepare once.**
  - **A SharePoint export's Markdown page keeps its own classification and restriction** (LF or CRLF). Before, a page marked confidential or restricted was read as internal and went into the snapshot and to the model.
  - **A spreadsheet that names a far-off row or column no longer hangs the reader, the index or the app.** Cells past Excel's limits are skipped with a note, blank gaps are bounded, and a read has a budget.
  - **Two proposals (or a proposal and a hand-off) at once each push only their own files**: one publish at a time per metarepo, each in its own index.
  - **A Copilot session can't answer an owner question or start or plan an initiative** (`questions owner`, `initiative new|plan|request` join the person-only list).
  - **Files already in an open proposal say so and aren't offered again**, on Home or in Propose for review, until their content changes.
  - **runtime.yaml's mock Git host pushes nothing**, so a dry run of the process with it reads as before.
  - **Apply, Propose and Bring in are their own kind of job**, so a slow one never reads as "an AI step is running".
- Round 36 (review) and the Windows review:
  - **The second UX walk's fixes.**
    - The Task board's runs and a prepared task link to their run page.
    - _Prepare it for your assistant_ links the run a step still waits on. A stage with nothing left to hand off says what it did, never "wasn't prepared".
    - A change started on Changes shows in the list at once, and the form closes.
    - The critic, Raise hand-off and Propose no longer say a PR is open when a manual Git host records it as a step.
    - Code excerpts on Questions can be scrolled by keyboard.
    - A task that waits points to Done on its run page.
  - **A wiki's scan runs from Knowledge › Sources** (or is prepared for your assistant). **Compare a prototype and attach a design export on Prototypes and UX. Fill a service-desk draft's missing fields in the app**, written into `snow.md`, then Propose for review. Filing still needs your click (API 2.67.0).
  - **Home lists what you changed in the app and haven't proposed yet**, with Propose for review, so a correction or an answer whose page has gone can still be proposed. Proposals now carry the map and specs that Apply writes, and an audit's proposal carries only its audit file.
  - **Bring in what merged takes a file deleted both here and in what merged as the same edit.**
  - **Initiatives start, are planned and have their commitments recorded in the app**: _New initiative_ (title, journey, target release), _Plan it_ (the system it is about), and _Update_ on a commitment (open, at risk, done or slipped, with a note kept in `initiatives/<id>/notes.yaml`; `atticus initiative commit --note` too). Each save, and recording an outcome after release, offers _Propose for review_. Propose for review in a metarepo that isn't a Git repository says so.
  - **A hand-off whose answer failed its checks says why on its run page and keeps Done and Cancel.**
  - **Owner questions are answered on the Questions page** (Kind › Owner questions) or with `atticus questions owner <id> --text`, then proposed for review (API 2.67.0). Home's question counts and links now match what the Questions page shows, and accepting the baseline offers Propose for review instead of a commit step.
  - **Apply, Propose for review and Bring in what merged run as server jobs** (202 and the job, API 2.66.0). Before, in a VS Code panel a slow push or verify was reported as failed after 30 seconds while it went on and opened the PR.
  - **Bring in what merged is where you come back**: on Home and on the change's At a glance, and on the command line as `atticus workspace bring-in`, not only right after a proposal.
  - **The assistant files, a manual Git host's step files, hand-down files and initiatives wait for a file Windows holds** (an editor or virus scanner) and leave no temporary file behind when a write fails.
  - **Bring in what merged decides before it touches anything.** A branch with its own commits, or an edit of yours that the merged commits also change, is refused with nothing changed and the files named. A staged new file is set aside cleanly. If the fast-forward still fails, every file is put back as it was. A remote that can't be reached is said, never "Something went wrong".
- Round 35 (review):
  - **An answer's proposal carries the org-pack files it wrote**: a repo type's skill file, a host in the manifest, an outside system in a profile.
  - **Bring in what merged**: after a proposal or a hand-off merges, your copy of the metarepo catches up. Before, `git pull` refused because the same edits were still uncommitted.
  - **Each proposal has its own branch**, a rename carries its old path's removal, and a Git host's refusal is said with what was already done, never as "Something went wrong".
  - **Raise hand-off in the app says when the branch couldn't be pushed.**
  - **An AI step prepared for your assistant has its own route** (`POST /api/runs/handoff`, API 2.66.0), so the contract says what it returns.
- Round 34 (review) and the UX review:
  - **The app runs the local steps and proposes them for review (ADR 0183, the product owner's decision; API 2.65.0).** After an answer or a correction, **Apply** runs `atticus verify <repo> --write`. **Propose for review** (after an answer, a correction, a sample audit or the AI settings) lists the changed files, the branch and the title. On your yes it pushes exactly the ticked files to that branch and opens the pull request. A person merges on the Git host.
  - **A hand-off's branch is pushed before its PR is asked for.** Before, nothing in production pushed it, so a live Git host refused the PR and a manual one's step named a branch nobody had made. If Atticus can't push, it says so and names the branch.
  - **Raise hand-off waits until nothing blocks the gate**, and says how many things do. Before, it stayed the primary button and a click returned the whole list as an error.
  - **The Stories and Tasks tabs open with the page where the work is done**: _Write stories with AI_ (Breakdown) and _Run tasks with AI_ (the Task board).
  - **Set up › AI offers Copilot, unattended (copilot-acp, ADR 0171).** Choosing it on a version 2 `runtime.yaml` lifts the file to version 3.
  - **In a browser with no model, an AI step is prepared for your own assistant**: _Prepare it for your assistant_ writes the prompt (`POST /api/runs/handoff`, API 2.66.0). The run's page has _Copy the prompt_ and _Done_, so stories, feature design and architecture no longer stop at a terminal command.
  - **A change starts in the app.** _New change_ (on Changes, Home and Run next) takes a title, an id made from it and a type from the org pack (`GET /api/change-types`, API 2.64.0).
  - **A run waiting on its hand-off answer has Done and Cancel on its Model runs page**, and the gate's waiting lines point there instead of only to `atticus handoff done`.
  - **Task board › Run with AI opens the task's worktree itself** (and clones the repo first when it isn't cloned yet), so it no longer stops at "has no worktree… run atticus workspace open".
  - **A Copilot session can't send a digest (`--send`), name a person (`--as`), set reading rules active or off, record a commitment or an outcome.** A test holds every approve flag in the command catalog to the person-only list. `rules draft` puts the layer file back if the session changed anything a person set.
  - **The same audit answer again keeps who gave it and its note; a new note is recorded as its writer's.**
- Round 33 (review):
  - **The CLI also refuses `--approve`, `--accept` and `--accept-proposal` inside a Copilot session**, not just the session's own check, so a session can't approve a hand-off, a submission or a task's pull request in its own shell. The same applies to `repo add`, a person's repo description (`repo describe --description` or `--reject`), `knowledge keep`, `alias` and `glossary define`, `link` and `alias`.
  - **A note added to an audit answer already on file keeps who answered it, and when.**
- Round 32 (review):
  - **A Copilot session Atticus starts acts as no one.** Before, it could answer questions (`questions sheet --apply`), answer an audit, verify or reject a knowledge item, accept a proposal, act on a finding, or mark a todo or need done, all as the person. A planted comment in a repo could ask for any of these. Now the session's check and the CLI itself refuse every command that records a person's decision, and no session may write a repo's audit answers or a person's corrections (ADR 0171 addendum).
  - **Opening a change's workspace again keeps IntelliJ's `.idea/`** (shelved changes, changelists, run configurations). Atticus writes only its own files and removes only the modules no longer listed.
  - **IntelliJ and `atticus doctor` find a program on a quoted PATH entry**, as VS Code already did.
  - **Each clone keeps `core.longpaths` in its own config**, so the person's git and editor also cope with paths past 260 characters, not only Atticus's own git commands.
  - **In the Sample audit, editing only the note of an item answered before is saved** with the answer on file. Before, it was dropped without a word.
- Round 31 (review):
  - **Contracts on a large estate are a page, not a silent first 200.** Each list says how many it shows of how many, has Show more and a filter by id or name, and the Contracts ring counts the whole catalog. The local API's catalog list takes a `confidence` filter (API 2.63.0).
  - **A Flyway migration Atticus suggests follows the repo's versions** (`V1_2_10__…` after `V1_2_9__…`, compared as numbers, padding and `__` kept). Before, `V1_2_10` sorted before `V1_2_9`.
  - **`atticus unadopt` restores a CRLF `CLAUDE.md` or `AGENTS.md`**, and writing the assistant files keeps a file's CRLF line endings.
  - **A clone that fails leaves no half-made folder behind**, and on Windows clones use `core.longpaths`. A "Filename too long" failure says how to turn long paths on for git.
  - **The starter org pack's README counts the code checks it ships.**
- Round 30 (review):
  - **Both editors read the offline bundle's `atticus.cmd` as the bundle writes it** (`%~dp0..\<version>\dist\bin.js`). They now start node directly. Before, every command went through cmd.exe, so Copilot's tool calls (JSON with quotes) and any argument with `&` were refused. A test reads the shim line from the bundle's generator, so the editors and the bundle can't drift apart again.
  - **Enter in a Todo step's "Reason to skip" skips the step with the reason.** Before, it marked the step done and lost the reason.
  - **A second Save in the Repos form after clearing the default branch, or after spaces around it, isn't refused.**
  - **The printed "Answer once for all" commands quote `--match` in double quotes**, which cmd.exe passes on (it kept single quotes). The commands also say how to set `ATTICUS_MAX_OLD_SPACE` in PowerShell and cmd.
- Round 29 (review):
  - **VS Code on Windows starts `atticus` the way Windows finds it.** It takes `atticus.cmd` rather than the extensionless Git Bash script npm writes beside it, and finds it on a quoted PATH entry. Before, every command went through cmd.exe, and an argument with `&`, `%` or `!` was refused.
  - **A second Save in the same Repos form isn't refused.**
  - **Settings › This machine › Check again keeps the checks shown while they run**, and says when they were checked.
  - **A simple TrueType or Type1 font with a ToUnicode map is read one byte a code**, whatever the map's codespace says. A code the map leaves out reads as Latin-1.
  - **A branch scan drops a repo the manifests no longer list.**
  - **A failed upgrade says where the backup is** and which steps had already run.
- Round 28 (review):
  - **PDFs from Chrome and Edge Save as PDF, Confluence exports and iText-made files are read as text.** Atticus now reads their Type0 fonts through each font's ToUnicode map. Before, the reader produced garbage and flagged nothing. A font with no such map gives no text and is flagged for a person to read.
  - **`atticus upgrade` writing a change's stories again.**
    - It backs up the whole change folder first, recursively. Before, the backup it reported was empty, and hand edits to tasks.md, stories and specs were written over.
    - If the backup fails, the upgrade stops before changing anything.
    - A rewrite that fails is reported, not followed by "Done".
  - **Scanning a branch from one repo's row on Set up › Repos keeps the other repos' scans of that branch.** Before, it erased them.
  - **Word text boxes and tracked moves are read once.**
  - **`claude` is found on a quoted PATH entry.**
  - **The Repos page's Edit forms open with what the manifest says now.** Save sends what the form showed, and a manifest changed since then (for example by a teammate's pull) is refused (409, local API 2.62.0), not written over.
  - **Picking another box or change shows loading, not the last one's data.**
  - **Placing a need's quote in a long file takes one pass, not tens of seconds.**
  - **IntelliJ's task, story and record pickers follow sources.yaml's layout and read ANSI files.**
  - **`atticus upgrade` keeps a Windows file's CRLF** in gates.yaml, change-types.yaml and sources.yaml.
- Round 27 (review):
  - **Build output beside its build is skipped anywhere, `src/` included.** Examples are a .NET project's `bin/` and `obj/` beside its `.csproj`, a web app's `dist/` and `coverage/` beside `package.json`, `target/` beside `pom.xml` and `build/` beside Gradle. Other folders with those names below `src/` are still read.
  - **`skills write` on a Windows checkout of the org pack.** It no longer marks every file "kept as edited", and `--check` says current.
  - **Headless Claude Code on Windows.**
    - `claude` is found on PATH the way Windows finds it: the native `claude.exe` runs directly, and npm's `claude.cmd` runs through cmd.exe.
    - Every word is quoted, so a metarepo path with `&` stays whole.
    - The timeout ends the whole process tree.
  - **On an older Node, `atticus --version` still answers.** The editors no longer report Atticus as not installed when only Node is too old.
  - **A hand-off's changed files are listed as they are.** Accented and renamed names come through intact, and that listing can no longer leave the task stuck.
  - **The app's Cancel of a run that isn't on this machine.** It says when a Done holds the task (409).
- Round 25B (review, the rest of the product):
  - **Folders named like build output are read when they are the product's own.** A package folder named `out`, `bin`, `vendor`, `build`, `target`, `dist` or `coverage` below `src/` is now read, counted and planned. Examples are a hexagonal `adapter/out/`, a card `bin/` lookup or an integration `vendor/`. `bin`/`obj` beside a .NET project and `vendor` beside `go.mod`, `composer.json` or a `Gemfile` are still skipped. Before, any such folder at any depth was skipped, so a Spring service's outbound calls under `adapter/out/` never reached the map.
  - **A Windows checkout's CRLF line endings aren't edits.** The stage loop, the critic's edited files, `skills write` and the catalog writer no longer take CRLF line endings for a person's edit or a change. Before, every stage file read as edited by someone, and `verify --write` rewrote every record.
  - **Facts files written from PowerShell.** A UTF-8 byte-order mark no longer costs the first fact, and a UTF-16 facts file is named as one.
  - **Copy-and-paste commit commands work in PowerShell 5.1 and cmd.** They are now two lines (`git add`, then `git commit`). PowerShell 5.1 rejects `&&`.
  - **Headless Claude Code installed by npm on Windows.** `claude.cmd` now runs through cmd.exe, and a stop ends its whole tree.
  - **Git wiki pages with non-ASCII or quoted names keep their dates.**
  - **An older Node says "Atticus needs Node.js 22.13 or later".** The troubleshooting guide already listed this message.
- Round 26 (review, task runs):
  - **A failure after a hand-off is validated ends the task for a person, with what it said.** The failure can be a pre-commit hook, commit signing, a push the proxy refuses or a protected branch. Before, the run stayed stuck for good.
  - **A task waiting on a run that isn't on this machine no longer blocks the task.** This happens when `.atticus/.runs` is per machine, for example after a teammate's pull. Cancel ends that wait, and Done says the run isn't here.
  - **The app's Run with AI says why a task can't run (422).** It no longer says "Something went wrong", and it makes no Git host it won't use.
  - **A cancelled run's Done says it was cancelled.**
  - **The id suggested for a reserved name is always one Windows can open.**
- Change ids Windows can open: a title such as "Con" or "Aux" gets the id `con-change`, and an id typed as one of Windows' reserved names (con, nul, com1…) or ending in a dot is refused with another to use, so a change made on macOS or Linux never breaks the metarepo's checkout on Windows. The id rule itself (C1) is unchanged; tightening it is planned with an ADR in beta.22.
- Round 25 (review, ADR 0036 addendum):
  - The app's Cancel on a waiting run takes the task's lock first. A Done running elsewhere, such as the assistant's in a terminal, now gets a 409 saying so rather than a 500, and the Cancel is never undone.
  - Done before the report is written, or with an empty report, waits for it and uses up nothing. A cancelled run's Done says so.
  - A task in a repo capped below the org's default runs at the cap rather than being refused. From the app a task runs at most at the draft level, because the PR is the person's `--approve` in an editor or on the command line.
  - The dry run sees a task that still waits.
  - A stopped Done says it was stopped, not that the build fails.
- Round 24 (review, ADR 0036 addendum):
  - A second run of a task that still waits on your assistant is refused, naming its Done. Before, it wrote over the first run, whose Done then said "valid" with nothing built. Cancel in the app's AI runs panel ends a waiting run, so the task can run again.
  - A run stopped by a signal no longer leaves its lock behind. A lock whose process ID Windows handed to another process, or one older than half a day, is taken over, and the refusal names the lock file.
  - Every refusal that needs `--approve` says the yes is the person's, and that an assistant never approves a push.
  - `--dry-run` at the pr level is only a dry run.
  - With no Git host, the pr level says you push and open the PR yourself.
- Round 23 (review, ADR 0036 addendum):
  - A pr-level task run's Done without `--approve` is refused before anything is built, and names the command with `--approve`. Before, it built, committed and then got stuck. The prompt now asks the person, not the assistant, to approve.
  - A second Done or run of the same task while one runs is refused, and the button is disabled meanwhile.
  - A stop during the model call (Ctrl+C or SIGTERM, which is VS Code's Cancel on macOS and Linux) ends the call and is recorded before Atticus ends. A Cancel that ends the whole process tree (VS Code on Windows, IntelliJ) stops everything the run started but records nothing: the run then shows as it last was, and its lock is taken over by the next run.
  - `atticus task run --dry-run`: the run's own checks (the task, runtime.yaml's default level, the repo's cap, the Git host), with nothing done. A default pr level is that dry run, and the editors then offer Approve and run.
  - Done or Retry on a run whose task needs a person is refused, with where to carry on.
  - Mark done on a failing build says so (422).
- Round 22 (review, ADR 0036 addendum): Mark done in the app on a task run's hand-off now builds, fixes and commits as `atticus handoff done` does (it only checked the answer, and the task run waited for good); VS Code and IntelliJ run a task once (the "dry run" before Approve and run was a whole run, so every task ran twice), asking first only for `--level pr`, whose run without `--approve` now says what it would do and does nothing; a stop also aborts the model call or the assistant's session, refuses a build asked for after it, writes no plan, and a stopped hand-off Done isn't counted as a failed attempt; the AI runs panel shows each run's task run, its state, last note and build log (local API 2.61.0, `taskRun`).
- Round 21 (review): Ctrl+C on `atticus ui` (or closing VS Code) while a task's build runs now ends the task run too: the build is reported stopped and the run does nothing more (no fix round, no model answer written, no commit), recorded for a person; Windows included, where the build was stopped by the console and the fix rounds used to run after "Stopped."; a stop signal is no longer swallowed after the app's own listener has gone; ADO and ServiceNow exports under `inputs/` that are links to files elsewhere aren't read.
- Round 20 (review): a Confluence or SharePoint export, the manual wiki's export folder and the diagrams folder skip a file that is a link to one elsewhere, as the Markdown folder already did; Ctrl+C on `atticus ui` while a task's build runs stops the build and then the app cleanly (exit 0, "Stopped.") instead of ending it by the signal; the Sketch panel shows it is loading rather than "none drawn yet", and says when the component types can't be read, with a retry.
- Releases (review): every GitHub release now carries the VS Code `atticus-<version>.vsix` and the IntelliJ `atticus-intellij-<version>.zip`, with `SHA256SUMS.txt` (`release-editor-plugins.yml`), which the IntelliJ guide already promised; the IntelliJ plugin is 0.5.0, with change notes for what it gained since 0.4.0 (the too-old atticus notice, Open in your editor opening here, the canvas's exports saving).
- Round 19 (review): a stray folder in openspec/changes (`_templates`, `WIP`) is skipped again rather than breaking every list of changes, and a mistyped change id gets the usage words; a PDF whose one text stream is shown by thousands of pages is refused within a 64 MB text budget instead of running out of memory, and a merely corrupt stream is skipped rather than called a bomb; a build stops with Atticus on Ctrl+C (one signal handler for all running builds, the signal then ends Atticus as before), a timed-out tree that ignores SIGTERM is killed after 5 s, and a run ends even when a grandchild holds its output open; the watcher follows a folder swapped in by a rename, an old watcher's late error never drops its replacement, and a folder followed again stops showing as broken; submodule wikis check out without links; the AI form keeps an edit typed while a save was on its way.
- Pages (review): the bulk action bar settles when what was selected is gone; the AI form takes a change on disk quietly when nothing is edited, and takes what the server stored after a save without calling it a change; a note about a node of the last picture clears with it; Reset's confirm is off while the picture isn't ready; an error's status is the answer's own.
- Component types (review): a cited org-pack type is never demoted to `service` in a merge; a scan's carry-verify checks types; a bad first component fact doesn't take the good one down; a type can be switched off without restating its words; the type's shape is drawn; the legend's filter keeps the rest of yours; the stage is told the type ids; `types add` takes any record id.
