- Pages: a lens change on the Architecture tab keeps the change; a node that couldn't be opened is said beside the picture, which stays; Reset asks first; Home says which parts of "Needs a person" didn't load and why a bulk action failed, and the bulk action follows what is selected; an artifact saved by someone else meanwhile offers Reload, keep my text; Ask follows its address; the Diagrams page says when there is no system to draw instead of loading for good; a knowledge document's quoting item opens that item; the knowledge kind filter follows a link; onboarding never ticks Answer questions off when the questions couldn't be read; Outside says when a system isn't there; the Intake facts count and Your notifications show a failed read as such; the AI settings form says when the file changed on disk instead of dropping your edits; map nodes work from the keyboard; a change's diagram, estimate and conformance reload on file changes only; many chips and notes moved to the copy file.
- The app's server answers a request target that isn't a URL with 400 instead of stopping; a mangled page address (#/changes/100%) is a name, not a blank page, and a fault in the app's shell shows a way home; a 500's detail no longer names the metarepo's path; findings routes take ids only; the command palette keeps focus and clamps its selection; links from files and wikis are followed only to web addresses; `runtime.yaml` saved from the app keeps every comment; the local ignore file is written atomically; `--root=<dir>` and a Copilot session's root reach the command log; a symbolic link is said to be one. VS Code and IntelliJ warn once when the installed atticus is older than they need, and the VS Code panel gives up a start that never says its address.
- Draw the change: a flow this change removes takes no stroke; Undo sends the files' fingerprint, so it never takes back a stroke it didn't show; the strokes list follows the file (another tab's stroke appears); `change sketch` reports two-way lines, reads bracketed labels, and a second `--save` draws nothing twice.
- Match with Copilot: a new brief drops the last answer (nothing stale reads as done); a session that ends without an answer is said so; one sloppy line of the answer never loses the good ones; `atticus connect match --read` reads a hand-off's answer back.
- A view of a thing that isn't there (a bookmarked feature that was renamed) is 404, not a blank page; `GET /api/coverage` (ADR 0021) is served; artifact saves and run retry/cancel write atomically and refuse a concurrent change; the Learning page's sandbox folder resolves against the metarepo and `~`; the Systems page shows a failed questions read as such; the Impact box follows a link's id.

- A link a person paired on the canvas is `inferred`, not `verified`, and carries their evidence (`source: manual`, who), so a filter on verified links shows only what the code proves; it isn't drawn as a gap, asked about again, or called "one end seen" in a feature's flow.
- A rule that names no files is recorded as given only by a whole read: a top-up no longer marks it given, and `atticus rules activate` says it waits for `atticus map <repo> --force`. A read and a staleness check work out the repo's type the same way (its checkout's fingerprint too).
- Another team's imported rules: `atticus rules activate <key> --team <team> --type <type>` sets one active; importing again keeps the ones you set active when they haven't changed them (a changed one comes back as a draft, said); `atticus rules list` lists every layer's file; two teams' active rules with one key are said.
- The Connect panel says to verify a repo whose facts aren't all verified (`why: unverified`, local API 2.56.0), instead of "no calls on record"; a pairing to such a repo says the same rather than reading as broken. The cached estate join can no longer be kept under a newer stamp than the files it read.
- The facts view says why a correction or pairing `atticus verify` can't apply, and the evidence says "Paired with <system> by <who>" for a pairing.
- Copilot runs from the app: a poll gets each tab's last lines (`tail`) and fetches a tab's missed lines (`/api/copilot-runs/{runId}/tabs/{tab}`), with **Show earlier lines**; the strip shows a run's end only to a page that saw it run, and a dismissal lasts the session; a new run opens on its Run tab; the dialog names the repos a stale or gaps run reads; Stop on Windows ends the Copilot language server's whole process tree.
- The canvas's exports save in a VS Code panel (the Save dialog) and in IntelliJ's; a panel's call that gets no answer gives up after a minute instead of leaving the bar on; a Run button's job polls no longer flash the loading bar; a kept view opens its lens and box, and keeping a name that exists says Replace.
- Map › Mismatches says when no finding is of the kind picked, or none at all.
- The commit hint of a fact's preview is safe to paste whatever the fact id says (and a fact id is checked); every YAML the app or CLI edits keeps a Windows file's CRLF (host answers, outside systems, descriptions, branches, reading rules, AI settings); the AI settings write refuses when runtime.yaml changed meanwhile (409); canvas layouts and kept views of an adopted repo go under `.atticus/`; a file a person added to an imported skill folder is kept on re-import; Skip to content keeps the page; a link's compare mark and gaps update without a new layout; the Compare snapshot follows a new snapshot and clears when another box is opened; Service desk counts match its change; the To-do change filter can be cleared; the palette never shows an older search's rows; an older lens's late answer never lands on the new picture; Print says when the window was blocked; a VS Code panel's file events are relative to the metarepo.
- A pairing with an outside system makes an inferred link flagged `joined-by-person`, with the person's evidence beside the code's (not a verified one); the verify report counts it as applied.
- Skills: a name the organisation (or another team) already has is never taken by an import, and `skills write` writes one source per name and says which; files another team removed since go on the next import (an edit kept here stays).
- Adding a box previews the lines the write itself makes and keeps a Windows profile's CRLF; the Connect commit hint names the questions folder of an adopted repo; a failed Copilot run still tells the pages the facts changed; the owner write changes the manifest before roles.yaml; a git wiki's `ref` must be a branch or tag name.
- Release, To-do and Service desk opened from a change's tabs show that change; Compare marks against the whole picture; health badges read "3 things missing"; lists cut short say how many more; more words come from the copy file.
- A write on the canvas is of what was previewed: changing Connect's pairs, a new box or an owner after Preview turns the write off until it is previewed again. Connect's Undo can't run twice, and after a failure tries only the lines left.
- Two edits of one manifest, roles file, profile or reading-rules file at once can no longer lose one: a file changed since it was read (by another edit, the CLI or an editor) is left as it is and the edit is refused (409), to be done again.
- A pairing added after a hand edit with no final newline goes on a line of its own; Undo keeps a Windows file's CRLF; Connect's "all calls" answer is checked before anything is written.
- Another team's skills and agents are written with only the frontmatter that describes them; an agent of theirs reads only, in Claude and in Copilot (`Read, Grep, Glob`; Copilot's `read, search`), whatever tools it asks for, and their skills run only when a person runs them (never picked by the model; an ask prompt in Copilot); import names what it left out. Keeping a canvas view under a name kept already replaces it only when asked (the button says Replace). Each imported item records its own remote and tag, never a local path or a sign-in.
- Imported skills: an edit kept here stays kept through every later import; `--only` keeps the record of the rest; another team's instructions never apply by themselves (a person attaches them); their clone's git hooks never run. An imported reading rule whose example changed comes back as a draft, and a rule's example reaches a reader only as `path:line`.
- The Console keeps a tab's last 5,000 lines, as the server does, says when earlier lines are no longer kept, says when Stop or Copy didn't go through, and puts focus on the Console once a run starts. The questions overlay and kept views say when they are loading or failed. In IntelliJ, "Open in your editor" opens the line in the IDE.

## 2.0.0-beta.20 (2026-11-06)

- A gateway route with no forwarding call (no call `from` it) is a gap, as an entry no flow follows is: verify lists it under `entriesNoFlowFollows` (marked `route`) and `atticus map <repo> --gaps` asks for the call it forwards to its RouteRule's target. A proxy read with its routes and none of its forwarding calls drew nothing past the gateway, and a `--match` answer for its calls had nothing to apply to.

## 2.0.0-beta.19 (2026-11-06)

- A call to a host a read repo lists joins that repo only where one of its endpoints serves the call, as the facts join already required: a host many repos call, listed on one repo (say by answering "which repo is this host"), drew every call to it there as an inferred link with nothing cited at that end. A repo not read yet joins by its hosts as before.

## 2.0.0-beta.18 (2026-11-05)

- A question's evidence (and a scan's `at`) may name a file with a space in its path (`Documents/Forge_Accounts_1.0 20250731.yaml:88`): such a question made `atticus verify --write`, and with it `atticus map --all`, stop with "evidence is path:line". The format is otherwise unchanged (no colon in the path, no space at either end).
- One repo's verify failing no longer ends `atticus map --all`: it says why, moves on, and the next run tries that repo again.

## 2.0.0-beta.17 (2026-11-04)

- An `atticus` command a Copilot session runs in Copilot's own shell no longer starts a Copilot session of its own (a session waiting on itself until it timed out): the Copilot language server is started marked as inside a session, and every atticus it starts hands off.
- `atticus map --all` writes a gaps pass's prompt (`--gaps`, and the rest of a part, `--part n --gaps`) before handing the session over, as it does a top-up's, and the gaps prompt names the repo's checkout: the session reads and writes files and runs only `atticus verify`.

- An unattended session gets Atticus's own step text without the workspace check and the closing `atticus next` (a metarepo whose prompt files were behind stopped every session at the check).
- A top-up session is done only once something was written for it (a fact, or a reviewed line for a file with nothing to add, which its prompt now asks for): a passing verify after a session that read nothing gives it again.
- A gaps pass planned in several sessions goes on to the next while the last closed some, instead of counting the repo done after the first; gaps no session closes aren't given again and again.
- A one-session read Atticus stopped (its time or its tool calls ran out) is done only once its coverage leaves nothing.
- Inside a session, `atticus map` starts no read loop of its own, and any atticus finds the session's metarepo wherever Copilot's shell has moved; an atticus command the session runs always runs in the metarepo.
- The Copilot language server not starting, not opening a session or not offering the model stops the run once, instead of trying every part twice; a part retried with nothing to plan from is given itself again, never part 1.
- A session that ends over a refusal is nudged also when Copilot reports it as cancelled or says it couldn't, and the nudge fits any step.

## 2.0.0-beta.16 (2026-11-04)

- A Copilot session that ends right after Atticus refused something (Copilot reads a refusal as "Operation cancelled by user") is told, in the same session, what was refused and why, and to carry on with its own file tools instead of a shell; up to three times.
- A rules top-up's prompt names the repo's checkout in the metarepo, so the session opens its files there instead of searching for it with shell commands.

## 2.0.0-beta.15 (2026-11-04)

### Copilot sessions that need no terminal

- `atticus map --all` writes a rules top-up's prompt itself before handing a session over, and the line it gives (pasted or sent through copilot-acp) says to read that file and run nothing but `atticus verify <repo>`, or nothing at all where the chat can't run commands. A Copilot chat whose terminal won't open (IntelliJ's "Could not create classic terminal window") now finishes the session; Atticus verifies it either way.
- An unattended session is told never to pipe, redirect or chain a command, and to carry on when one is refused instead of ending.
- A top-up planned before is planned again for what is left when another `--models` is asked for (`--models entries` now shrinks one under way); the sessions done stay done.
- A test source set with a suffix (`src/test-qa/`, `src/tests_integration/`) is tests: never read for facts, nor in a top-up.

## 2.0.0-beta.14 (2026-11-04)

### Fewer target questions on gateways and client helpers (ADR 0172)

- A `--match` rule (`/**` included) now answers a gateway's forwarding calls whose URL can't say where they go: a host still a deploy token (`@targetUrl@`) or a placeholder, a target written as the token, or a base with a different host per environment. Their open questions retire on the next `verify --write`, naming the rule.
- A call's path written as a setting (`${forge-remote.uris.download-report}`) is resolved through the repo's `application.properties`/`.yml` config facts, as a host is; a whole URL there gives the host too.
- A call's path that is only the name of the field or variable holding it (`requestRefundPath`) is rejected at verify (`call-path-is-a-name`), and the reading rules tell the assistant to follow it to the literal or the setting's `${key}`. Run `atticus map --all --stale`, then `atticus verify --all --write`.
- `atticus join contracts` and Map › Mismatches say how many calls join nothing yet, so their payload isn't checked against the other side (`counts.unlinked`, local API 2.50.0): every link is checked once its call joins, so these are what to answer or re-read next.

### The Questions page

- Counts at the top: open, answered, retired and all (in the picked repo), and how much is settled; Open and All pick the list.
- The list is grouped by repo, each question a quiet row (two lines, its state and id) instead of a run of links, the one open marked.
- Pick several open questions (or a repo's at once) and answer them the same way: where their calls go (a repo, system or team, with the picker's names offered) or the same words. Each is answered on its own; one it doesn't fit says why and the rest stand.

## 2.0.0-beta.13 (2026-11-04)

### Copilot driven unattended (copilot-acp, ADR 0171)

- runtime.yaml version 3 can allow `copilot-acp`: Atticus starts the GitHub Copilot language server in its Agent Client Protocol mode and runs each step, and each part of `atticus map --all` (also `map <repo>`, `--gaps`, `--force`, `--rules`, and the new `atticus questions propose`), in a new Copilot session on the person's own sign-in, verifying each part and giving it once more with only what is left. A session may read the metarepo, write only its step's result and run only `atticus` commands; these rules aren't settings, and Atticus never handles a sign-in. `atticus doctor` checks the server and its sign-in; `atticus upgrade` moves a runtime.yaml that names the mode to version 3.
- A build or deploy token, `@name@` (Maven resource filtering, or a gateway bundle's target URL replaced from its CI properties at deploy), is a config placeholder like `${name}`: a proxy's target `@targetUrl@` resolves through the CI properties that set it, so its routes join the service on that host instead of being asked about.
- `atticus verify --write` no longer drops a merged record as "isn't valid C1 (a bug): id … Too big": any record id past 120 characters (a long service name and path, joined) is shortened to a stable one, its start and a hash, and every reference to it follows.
- The run record is at version 5: a copilot-acp run keeps the server's name and version beside its model, prompt and full transcript; older records read as before.

## 2.0.0-beta.12.2 (2026-11-03)

### Answers and the Questions page

- `--match "/**"` is a rule for every path of the repo, so one answer covers a proxy that forwards everything to one place (this repo only, unless `--scope repo-type`).
- Each question card on the Questions page shows its id, with a button to copy it.

### The waiting loop agrees with verify (ADR 0170)

- `atticus map --all` counts a part done when verify's coverage leaves no file under it unaccounted (cited, reviewed, excluded or not reached), as verify records it in the read plan, and moves on without a keypress; when verify passes but something is left it says exactly what and that `r` gives only those files. After `--idle` minutes (30) with nothing written it stops waiting once, saying how to carry on, instead of reminding forever.
- `atticus map --all --force [--repos a,b]` reads repos whole again in the same loop, each part by its number (`/atticus-map <repo> --part n`), the facts on file kept as already kept; `atticus map <repo>` (and `--force`) runs that loop for one repo when the reader is Copilot, and the batch hand-off says what to run if you use Copilot. `--models entries` reads the model classes the repo's own entry points reach and leaves those only an outbound client reaches as `notReached` (`--models reachable` reads them).

## 2.0.0-beta.12.1 (2026-11-03)

### Model classes read where they are used (ADR 0169)

- `atticus map <repo>` (the estimate and its parts, a read in batches, `--gaps`, the rules top-up and `map --all`) reads the entry points and clients first, then the request, response and message classes they name and the classes those name in turn, found by text alone for Java, Kotlin, C#, TypeScript, Python and Go; the reader writes their fields. Classes nothing reaches are left out, recorded in the read plan as `notReached` and listed by `--estimate`; `--models all` reads every one (ADR 0169).
- `atticus verify` counts the left-out model classes apart from the files reviewed with nothing to record, so the coverage percentage isn't misleading, and refuses a reviewed glob that covers a controller, route, handler or listener no fact cites: read it.

## 2.0.0-beta.12 (2026-11-02)

### A top-up for reading rules added since a read (ADR 0167), and why calls are left

- A read records which reading rules it was given (the rules' hash and rule sets, in its read plan). A repo whose facts are fresh but whose read lacked rules added since — every repo read before beta.12 lacks beta.11's field types and required, mappings and envelopes, message types, terms and a call's auth and TLS — is `rules-changed` in `atticus estate fresh`, `atticus next` and `doctor --counts`. `atticus map --all --stale` (and `map --all`) now tops it up instead of saying "nothing to read": a new chat per part, given only the rule parts it lacked, the files they apply to and the facts it has by id; `atticus map <repo> --rules` does one repo (Copilot, or batches with a mode), and a passing verify records the rules (ADR 0167).
- `atticus doctor --counts` breaks unresolved calls down by why the join left them (`no-host`, `unresolved-placeholder`, `host-no-route`, `target-no-route`, ...) and asked calls by the join's reason, and counts the open target questions by how many calls each covers (alone, or in groups of 2-4, 5-9, 10+). Counts only, nothing named.

### Questions asked before, put right (ADR 0168)

- `atticus verify <repo> --write` refreshes an open target question asked before in place (same id, its history kept) with today's ranked options, the repos read since among them; one whose calls a group asks now, or an answer's `--match` rule covers, retires saying which; an answered one is never touched. A gateway proxy's routes are asked once per TargetServer and path prefix ("Which repo serves the routes under /accounts/** that forge-core-v1 forwards to core-backend?"), and a host whose calls reach a proxy's routes, that no proxy repo lists, says the one-time fix (list it under `hosts:` on each proxy repo).
- An option says why it is suggested ("serves PUT /accounts/{id}/status and 6 more under /accounts", "its basePath /accounts matches", "named like the host label", "no evidence; listed so you can pick it"), never that a repo answers the call; and open questions sharing a path prefix or host offer "Answer once for all N: --match '/accounts/**'" in `questions show`/`list`/`--json` and on the Questions page, which fills in the rule (local API 2.49.0).

### The Architecture canvas, intake and quieter notes

- The picture no longer waits on the contract check: the canvas asks for it once the picture is drawn, says what it is waiting for in words ("Reading the estate's catalog…", "Laying out N boxes…", "Joining the estate…"), and the check and the estate join are kept in `.atticus/cache/` under the stamp of the files they read, so a restart or another page reuses them.
- A YAML spec the strict parser refuses is read leniently where it is safe (a key written twice keeps the last, more aliases than the strict limit), and the multi-line `servers[].variables` layout of gateway and Swagger UI exports is read when the file is then a spec; what was read so is noted. A file still unread is `unreadable` in `atticus intake list` and its JSON, with the parser's reason and where.
- `atticus map --all`'s summary and each part's header count parts the same way when some are read already ("4 parts to read (of 5 planned; 1 is read already)", "part 1 of 5, 1 of the 4 to read").
- The lines no fact cites no longer list a build file's artifact repositories, registries, distribution and SCM URLs, CI settings pointing at the git host, or HTTP header names as topics.
- A framework fact citing `@SpringBootApplication` (bare, qualified or with arguments), `@EnableAutoConfiguration` or `@QuarkusMain` is Spring Boot or Quarkus, never a house framework to learn; one an earlier read named after the annotation is kept.
- `verify --write` says a shared link is "verified by another call; this one by path" instead of pairing verified with a path guess.

## 2.0.0-beta.11 (2026-11-02)

### Fixed before release: from the review

- A difference on a field the other side lacks is unconfirmed, not a mismatch, when that side has a converter nobody traced that matched nothing; the finding cites its line. A listener's findings against an outside publisher's spec now mark its link on the Architecture canvas and in Why is this linked?.
- A repo's specs are read at the commit its facts were read at, from the clone's local git (an uncommitted edit or untracked file is no spec), and the check is worked out once per metarepo and shared by Map › Mismatches, the canvas, Trust and Outside systems until a file it reads changes; a 40-repo estate checks in a fraction of a second.
- A system in a profile takes `topics:`, the topics and queues it owns, apart from its `aliases`: a publish or listener no repo serves the other side of lands on the system whose `topics` name it, and a dotted topic never reads as a host or a vendor's name. Topics need `schemaVersion: 2` in `profile.yaml` (the number only; version 1 profiles read as before). `atticus questions answer <id> --target` on a topics question writes the topic there.
- An answer opens again only once the repo it names is read whole and fresh (its read plan done, no `--gaps` pass waiting, `atticus estate fresh` saying fresh), never when you gave the same answer again after it reopened, and never for an answer given as a rule. A `--target` answer that is refused no longer writes the profile, and a repo id with a dot shows as read. The same answer in several repos now offers only the `--match` rule for their repo type, not a note. The questions upgrade step says it writes version 4.
- **`call-security` judges the call that reaches the system.** A gateway's forwarding call must show the TLS and auth itself; a caller's mTLS to the gateway no longer counts. A call whose code cites `none` is a finding a declared word doesn't cover. `to: vendor` covers only systems the profile gives boundary `vendor`; the new `to: outside` takes other teams' systems too. The verifier accepts a call's `tls: mtls` only on a line (or a `with` line) that writes a client certificate, key store or SSL bundle, and `tls` only where https, SSL or TLS is written (`security-line-kind`).
- **Notes are one line, keyed by their words, and handed over as quoted data.** Each note gets a short key; a term fact cites the key or at least four of the note's words, rule drafts are named by it, and reordering notes changes nothing. A term whose note was changed or removed is flagged at verify (`term-note-stale`) and read again. A gateway caller with no id of its own is now a note, not a finding; consumers listed by several gateways are no longer a possible link to each other; term access grants are `service-account`, and long grant ids no longer collide.
- A topic or queue the profile names as another team's or a vendor's is drawn on that system whichever way the estate's code uses it, and is marked "Declared in the profile" on its box, its links and in Why is this linked?, with the profile's line.
- Full screen keeps Tab and Shift+Tab inside the picture and gives focus back to the Full screen button on leaving; Esc in Find a box clears the search without leaving full screen.
- `atticus doctor --counts` counts a question asked by a step that isn't Atticus's own (a plugin's) as `other`, and counts a file it can't read among the problems without printing its message or path.

### Map the whole estate in one command (ADR 0161)

- `atticus map --all` plans every repo's read at your model's limit (`--max-tokens`, runtime.yaml's `maxInput` or the probe; it won't guess), in the onboarding queue's order, and skips what is read and fresh. Under Copilot it prints one line per part, puts it on the clipboard and waits for the facts, then verifies them and moves on (`s` skip, `r` retry, `q` stop; running it again resumes). Where runtime.yaml allows model mode it runs unattended, capped by `--max-parts` or `--budget-tokens`. It joins the estate once at the end and lists what is unfinished with the command for each. `--stale`, `--gaps`, `--repos` and `--dry-run` narrow it. In IntelliJ: Tools → Atticus → Map the Estate…; in VS Code: Map the estate.
- A Copilot chat that fails partway loses nothing: after 15 quiet minutes (`--idle`) Atticus says so, and `r` keeps the good facts and gives a new line for only what is left of the part (`atticus map <repo> --part n --gaps`). After three tries it suggests a smaller limit.

### A contract check on every link (ADR 0164)

- `atticus join contracts` and Map › Mismatches compare the payload on both sides of every REST call, gateway hop, topic and call to an outside system, from the fields your assistant read and the specs on file (a repo's own OpenAPI, `.proto`, Avro, JSON Schema or AsyncAPI, read at `verify --write` into `facts/<repo>.specs.yaml`; a vendor's or team's through intake, which now takes `.proto` and `.avsc`): missing and extra fields, required but not sent, types, error statuses and reused protobuf field numbers. Envelopes, mappers and gateway policies are followed as cited mappings; one nobody traced makes a difference unconfirmed, never a mismatch. A link with a finding is marked on the map and in Why is this linked?, the Trust tab counts them, and an outside system's page says what a new spec brought. Catalog schema 4 (a Schema can be `protobuf`; `atticus upgrade` migrates); local API 2.47.0. Repos read before need `atticus map --all --stale` for their DTO fields, mappings and message types.

### Answers that carry and suggestions that know the estate (ADR 0162)

- A call's target question suggests every repo of the manifest, read or not, and the outside systems, ranked by the call's config key, client, host and path against each repo's names and base paths, its entries, its description and your earlier answers, each saying why and whether the repo is read; "Another repo…" (`atticus questions answer <id> --target <id>`, and the app's Questions page) names any repo, system or team, adding a vendor or a team's system to the profile for review with `--add-system`. An answer naming a repo whose routes, once read, don't serve the call opens again with why (question schema 4, `reopened`); `/atticus-map` is given the rules that cover the repo; the same answer in several repos offers one `--match` rule for their repo type.
- A topic or queue another team or a vendor owns, listed under their system's `topics:` in the profile, links a publish or listener no estate repo pairs with to that system, marked declared; a loose topic is asked ("A topic owned by another team or vendor"), and the answer writes the topic under that system's `topics:` for review.

### Org notes: what your terms mean, and what must hold (ADR 0163)

- A repo type (and conventions.yaml) can hold `notes` in your own words; every read of a repo is handed its type's and the org-wide ones, and records your identifiers as `term` facts cited on their lines (role `unknown` when no note says, asked once per term and repo type). `atticus repo-type note` lists and adds them.
- With terms read, `verify --write` ties each repo to its own id, makes a gateway's listed consumers access grants on its routes (the critic names them when a route changes), checks who is listed against who calls, and shows a shared value as a possible link; a note that reads as a rule is drafted as a checklist rule by `atticus repo-type rules --save`, and once active is checked on the facts (a call's cited `tls` and `auth`, new on call facts). Facts file 3, repo type and conventions 2, local API 2.48.0; old files read as before.

### Diagrams you can see, topics as nodes, and counts safe to paste (F10)

- Every picture (the estate canvas, Design, Know, Explore, Home's map, a change's diagram) takes the page's width and the height left on it, with the details laid over it, and has one viewer: fit on open, zoom in, out, to actual size and to the picked box, wheel and pinch zoom, drag pan, Find a box, Focus (all but a box and its neighbours dimmed), a minimap on large pictures, keys, and Full screen (F, Esc to leave), the same in VS Code and IntelliJ. A flow's sequence has Full screen too. On the canvas each event topic and MQ queue is a box of its own (publishers → topic → listeners) with its owner, marked "No listener in the estate" or "No publisher in the estate".
- `atticus doctor --counts [--json]`: open questions by kind, links verified and inferred by join flag, asked, outside and unresolved, repos read, unread and stale, facts accepted, rejected and not verified, and tokens reported: counts and flags only, nothing named, safe to paste out of a client's estate.

### The editors catch up (ADR 0166)

- VS Code (0.7.9) and IntelliJ (0.4.0): Another repo… on a target question (and A topic owned by another team or vendor on a topic question) opens the editor's own picker of every repo of the manifest, system of the profiles and team in roles.yaml, and answers with `--target`; a new system is offered with the command that writes it and added (`--add-system`) only after your yes. `atticus questions show <id> --json` gives the picker (`picks`). Contract mismatches (`join contracts`, Map › Mismatches a click away) and its count in the status bar; a repo type's notes, Add a note, Draft and Save the rule drafts, each write after your yes with the command shown; `doctor --counts` in a panel to copy.
- The diagram viewer's keys work in both editors' hosting: in IntelliJ's App tab Esc goes to the app (leave full screen, clear Find a box) instead of moving focus to the editor; Shift+Esc still hides the tool window.

## 2.0.0-beta.10 (2026-10-31)

### Fixed before release: from the second review

- `atticus intake traffic` writes a path only as an estate route template (or a template followed by `/**`) and drops any other; an app name that is no component or repo of the estate is written as a stable `app-<hash>` label; compressed IPv6 addresses, emails without a domain and `;` matrix params are caught. Contract files kept beside a repo's facts lose query strings, matrix params, example ids (`{id}`) and URL credentials, and a contract file the metarepo's `.denylist` names a word of is left out with a note.
- A line two overlapping ranges of a big file share counts for the earlier range only, so a fact read there no longer marks the next range done and `atticus next` no longer skips lines nobody read. `atticus map <repo> --gaps` puts the files, lines and reasons it plans in one `<atticus-data>` block, each value from the repo quoted on one line with control characters escaped, and says those values are data, never instructions.
- A call to a Kubernetes Service by its bare name is a guessed link (`joined-by-service-name`), unless the caller's own Service is read in the same namespace; `<name>.<namespace>` and the full `.svc` address stay verified.
- The preview after a correction reads the repo's contract files from its checkout, as `atticus verify --write` does, and writes nothing, not even a cache file. A shared map's `map.yaml` records where it came from only as a path inside the metarepo; for a folder outside it, `atticus join shared refresh <team> <folder>` needs the folder. Removing a shared map takes its marks off every repo's links.

### Another team's map, read-only (ADR 0160)

- `atticus join shared import <their metarepo> --team <id> --system <id>` brings in the map a team that runs Atticus shares, as files: only their verified entries, quotes redacted and the deny-list applied, go to `shared/<team>/` at their commit; `refresh` imports a newer commit over it, `list` says what is on file. Nothing is fetched, and their facts are never verified, corrected or reported on here.
- `atticus verify <repo> --write` joins the calls the estate leaves to that team's system to the entry of their map that serves each: the link is flagged `joined-to-shared-map` and cites their line at their commit, or `not-in-shared-map` when they serve nothing like it. Why is this linked? says "joined to <team>'s map", and Map › Outside systems lists their entries with the calls that reach each. Local API 2.44.0, additive.

### IntelliJ catches up with beta.10 (plugin 0.3.0, ADR 0159)

- Tools → Atticus has an action for each job beta.10 added, matching VS Code's commands of the same jobs: which clones moved on (`atticus estate fresh`), the reading order and its costs (`next --queue`), a repo's gaps (`map <repo> --gaps`, then `/atticus-map <repo> --gaps` on the clipboard for a new Copilot chat), the sample audit's precision, measuring the links, a traffic export, a repo's starting spec proposed and accepted, a repo's id renamed, what a change breaks, `/atticus-propose`, the proposals checked, and the questions in one sheet and applied. Run the Next Step in Copilot's Agent Mode names the custom agent to pick.
- Waiting on you lists the questions in the order to ask them, each with its owner, with My Teams' Questions Only (`--mine`), a checked proposal to Accept as My Answer, and a rule field for target and hosts answers (`--match`). The Next tab shows each step's reason, near ids to rename, the seed to accept, and the reading order with each repo's estimated cost and what each read took.

### The VS Code extension and @atticus catch up with beta.10

- The Command Palette and the Repos, Changes and Questions menus run the new jobs (estate fresh, next --queue, map <repo> --gaps with /atticus-map <repo> --gaps opened in Copilot Chat, join measure, verify audit --report, change impact, spec seed and accept, questions sheet and --apply, questions proposed, /atticus-propose, intake traffic, repo rename), a report shown whole in a tab beside its app page. The Questions view lists questions in the order to ask them, each with its owner, filters to My teams' (`--mine`), offers Accept as my answer (`--accept-proposal`) and a rule for target and hosts answers (`--match`); Next shows the onboarding queue with reasons and costs, the re-read and accept-seed steps, and a near repo id to rename.
- `@atticus` gains /impact, /fresh, /queue, /gaps and /propose and routes plain requests ("what does this change break", "which repos are stale", "propose answers") to them; Copilot's agent mode gains `atticus-question-proposer` for /atticus-propose (`pnpm copilot-agents`). `map <repo> --gaps` is no longer run as an AI step by the editors.
- `atticus commands --json`, which the editors' Run a Command reads, no longer takes a summary's first word as a flag's value or a flag named in a summary's words as the command's own.

### A change's impact up the chain, an open seed's collisions, and a vendor's headers and errors (ADR 0156)

- `atticus change impact` and the Architecture tab walk up from each caller a change breaks, up to five hops up: each hop with the callers it calls through and its owner, a hop resting on a guessed link shown as may break and counted apart (`upstream`). Local API 2.43.0 (`chain`, `capped`, `upstream`; additive).
- The stories stage, `atticus spec seed` and `atticus next` warn when a change adds a flow that a repo's open seed adds too, and say to accept the seed (`atticus spec accept <repo>`), then run the change's stories stage again. The vendor spec reader reads response headers, and `atticus intake impact` lists the error responses added, removed or changed between two versions.

### A second pass on the gaps, overlapping line ranges, and the method note measured (ADR 0157)

- `atticus map <repo> --gaps` plans a second pass on what the last verify left (files no fact cites, skipped lines, reviewed globs that hold some, spec operations no entry covers, entries no flow follows) for a fresh session sized to the model, and writes its prompt to `facts/<repo>.gaps.md`; `/atticus-map <repo> --gaps` in a new chat reads it. The line ranges of a file bigger than a session now overlap by 20 lines or 2%, so nothing is cut at a boundary (a plan with ranges is re-planned at the next `--estimate`).
- The HTTP-method note now reads the method chain a cited line is in and a gateway forward's route: over the fixture estates it went from 7 false notes of 78 to none, pinned in a test; it stays a note. `atticus map --estimate`'s help names the house-package warning.

### Where a component is reached: regex paths, Services and modules

- An Ingress path written as a regular expression gives its plain prefix as the base path, citing the line; a base path that is itself a regex is rejected (`exposure-regex`).
- A Kubernetes Service's in-cluster address (`<name>.<namespace>.svc.cluster.local:<port>`) is an exposure: a call by that name, `<name>.<namespace>` or the bare name joins the component it selects, cited, instead of the host's first-label guess. `exposes` may name a module the manifest doesn't list as `<repo>/<module>`.

### Another team's system is outside the estate everywhere, and a link several calls share

- The target question, a change's footprint and risk, and the release plan, notes and estimate now treat a profile system no estate repo belongs to as outside, saying "another team's system". The critic checks a call to it against that team's published spec once the spec is in the catalog.
- An Interface several calls share takes every call's citations and the union of their flags, and is verified when any call's join is (ADR 0135 §5).

### Corrections without git, the audit answered in the app, and the outside systems (ADR 0149)

- After Correct this or This link is wrong, the app lists the commands to run, each with a copy button (`atticus verify <repo> --write`, then the branch, the exact files to add, the commit, the push and the pull request for your Git host), and what that write will change in the facts, the links and the catalog, worked out by the command's own checks without writing anything. The app still runs nothing.
- Map › Trust's Sample audit can be answered in the app: the same seed draws the same sample as `atticus verify audit`, and the answers go to the same `facts/<repo>.audit.yaml`. Map › Outside systems has a page per vendor or other team's system: who calls it, its spec's operations, the calls matching none, its questions, and how to add a spec. Local API 2.42.0, additive.

### Contract files and traffic as evidence for links (ADR 0151)

- `atticus verify <repo> --write` reads the repo's Pact files and the OpenAPI specs it keeps for the APIs it calls, flags each link they bear out `confirmed-by-contract` (citing the line), and lists the requests that name another provider, or that no link carries, in its output and the verify report. A guessed link stays a guess.
- `atticus intake traffic <file> --source apigee|catalog [--from --to]` keeps a gateway or API catalogue export only as counts per app, method, path template, proxy and base path in `evidence/traffic/`, for review in a pull request. Client ids, keys, IPs, user agents, query strings and tokens are dropped before anything is written, and a file that fails a check is refused. Links get `seen-in-traffic`, and the write lists traffic with no link and links nobody used.

### Answers by rule, in one sitting, and where they came from (ADR 0153)

- A target or hosts answer can carry a rule: `atticus questions answer <id> --option <n> --match "*.payments.forge.internal"` or `--match "/forge/limits/**"`, or the rule field on the Questions page. One answer then covers every call that matches, the calls found later too, in the repos its scope allows. A hosts rule goes into the manifest (the pattern under `hosts`, or the prefix as `basePath`) and retires the open hosts questions it covers.
- `atticus questions sheet` writes every open question into one YAML answer sheet, and `--apply <file>` answers the filled entries as you, skipping blanks and refusing any whose question changed.
- A change's own questions go to the people its `change.yaml` names under `owners`, for `--mine` and the Questions page. An answer accepted from the assistant's proposal records the proposal and the lines it cited. Open hosts questions asked before beta.10 gain the outside-system options on the next write. Question files are now schema version 3 (`atticus upgrade`, step `questions-v3`), and the local API is 2.41.0.

### Fixed before release: from the independent review

- A proposed answer's citation must name the question's repo or one a manifest lists, and that repo must have a checkout here. Only a change's own question cites the metarepo, and never Atticus's own folders (proposals, questions, facts, knowledge, judgments, runs); links are followed and must stay inside the checkout, and a refused citation never says what the file holds.
- A call a person sent outside the estate is no longer asked about, even when its config gives each environment a different repo's host, and it is listed with the outside calls.
- A link joined by a host that came from a platform-wide setting, from a setting found only by its folder name, or from a deploy file the manifest disagrees with is `inferred` and flagged `joined-by-platform-setting`, `joined-by-folder-name` or `joined-despite-conflict`; Why is this linked?, the critic's guessed-link check, a change's impact (may break), question retiring and `atticus join measure` treat them as guesses (C4 2.38.0).
- The sample audit, the prototype comparison and `atticus intake impact` join with the org pack's conventions, so they see the same links as the catalog.
- A facts file, corrections file or reviewed list written on Windows (`src\main\...`) is read with `/`, so it verifies and joins exactly as one written with `/`.

### Placeholders through valueFrom, and slips of form fixed everywhere

- The join follows a Deployment's variable set `valueFrom` a ConfigMap (`configMapKeyRef:<configmap>/<key>` in the config fact's `source`) to that ConfigMap's entry, in the same repo or the caller's, citing both lines; one from a Secret (`secretKeyRef:`) is followed only to a plain host, and a Secret's values are never facts. A value with two placeholders (`${SCHEME}://${HOST}/v1`) is resolved when each has one value.
- The reader's slips of form (a camelCase id, a flow with no cite) are now fixed in `atticus map`'s batches, across batches, and in the Map's facts API too, with the same notes as `atticus verify`.

### Upgrade everyone who shares the metarepo together

- Facts written by beta.10 can carry new deploy fields (`host`, `basePath`, `exposes`; ADR 0143), which beta.9 and older reject, and `roles.yaml` moves to version 2 with `atticus upgrade`. Upgrade every teammate who reads the same `facts/` folder at once.
- A key in the new `org-pack/conventions.yaml` replaces its default list whole: to add a folder to `notProductFolders`, list the defaults with it (docs/guide/admin.md, "Your house's conventions"), or tests come back into coverage.

### Sample audit (ADR 0148)

- `atticus verify audit <repo> [--size n] [--seed s]` draws a reproducible random sample of a repo's kept facts and its links (with how each was joined), shows each with its cited lines and asks right, wrong or unsure, or takes the answers from `--answers` or `--right`/`--wrong`/`--unsure`; the answers go to `facts/<repo>.audit.yaml`, a person's record for a pull request. A wrong one points to Correct this or This link is wrong.
- `atticus verify audit <repo> --report` (and `--report` alone for the estate) gives precision per kind of fact and for links, with the sample size and a 95% interval. Map › Trust shows it, and a fact answered right shows as `verified: sample` on the Map (C4 2.37.0).

### Stack helpers stay optional

- The framework models, the gateway walk, the built-in readers and the verifier's per-framework naming rules can each be switched off for a run with `ATTICUS_HELPERS_OFF` (a comma list, or `all`); docs/guide/frameworks.md says what the read does without each.
- A test verifies and joins the bank-shaped estate with every helper off: every fact is kept, and every link lands but the two that stop at their gateway proxy.

### House conventions in the org pack, and frameworks taught before the first map (ADR 0147)

- New optional `org-pack/conventions.yaml`: the API spec folders, gateway bundle folders, facing words, your own framework entries, the folders coverage and verify's skipped-lines note leave out, and your house's package prefixes. Each defaults to what Atticus used before.
- With `housePrefixes` set, `atticus map <repo> --estimate` and `atticus next` warn before the first read of house packages no taught framework covers, naming the `atticus frameworks learn` command to run first.
- `/atticus-map` now asks the reader to check verify's `skipped`, `reviewedHolds` and `specOpsUncovered`, record headers and enum values on fields and the HTTP status on error paths, and record the tokens a read used with `atticus map --spent` when its assistant shows them.

### A change's impact, and answers the assistant proposes (ADR 0144)

- `atticus change impact <change>` and the change's Architecture tab (**What this change breaks**) show, from its target flows, who each broken entry or table is called or used by, the fields that stop crossing and the gateway routes to update, each with its owner team. A link the map only guessed may break and is never counted as a break; the critic's `constitution.change-links-known` asks about it. API 2.36.0: `GET /api/changes/{changeId}/impact`.
- `/atticus-propose` has your assistant propose an answer to each open question from the code, citing its lines; `atticus questions proposed` checks every citation, only checked proposals are shown, and `atticus questions answer <id> --accept-proposal` (or Accept on the Questions page) makes it your answer. Question files are unchanged.

### A repo's starting spec, seeded from its verified flows (ADR 0146)

- `atticus spec seed <repo>` proposes a repo's starting `openspec/specs` as the change `openspec/changes/seed-<repo>/`: a capability per verified flow, one requirement per flow, a scenario for its main path, each branch and each error case, every line citing the code it was read from. Nothing is written into a code repo or into `openspec/specs` until a person runs `atticus spec accept <repo>` (`openspec validate --strict`, then `openspec archive`).
- The first change on a seeded flow then writes MODIFIED against the seed, keeping its scenarios, and OpenSpec's archive keeps the specs current from there.

### Lines the reader skipped

- `atticus verify` lists the lines of the files read that write a path, URL or topic no fact cites or names (plain text patterns, any language; comments, imports, tests, generated and vendored folders left out), the reviewed globs whose files hold such lines, and the operations a repo's OpenAPI spec declares that no entry covers. Notes for the reader's second pass, never rejections: under `skipped`, `reviewedHolds` and `specOpsUncovered` in `<repo>.verify.yaml` (capped at 100, with counts); `atticus verify <repo> --skipped` prints every one.

### Where a component is reached, read from its Routes (ADR 0143)

- A deploy fact can say where its component is reached: `host`, `basePath` and, for a deploy repo's Route, the component it `exposes` (facts schema version 2, every field optional, no upgrade step). The verifier checks each on its cited line (a templated host on its values file's line) and that the component is the estate's; the join takes hosts and base paths from these facts as well as from repos.manifest.yaml, and a conflict between them is reported by `atticus verify --write` and under `exposure` in the verify report, with both joined. So the manifest no longer has to list every service's host and base path by hand: reading the deploy repo is enough.
- The link estate's services are now placed by their Routes and its proxies by their BasePath, with only the gateway host left in its manifest: precision 0.909 and recall 0.769 as before (0.5 and 0.231 without the facts). `atticus next` reads a deploy or config repo right after the gateways, since its Routes and values place the others.

### The estate's freshness, and the onboarding queue with what each read cost (ADR 0141)

- `atticus estate fresh` lists, for every repo at once and most stale first, the facts' commit and the clone's, the files changed that the facts cite, the facts to read again and the command to run; local git only, nothing fetched or written. The Map's Trust tab shows the same under Freshness (`GET /api/trust/fresh`, C4 2.34.0).
- `atticus next` reads the repos in the order that lands links early: reads under way, then the repos the read repos call, then gateways, then the rest; `--queue` lists the order with each repo's estimated cost. A mapped repo is no longer offered again because an older spec header kept another template; one whose clone moved past its facts gets a re-read step. The read plan (schema 2, an older one is read as before) keeps `--estimate`'s estimate for every repo, the parts `atticus verify` saw read, and what your assistant reports with `atticus map <repo> --spent`, each said for what it is.

### A prototype compared further, and another team's spec as their system (ADR 0142)

- `atticus change attach --prototype` now also says where enum values, headers and error responses differ (from field types, header-named fields, flow error paths and spec enums, header parameters and 4xx/5xx responses; no facts change), and the attached comparison lists the calls nothing serves for the architecture stage, whose drafter is told to place each one. C4 2.35.0, additive.
- Another team's OpenAPI spec (a profile system with no estate repo, boundary `internal`) is read as a vendor's: `atticus intake impact` lands a service's own call on its operation, checks the field it sends and walks up its callers, and the prototype comparison names a call only that team publishes as theirs. A host alias counts as the system's host.

### A change warns on guessed links; questions come in the order a change needs them, each to its owner (ADR 0140)

- The critic's new constitution rule `constitution.change-links-known` says when a call the change plans rests on a link the join guessed (joined by path or by a host's label), on a link question still open, or on a repo whose read is stale or incomplete, with the question to answer or the command that reads it again.
- `atticus questions list`, `atticus next` and the Questions page list first what a change in flight rests on, then the answers that join the most calls, so a lone /ping comes last. Each question names the team that owns its repo (or says it has none), and `questions list --mine` / My teams' lists only yours, from `roles.yaml` version 2's `teams` (`atticus upgrade` runs `roles-v2`). GET /api/questions gains `about`, `me` and `mine=true` (C4 2.33.0).

### Config placeholders resolved through the config that sets them

- A call whose base URL is a placeholder (`${PAYMENTS_URL}`, Spring's `${PAYMENTS_URL:default}`, `env:PAYMENTS_URL`), or a publish or listener whose topic is one, is joined through the config facts that set it: in its own repo, or in a deploy or config repo of the estate where the setting is for its component (a folder named for it, a Deployment of that name in the file, or the fact's `target`), else one for every service (a platform chart). Every environment's host the manifest's hosts and base paths claim is accepted, and the link cites the placeholder's line and the value's; environments pointing at different repos are asked. No model; run `atticus verify --all --write` again.
- A config key is no longer read as a host (it was asked about as "Which repo answers on forge.notify.base-url?"), and the reading rules say how to write an environment variable a deployment sets. On the bank-shaped link estate, precision goes from 0.889 to 0.909 and recall from 0.615 to 0.769, with 1 call asked instead of 4.

### Measuring the links the join makes

- The estate join is measured against reference links on an invented estate shaped like a large bank's (a proxy per repo on one gateway host, services under one ingress host, a house starter, a deploy repo): reading recall, link precision and recall, and the calls asked, guessed and taken by outside systems, pinned in CI.
- `atticus join measure <reference>` scores your own map against the entries and calls you list by hand for a few repos you know, each call with the repo it should reach (docs/guide/measure.md). It reads files only and writes nothing.

### Why a link is there, and every count opens its list (ADR 0135)

- On the estate canvas, each link's details open **Why is this linked?**: every call on it, how it was joined (a person's answer, the target the call names, the host, a guess by path), verified or inferred, and its cited line at both ends. **This link is wrong** writes a person's correction (another repo, an outside system, or not a call), says what `atticus verify <repo> --write` will change and what moved afterwards; a person's target now outranks every join rule. Every Interface the join writes is flagged how it was joined (C4 2.32.0: `joins` on graph edges).
- On Map › Trust every count on a repo (kept, redacted, rejected, not read, excluded, questions open, not checked) opens its list with file and line and what closes each (`lists` in GET /api/trust). The canvas's "thing missing" count says beside each gap what closes it.

### Verify --write says what it did to the links

- `atticus verify <repo> --write` counts the repo's calls joined (of them inferred), asked, outside the estate and not joined, and lists each Interface added, removed or changed in confidence, with how its callee was found; all of it under `links` in `<repo>.verify.yaml` for the pull request.

### The reader's slips of form are fixed, not refused

- A fact id that isn't a lowercase slug is slugged and every reference to it rewritten (a collision is reported and stays refused); a flow with no cite takes its entry's line; both listed under `slips` in the verify report. Each fixed fact is still checked on its cited line.
- A reviewed glob that matches no file is dropped with a note (`reviewedDropped`) instead of failing the run.

### The HTTP method is looked for on its line

- The method an entry or call claims is looked for on its cited line, the next line or a `cite.with` line, in any language; a method found on none is a warning, counted in the verify report (`methodUnseenCount`, `methodUnseen`), never a rejection.

### Repo ids that don't drift

- `atticus repo add` warns when an entry looks like one already listed (same clone URL, or an id equal once case, `_`/`-` and a version suffix are set aside), and `atticus next` names a near id that has facts when a repo has none.
- `atticus repo rename <old> <new>` moves a repo's id with its facts and their companions, scan settings, questions and catalog records, in one change you review; Atticus commits and pushes nothing.

### Questions asked once, and retired when the code answers them

- A call's "Which repo or system answers…" question is asked once for every call of a repo that goes the same way (the target it names, or its host or base, and its path prefix); one answer names the target of all of them, and a call added later reads it too. "Not all to one place" asks each on its own. Answered per-call questions keep working.
- An open target or ambiguous question retires itself once the code joins its calls without an answer (the call names its target, or the manifest's hosts and base paths decide it), saying how. A host question can be answered "An outside system (new)", another team's or a vendor's, which writes the system into the profile with the host as its alias.

### Fixed: a read part fits the model it is read with

- `atticus map --estimate` planned every part of a big repo at 150K tokens of source, whatever the model: `--max-tokens 136000` (Copilot's Claude Sonnet) still gave 150K parts, which overflowed the context, so the assistant cut a part short or summarised it mid-read and missed what was in it. A part's source is now 45% of the model's limit (about 61K for 136K, 450K for a 1M-token model), leaving room for the reading rules, the facts written and the verify loop; with no limit given it stays 150K. Line ranges of a file too big for a session are sized the same. Run `atticus map <repo> --estimate --max-tokens <limit>` again: the plan is redone at the new size.

### Fixed: a guessed link says it is a guess

- A link the join found without anyone naming the callee was shown as verified when the facts at both ends were: a call with no target joined to the one entry in the estate that serves its path, or a call on a host whose first label happened to be a service's name (`forge-limits.apps.example.internal`). Both are guesses, and a wrong one drew a line between two services that don't talk. Such a link is now `inferred`, with a flag saying how it was found (`joined-by-path` or `joined-by-label`), so it shows as unconfirmed on the map and in the drill-down. A link a call named, a person answered or the manifest's hosts and base paths decided is unchanged. No repo needs reading again: run `atticus verify --all --write`.

### Fixed: "None of these" and another team's system now hold

- Answering a "Which repo or system answers …?" question with **None of these** was dropped, so the next `atticus verify --write` could still join the call by path to whichever entry happened to serve it. The answer now binds: the call is left out of the join and not asked again.
- Only systems with `boundary: vendor` counted as outside the estate, so a call to another internal team's system (declared in the profile with `boundary: internal` and no repo of yours) was guessed onto one of your own services. Every system the profile declares that no repo in `repos.manifest.yaml` belongs to is now outside, as a vendor is: a call naming it, or on a host named for it, goes to that system. Declare other teams' systems in the profile with `boundary: internal` (the workaround of marking them `vendor` still works). Run `atticus verify --all --write`.

## 2.0.0-beta.9 (2026-10-29)

### A repo's `basePath` on a shared host (`repos.manifest.yaml` version 3)

- A repo, or a module, can give the path its routes sit under on its hosts: `basePath: /forge/limits` beside `hosts:`. When several repos share a host (services behind one OpenShift or GCP ingress, each under a common prefix and its module name), a call whose path starts with a repo's base path goes to that repo, so even a path every service serves (`/ping`, `/retrieve`) is joined rather than asked. Leave it out and nothing changes. The manifest moves to version 3; older manifests are read as they are, and `atticus upgrade` rewrites the number (ADR 0134). A `{placeholder}` in a base path stands for any one segment, and a repo whose base path rules a call out is never joined to it. No repo needs reading again: run `atticus verify --all --write`. Files this version writes are at version 3, which beta.8 and older refuse to read, so upgrade everyone who shares the metarepo together.

### A host question retires itself once the manifest lists the host

- "Which repo answers on <host>?" stayed open after a person wrote the host into `repos.manifest.yaml` by hand, and answering it puts the host on one repo only, which is wrong for a gateway or platform host many repos share. The question now retires itself, saying which repos list the host, the next time `atticus scan` or `atticus verify --write` runs, and the line that asks it says to list a shared host on each repo instead of picking one.

### Fixed: one gateway host shared by many proxy repos is joined by path

- When several repos list the same host in `repos.manifest.yaml` (an Apigee gateway host such as `api-gw.example.internal`, with each proxy in a repo of its own), a call on that host was joined only against the first of them, and a call to any other proxy's base path was missed. The join now looks at every repo that lists the host and picks the one whose route serves the call's path; two routes that both serve it are asked, as before. List the gateway host under `hosts:` on every proxy repo, then run `atticus verify --all --write` again.

## 2.0.0-beta.8 (2026-10-28)

### No file is too big to read: the read plan splits it into line ranges

- A file bigger than one session (a 200K-token OpenAPI spec, a generated schema) was a part of its own that no session could hold, and an agent-mode reader gave up on it. `atticus map --estimate` now plans such a file, whatever its size, in line ranges (`oas/spec.yaml#L1-L4000`, `#L4001-L8000`, ...), each fitting a session. A range is done when a kept fact cites a line in it, or when `<repo>.reviewed.jsonl` names the range itself (verify checks the file has those lines). `/atticus-map` tells the reader to open only that range's lines. A part's progress shows in `--estimate` and `atticus next` as before.

## 2.0.0-beta.7 (2026-10-28)

### Fixed: an API spec in an `oas/` folder is read, not proposed as data

- An OpenAPI or Swagger spec was known only by a file name starting with `openapi`, `swagger` or `asyncapi`. One in a folder named for it (`oas/`, `openapi/`, `swagger/`, `api-specs/`), named `*.oas.yaml`, or with the word elsewhere in its name was taken for data: `atticus map --estimate` proposed excluding it as "data, not code", and a reader would leave it unread. As an Apigee proxy's repo keeps its spec, it is now read like any API spec (split into line ranges when it is too big for one prompt), whatever its size.

## 2.0.0-beta.6 (2026-10-28)

### Fixed: a flow whose call joined a long-named interface is rendered

- `atticus verify --write` could stop with "A merged record isn't valid C1 (a bug): schemaRefs.0 / id: Too big". When a call joined the endpoint it reaches, its request and response schemas were renamed after the joined interface, and a long service name and path took the id past 120 characters. Such an id is now shortened to a stable one (its start and a hash), the same wherever it is renamed, so the flow renders. Nothing in the facts needs to change: run `verify --write` again.

### `/atticus-map` says a flow fact needs its own `cite`

- Readers (Claude Sonnet among them) wrote flow facts with an `entry` and cited steps but no `cite` of their own, and the line failed the facts schema. The reading rules now say a flow's `cite` is the line that starts it, as its entry fact cites it.

### Fixed: `frameworks learn` says why it can't tell a framework's package

- Learning from a folder whose Java sources have no package, or packages with nothing in common, printed a raw schema error ("packages: Too small"). It now says which, names the package roots it found, and asks for `--package <base package>`. Generated code in a module's `target/` or `build/` folder is left out, so OpenAPI or SOAP stubs no longer hide the framework's own package.

## 2.0.0-beta.5 (2026-10-28)

### `/atticus-map` reads with the frameworks you've taught

- A framework you taught Atticus (`frameworks learn`, `frameworks kit`, or a model written by hand in `org-pack/frameworks/`) is now handed to the reader of every repo whose build file names one of its artifacts. `atticus map --estimate` lists it (and `--json` gives `taughtFrameworks`), and each batch's instructions say what its wrappers, routes, annotations, service ids and constants stand for, so a call through a house HTTP client is read as a `call` fact rather than missed. Before, verify knew the framework but the reader didn't (ADR 0124 addendum A-taught).

### `atticus verify --all`

- Verifies every repo with a facts file in `.atticus/facts/`, one after another, and prints a line for each and a total. With `--write` it projects each that passes. The side files (`.questions`, `.reviewed`, `.rejected`, `.corrections`) are skipped.

### `repo add` on a folder you've cloned

- `atticus repo add <folder>` on a git clone now records its `origin` URL and checked-out branch in `repos.manifest.yaml` (as `defaultBranch`), and says the `workspace sync --from` command to copy it in.
- `workspace sync --from` matches a clone to a repo by its folder name in any case, when the URLs don't match.

### Fixed: frameworks you've taught are recognised by `atticus verify`

- `atticus verify` loaded the org pack from the metarepo's root instead of `org-pack/`, failed quietly, and so knew none of your framework files: every house starter, even one you had learned, was offered as "nothing here can read yet". It reads `org-pack/frameworks/` now (or `.atticus/org-pack/` in an adopted repo).
- A framework fact citing `@SpringBootApplication` is read as Spring Boot, not offered to learn.

### `/atticus-map` says `authCite` takes no quote

- Readers (Claude Sonnet among them) gave an entry's `authCite` a `quote`, as `cite` has, and the line failed the facts schema and went back to be rewritten. The reading rules now say `authCite` is `{file, line, value}` only.

## 2.0.0-beta.4 (2026-10-28)

### Fixed: `frameworks learn` leaves out Java's `serialVersionUID`

- Learning a framework from its JAR kept every exception's and bean's `serialVersionUID` as a constant, and called the ones in a class named like a service "service ids" to give base URLs for. It is Java's serialization version, never a value of the framework's: it is left out.

### Fixed: a house starter versioned `${project.parent.version}` is found

- `atticus frameworks discover` read a starter declared at `${project.parent.version}` (or `${parent.version}`, `${project.version}`) as that literal text, so it never found the JAR in the Maven cache and said it wasn't on the machine. Those versions now come from the pom's `<parent>` and `<version>`.

### Fixed: an Apigee proxy or shared flow is never offered as a framework to learn

- Reading a gateway repo, an assistant could write a framework fact for the proxy (`<APIProxy>`) or each shared flow (`<SharedFlowBundle>`), and `atticus verify` then offered `atticus frameworks learn` for each, whatever the repo's type. They are the gateway's routes, which map already reads: the verifier offers nothing for them, and `/atticus-map`'s framework rule says they are not framework facts.

## 2.0.0-beta.3 (2026-10-27)

### Base clones copied from the clones you already have

- **`atticus workspace sync --from <folder>`** copies each repo's base clone from your own clone of it in that folder, matched by its remote URL (SSH and HTTPS alike) or its folder name: no network, the files hard-linked on the same drive, your clones only read. It takes the branch as your clone last fetched it (`origin/<branch>`), never your unpushed commits or edits, and points `origin` at the server, so the next sync fetches from there. A repo with no clone in the folder is cloned from the server as before.

## 2.0.0-beta.2 (2026-10-27)

### Fixed: a scan no longer calls every repo a prototype

- `atticus scan` said "a prototype, so not scanned into the catalog" for every repo, though it scanned them all. It now says it only of a repo the manifest types `prototype`.

### A change's knowledge has its own page

- **Change › Knowledge**, a tab beside Feature design, shows every page attached to the change, ranked, with its source and authority, the near-misses, the tasks that use each page and the pages that disagree; Confirm and Exclude work there. It was only at the foot of the Feature page, which now links to it. Ctrl+K finds it, and the editors' app panels reach it like the other tabs.

### A first install on Windows 11: what it found, fixed

- **A new metarepo is current.** `atticus sandbox create` wrote the Forge org pack at old schema versions, so its first `atticus doctor` asked for five upgrade steps and 66 slash commands. The sandbox's metarepo, and one `atticus init` makes (from the command line or anything that calls it), is brought to the current version of every schema with its slash commands written, so its doctor has nothing to upgrade. A test makes each and runs `upgrade --check` and doctor, so a future schema bump that forgets them fails.
- **`atticus doctor` outside a metarepo says so, once,** with the guidance `atticus next` gives, and checks only the machine (Node, Git, OpenSpec, the model). It no longer says "Org pack: valid" or asks to run `atticus upgrade` in a folder that isn't one.
- **The sandbox's next command is printed.** `atticus sandbox create <name>` ends with the `cd` line to run; doctor and `atticus next` in the sandbox's outer folder say the metarepo is in `.\metarepo`.
- **The app's session token never reaches an editor's log.** `atticus ui` no longer prints it when an editor starts it (`--embed`, `--parent`): the VS Code extension gives it the token in its environment (`ATTICUS_UI_TOKEN`), and IntelliJ's page has it. `--show-token` in your own terminal prints it as before. The extension 0.7.0 is needed with this command line.
- **The extension's and IntelliJ's output say the time on each line,** and the extension's machine check no longer warns about port 4321, which only `atticus ui` in a terminal uses.
- **The Set up page's first load doesn't wait for the machine checks** (3 s on Windows): `atticus ui` starts them as it starts.
- **The published manifests name no private package.** `@maycombhq/atticus` and the SDK leave out the devDependencies they are built from (a prepack step), and the release's pack check refuses a packed manifest with a `workspace:` spec or a dependency, devDependencies included, on a package that isn't published.

### The Atticus site

- **The guides as a site, at maycombhq.com/atticus.** `website/` builds them with VitePress, with a landing page, a short "What Atticus is" page and local search. `pnpm docs:site` serves it while you edit; `pnpm docs:site:build` builds it and runs the deny-list over every built file. `.github/workflows/pages.yml` deploys it to GitHub Pages on each push to main.
- **One source.** The pages are `docs/guide/*.md`, the command reference and the README, copied in on every build with their links rewritten: a link to another guide stays on the site, and a link to an ADR or the spec goes to GitHub. A change to a guide changes the site with no second edit.
- **`node scripts/check-denylist.mjs <folder>`** checks every file under a folder, tracked or not, such as a build's output.
- **Guide fixes:** the troubleshooting table no longer repeats its first 35 rows, and the setup checklist's step 7 is back in the list.

### Start a change from a working prototype (ADR 0133)

- **A prototype is a repo of type `prototype`.** Atticus reads it and verifies each claim on its line, as it does for any repo. It is kept out of the production catalog: it gives no Endpoints or Interfaces, none of its mocked calls becomes a gap or a question, and no other repo's call joins it. `atticus verify <repo> --write` and a scan say so.
- **Its mocks are the backend it expects.** A new reading rule covers MSW handlers, WireMock mappings, json-server, Postman mock collections and `@Profile("mock")` controllers. It reads each mocked operation, with the fields it accepts and returns, and gives each BFF route the auth and scopes from the prototype's sign-on configuration.
- **Each call placed in one of four groups.** `atticus change attach <change> --prototype <repo>` places every call the UI makes to its BFF, and every call the BFF makes to a mock, as:
  - **exists:** the estate serves it, through the gateways;
  - **changes:** a field is missing or retyped, required-ness differs, or a scope or the auth differs;
  - **new:** nothing serves it;
  - **only a vendor:** it needs a route through the external gateway.

  Each call cites the prototype's line and the estate's or the vendor's. No model is called.

- **It reaches the change.** The comparison is attached for the feature and architecture stages to read, cited by line. The change's footprint gains the repos, gateways, Endpoints, vendor operations and systems. `--json` gives the comparison as data. The app's change page shows the four groups (`GET /api/changes/{id}/prototypes`, C4 2.30.0).
- **New in the guide:** [Starting from a prototype](docs/guide/prototypes.md).

### A task's knowledge is kept by meaning as well as by words (ADR 0132 addendum)

- **One common word no longer attaches a page.** With the meaning model installed, a page reaches a task when it shares two of the words the task changes, or one of the task's names (`createdAt`, `POST`); a page whose only link is one common word ("payment", "status") must also be close in meaning to the task's step. Precision across 12 wikis rises from 0.46 to 0.58 on the planted pages and from 0.31 to 0.51 with 4,800 filler pages, recall unchanged at 0.96 (`docs/evidence/many-wikis.md`).
- **Each page says why**: the words it shares, or "close in meaning to the task's step (…, similarity 0.71)"; the list is ranked by words and meaning together. The closeness is read from the vectors the index already has, so `atticus task context` stays about 1.4 s a task at 4,800 pages.
- **Without the model** (`--omit=optional`), nothing changes, and the task context says "Knowledge matched by words only: the meaning model isn't installed".
- **Not yet:** a how-to that says only "SSO" for a task that says "single sign-on" is still missed; the bundled model doesn't link the two. Confirm it for the change.

### A vendor's new API version, and what in your estate it reaches (ADR 0130)

- **Two versions of a vendor's spec, compared.** Add each version of a partner spec beside the last in `inputs/` (`atticus intake add`), never over it. `atticus intake impact <spec>` lists what changed: operations added, removed or moved, auth and scopes, and request and response fields added, removed, renamed, made required or optional, retyped, or given new enum values. Each change is marked breaking or additive by fixed rules, said in one sentence, and cited in both versions. No model is called.
- **Followed through your gateway.** For each change, Atticus finds the forwarding calls in your external gateway that reach that vendor operation, the service calls that go through each route, and their callers further up the chain, each cited to a file and line. When the change is to a field, it says whether a call sends that field. A forwarding call to an operation the vendor doesn't publish is listed as a gap, and one that two operations fit is listed as a question. `--json` gives the same data; the command exits 1 when a breaking change reaches the estate.
- **A change that starts with its footprint.** `atticus change new "<title>" --type <type> --impact <spec>` fills the change's systems, repos, Interfaces, flows and vendor Endpoints from the impact, and attaches the impact for every stage to read.
- **The vendor's operations in the catalog.** A partner spec's current version now gives each operation a request and a response Schema: each field with its type and whether it is required, and each citing its line in the spec. This is ADR 0125's OpenAPI rule, now wired. No schema version change.
- **New in the guide:** [A vendor's API changes](docs/guide/vendor-specs.md).

### Who calls a gateway route, even when Atticus can't read the caller (ADR 0124 addendum A-callers)

- **A route says who it lets in.** Reading an Apigee gateway now records, on each route, the consumers its own files name: the API product it belongs to, the developer apps subscribed to it, a client id or certificate subject a check admits. Each is cited on the line that names it and checked like any other claim, so an invented one never reaches the catalog. In the catalog they are AccessGrants on the route, and `map.md` prints `callers …` on each route's line, or `callers unknown`.
- **Declare a caller you'll never scan.** Another team's product calls your internal proxy and its repo isn't yours to read? Add it under `systems:` in the org pack's profile with its owner and `calls: [{ as: app, name: <the app the proxy names> }]` (or the route itself). It links to the cited app when the proxy names it, stays marked as your word everywhere, and never replaces what the code says. The join reads the chain from it: `Harbor Desk (declared; app harbor-desk-portal) → edge-gateway forge-refunds-v1 (oauth2; scopes refunds.write; internal) → refunds-api POST /refunds → partner-gateway northwind-refunds-v1 (…; external) → northwind-pay-api POST /v2/refunds`.
- **The critic names them.** A change to what a route carries lists every caller the route names or a person declared as affected, beside the catalog's other consumers; and a route nobody names, declares or calls asks who calls it, under the "complete" question, instead of passing in silence.
- **Fixed on the way:** writing a gateway's facts after its caller's no longer drops the route the call goes through, and a person's asserted call no longer turns `verified` when the service's code is found.
- **New in the guide:** "Apigee: naming your consumers" and "Declaring a caller you can't scan" in [Teaching Atticus your house frameworks](docs/guide/frameworks.md).
