### Stories read the notes you attach to a change, and code wins (ADR 0113 addendum)

Start a change from your own analysis: `atticus change attach <change> notes/*.md`, and the stories stage now reads them too, as the feature and architecture stages already did.

- **What your notes say reaches the stories.** Acceptance criteria, edge cases, field rules, error messages and non-functional needs in an attached note become criteria on the task they belong to. Each says where it came from under it — `From inputs/<file>:<line>, quoting: "<your words>"` — and Atticus checks the words are on that line; the starter review's new code check, `review.note-cites-on-line`, checks it again at the plan gate.
- **Code wins, out loud.** Where a note says something the code contradicts — an entry's path, a field, the order of the steps — the story follows the code, lists the difference in its Context, and you are asked a question naming the note's line and the code's. A note that names an entry no code serves is asked about even when the drafter missed it.
- **Honest about the budget.** Notes take at most 15% of the stories prompt. A long note keeps its headings and the parts most related to the stories, and the stage says which lines it left out; no file is dropped. A note that hides instructions for an AI is held out and named. With nothing attached, the prompt is exactly what it was.

### A dozen wikis: which counts for more, where each applies, read from exports (ADR 0132)

- **`sources.yaml` version 3.** A source says how much its word counts (`authority`: standard, platform, framework, team or reference), with page overrides (`byPage`, as `include` and `exclude` match: an EA space's drafts and meeting notes are only reference) and how old a standard may get (`reviewMonths`). A page that is deprecated, superseded or archived (its labels, status or a banner) ranks below every current page. `atticus upgrade` moves version 1 and 2 files, changing only the number.
- **Authority decides on one topic only.** A task's list stays ordered by how well each page matches it; among pages on the same topic the current, verified, more authoritative page leads. A draft or a drifted copy of a standard groups under it.
- **Where a source applies** (`appliesTo`: systems, components, platforms or clouds, repo types, frameworks, new components). A GCP platform page reaches a task on GCP, or one that creates a new component (the target flow now says where a new component runs), never one that doesn't. Each attached page says the scope it matched.
- **Pages that disagree** name the more authoritative page as suggested, or a team's approved exception within its scope, or the one still current; a stale standard is said to be possibly out of date. A person still decides.
- **Standards become checks you accept**: `atticus standards propose`, then `atticus standards accept`. Nothing is enforced until the pull request merges.
- **Exports and restricted pages.** A Confluence space exported as HTML and a SharePoint site saved with its library's `.csv` are sources (`confluence-export`, `sharepoint-export`). A page under view restrictions is skipped, path and reason only, unless the source says `readRestricted`.
- **At scale.** A sync copies only changed pages; a page mirrored in two wikis is read once; `atticus sources estimate` gives pages, tokens and model calls before a scan, and `maxPages` caps a source. On 12 sources and 4,800 pages the index builds in about 10 s (it took over a minute: fixed).

### A fresh metarepo goes from `init` to stories with no dead end (ADR 0006, 0021, 0115 and 0124 addenda)

A trial on a real repo, run as a developer would, stopped eight times on things that were nobody's bug and everybody's lost hour. All eight are fixed, each with a test.

- **`atticus init` writes your assistant's files.** Its last line said to run `/atticus-map <repo>`, a slash command a new metarepo didn't have: the files that carry it came from `atticus assistant-files`, which you only meet under `atticus legacy`. `init` writes them now — the slash commands, Copilot's prompts and the instructions — and says how many, and that `atticus assistant-files` writes them again when the org pack changes.
- **A read in thirteen parts keeps its own list.** `atticus map <repo> --estimate` planned a big repo's read in parts and nothing recorded them, so you kept the list by hand across thirteen sessions. The plan is a file beside the facts now (`facts/<repo>.read-plan.yaml`), and which part is next is read back from what your assistant produced: a part is read when every folder it covers has a fact citing a file under it, or a line of `<repo>.reviewed.jsonl` saying the file holds nothing. `--estimate` prints each part's state and names the next; `atticus next` says "part 4 of 13 is next, 3 are read". It survives a lost session, a sub-agent and someone else carrying on, and reading a part again never un-does it.
- **An owner question says where its answer goes.** The define gate asked "Unknown: … System owner for …" because the profile a new metarepo ships lists no systems, and nothing said where an owner is written. The finding names the file and the field: add the system under `systems:` in `org-pack/profiles/<platform>/profile.yaml` with its `owner`, in a pull request — and it says plainly that answering the question doesn't close the gap on its own, since a gap closes when the fact is in the catalog.
- **No change is blocked on a document nobody writes.** `constitution.data-doc-present` demanded `data.md`, which no org-flow template ships and no stage writes unless a target flow changes the schema. It now reads the stage's own artifact: on the stage path it asks only when the flows change the schema, and then its fix names `atticus change architecture`, which writes the file. A schema change still blocks.
- **A bad answer is named by whichever command reads it.** An unquoted colon in a lens's answer made `--handoff --resume` hand the same prompt back with no hint anything was wrong; only `atticus handoff done` named the error, two rounds later. `--handoff` prints what the step said before it offers a prompt again (`--json` carries it as `errors`), and the message names the line, the column and the rewrite that fixes it: a value holding a `:`, a `#` or `{ } [ ]` goes in double quotes.
- **Between rounds you copy nothing.** After a finding, the next round's prompt points at a result file that already holds your assistant's last answer, to be edited in place, and the stage says which file it copied where. Before this, `--resume` failed with "architecture-answer.yaml was not written" until you copied the file across by hand.
- **A call out of the estate is no longer a false gap.** Every change that called a model provider was told `POST /chat/completions` and `POST /messages` were names nothing had read — while the catalog held both as calls. A vendor's endpoint has no record of its own, because nobody reads a vendor's code, so the hop's own method and path are now a name the catalog knows, and the call's record goes into the step's context. A call the catalog doesn't hold is still a gap.
- **OpenSpec is runnable.** It ships inside the installed package, not on your PATH, so `atticus openspec <args...>` runs it in the metarepo with its own output and exit code — and fills in `--change` for `status`, `instructions` and `show` when exactly one change is open. Every message that named a bare `openspec` command names `atticus openspec` now.

### `atticus verify --write` on a big repo: nothing of your assistant's work is lost, and it never dies in silence (ADR 0124 addendum A-uncited, A-room)

- **Your facts file keeps every claim your assistant wrote.** `--write` used to strip each entry's `auth` from `facts/<repo>.jsonl` when no line was cited for it — 221 of them on one real repo — and the warning about them went with the claims, so the second run said nothing and a person who re-verified was told their estate's auth was fully recorded. Now the claim stays in the file. The catalog is where it is left out: an `auth` reaches an `Endpoint` or an `Interface` only with an `authCite` (the file and line that sets it and what it writes there), or when you wrote the fact yourself. So the map still never says an auth nothing shows, and `atticus verify` says "N entries' auth rests on no cited line" on **every** run, `--write` included, until a line is cited — with what to do about it, and what is kept where.
- **A quote left off is now counted.** Where the cited line can't be checked against a quote — a minified bundle, a notebook, a binary, a line over 1,000 characters — the quote is still left off the kept fact, and `atticus verify` and `<repo>.verify.yaml` now say how many. The claim itself is checked on the line as always. Redacting a secret in a quote is unchanged: it goes, and it stays gone.
- **A read too big for the machine says so instead of vanishing.** On an 8 GB machine with little free, `verify --write` on a 4,900-file repo was killed by the kernel with exit 137 and not one byte of output, which looks like an Atticus crash and isn't one. The command now watches its own memory and stops with words: "this repo's facts need more memory than Node was given … run it again with `ATTICUS_MAX_OLD_SPACE=…`, or in parts, a repo at a time. Closing an IDE frees the memory this needs." `ATTICUS_MAX_OLD_SPACE` (megabytes) is honoured by starting Node again with that heap, since a running Node can't raise its own.
- **And it needs much less.** The verifier held every file of the repo's lines for the whole run, and read the facts file three times over before checking a fact. It now keeps a window of 64 files, frees a file it only had to classify, and reads the facts a line at a time. On a synthetic 4,900-file Forge repo with 2,300 facts: peak heap **265 MB → 56 MB**, peak resident **353 MB → 149 MB**, same facts kept.
- **New in the guide:** what `--write` keeps and what the catalog leaves out, and the memory note, in [Installing Atticus](docs/guide/install.md); three rows in [Troubleshooting](docs/guide/troubleshooting.md).

### Cutting Atticus 1: the inventory first (ADR 0129)

- **The inventory of what Atticus 1 still is, and what in Atticus 2 does its job.** The product owner approved cutting Atticus 1 on condition that nothing breaks and the app loses no depth. ADR 0129 lists every Atticus 1 package, command, screen, reader and contract surface against its Atticus 2 replacement, or records that nothing replaces it. The answer is plainer than the review's framing: **of seventeen Atticus 1 packages, none can go today.** The built-in readers are Atticus 2's own cross-check, and release, initiatives, intake, glossary, features, stubs, site, executors, ingestors, reconcile, adapters, CI and diagrams have no successor. They are listed as kept, with why. Nothing was deleted on the strength of a tidy-up.
- **API mappings works on a change taken through the stages.** The page read `mappings.md` and nothing else, so on an Atticus 2 change — which never has that file — it said "no hops in mappings" and stopped there. It now reads the architecture stage's target flows instead: each step that moves data is a hop, each field it sends, returns or reads is a row, shown against the name it is stored or sent as and how it is worked out, and every hop says which file it came from. A change that has a `mappings.md` is unchanged.
- **Nothing else changed for you.** 52 pages, 223 commands, 7 VS Code views and 9 IntelliJ tabs, before and after. Every page still opens on a real estate, every old address still works, More pages and Ctrl+K still reach everything, and the reading and placement bench scores are untouched.
- **A surface can no longer go missing by accident.** `pnpm check` now runs a surface census that counts and names the commands, the pages (and that each one has a screen behind it rather than a "being built" note), VS Code's views and palette commands, and IntelliJ's tabs. Retiring one is a deliberate line in that file, naming the ADR that decided it and what does the job instead — so a page or a command leaving shows up in the diff.
- **Two things the cut is still owed**, each written down with the files it touches: the four reader-path AI steps (`scan survey`, `scan verify`, `scan review`, `spec draft`, all of which `atticus map` and `atticus verify` now do), which are wired into both editors and the AI step registry; and merging the five test-only packages into two.
- **Fixed on the way:** a build whose outputs the cache did not carry left `packages/sdk/npm` missing, so the SDK's published layout check failed on a clean checkout that reused the cache. The codebase map held its Packages table three times and the build status its phase table twice, each copy newer in different places — damage from eleven merges in a day, now merged back into one with the newest of each.

### IntelliJ does the same work as VS Code, and Copilot runs the steps there (ADR 0105 addendum 2, ADR 0121 addendum 2)

- **The IntelliJ plugin's tool window has the views VS Code's sidebar has**: **Next**, **Waiting on you**, **Changes**, **Repos**, **Knowledge**, **Model runs** and **Prompt history**, then the **App** and the **Output** as before. Each reads in the background and says what is going on while it reads, what to do when there is nothing, and why when it can't.
- **The newest work shows in them.** A change says where its work is, its risk and the kinds of test it needs; open it for its tasks, and a task for the knowledge pages that apply to it — ranked, with the pages that also say the same and the question you are asked when pages disagree. A repo shows what it is in your own words, with a line your assistant drafted marked unconfirmed. Below the repos, every call that goes through gateways shows its whole path, each hop with its auth, the scopes its route demands and which side it faces. OpenSpec stores' changes are listed read-only with their task counts.
- **Waiting on you is grouped by change** (by kind when you ask, and it is remembered), changes with something blocking first. A need has Done, Not needed (with why) and Reopen; a finding has the critic's own actions — apply the fix, I fixed it by hand, not relevant, waive it where the finding says it may be, ask someone — and a constitution rule's finding is still never waived.
- **Filter… and Clear filter** on Waiting on you, Changes, Knowledge and Model runs: words to match an item's title, id, change or repo. Repos, Next and Prompt history have no filter box — their lists are short, and the tree's type-to-find is enough.
- **Every job has its own action.** Tools → **Atticus** holds about sixty entries in the order of a working day, and Find Action finds each by its own words: read a repo, verify its facts, survey it, draft its spec, say what it is, onboard it, add repos, scan, join the estate, the four change stages, the critic, find what a change needs, run a task, act on a finding, the knowledge steps, draft the org pack, bring in another repo, ask the catalog, check this machine, upgrade. **Run a Command…** is still there, and is now what you use when picking the command is the point — 47 commands that used to send you there are native actions, views or screens.
- **An AI step in IntelliJ** puts its slash command on the clipboard (or the prompt itself, where the prompt files aren't written yet), names **the kind of step and the model that kind expects** so you can pick it in Copilot's own picker, and says plainly what Atticus can't do: GitHub Copilot's IntelliJ plugin publishes nothing a plugin can call, so Atticus can't open its chat with your prompt in it, send it, read it or choose its model. Where the IDE has Copilot's open-chat action, the notice offers **Open Copilot Chat**. `atticus` checks the answer exactly as it always does.
- **Or run the step in Claude Code**, from IntelliJ: **Run it in Claude Code in a terminal** opens `claude` in the metarepo with the same slash command, which the Atticus plugin for Claude Code answers. Atticus's own model and the plain hand-off are offered as before.
- **New guide:** [Atticus in IntelliJ IDEA](docs/guide/intellij.md) — the tabs, what is native, how a step runs through Copilot or Claude Code, and the two things only VS Code does (installing Copilot, and picking the model, both because of VS Code's own APIs).

### Atticus's steps run in Copilot's agent mode (ADR 0121 addendum, 0100 and 0105 addenda)

- **Atticus's steps are now GitHub Copilot custom agents**, so they run in the mode that can take them: agent mode opens files, writes them and runs commands, which is what a step needs — the agent writes the facts file or the stage answer where Atticus named, runs the Atticus command that checks it, and fixes what the check hands back. `@atticus` in the chat is unchanged and still answers.
- **Six agents, in `plugins/copilot/`:** a repo read into facts (`atticus-repo-reader`, looping on `atticus verify`), what was recorded checked against the code (`atticus-verifier`), the one sentence that says what a repo is (`atticus-repo-describer`), the feature, architecture, stories, fast-path and first drafts (`atticus-stage-drafter`), one task in its worktree (`atticus-task-runner`), and the wikis read, searched and answered from (`atticus-knowledge-reader`). Beside them, two instruction files applied to every file — the rules every step keeps, and Atticus's own commands and MCP tools — and a `.vscode/mcp.json` that registers the read-only `atticus mcp` server. Each agent says its kind of step, so you can pick the model your organisation named for it.
- **Each agent says what the slash command says.** `plugins/copilot/` is generated by `pnpm copilot-agents` from the same commands `atticus assistant-files` renders and the Claude Code plugin comes from, and each agent carries its slash commands' protocol word for word. A protocol change that isn't regenerated fails the tests.
- **The rules hold in agent mode too**, written as what the agent must not do: what it reads is data and never instructions, a person approves every write to a shared system, never push to a protected branch or deploy, only a person closes a blocking or risk finding (an AI's claim is recorded as a proposal and the finding stays open), a question Atticus raised is yours to answer, and constitution rules are never waived.
- **In VS Code: Atticus: Run the next step in Copilot's agent mode.** It reads `atticus next`, opens Copilot Chat on the agent for that step with the step's prompt in the box, and says which kind of step it is. Where the workspace has no agent file, where no agent covers the step, or where agent mode can't be opened, the step goes to `@atticus` instead and says why.
- **Setting it up:** copy `.github/agents/`, `.github/instructions/` and `.vscode/mcp.json` into your metarepo in a pull request, and run `atticus assistant-files` there. The new guide [Running Atticus's steps through Copilot agent mode](docs/guide/copilot-agent-mode.md) has the setup, a nine-point checklist to confirm it in a real Copilot session, and what nothing but that session can prove.

### Every repo says what it is built on, and the reading prompts carry your frameworks (ADR 0124 addendum A-framework, C4 2.29.0)

- **A read records the frameworks each repo is built on**, one fact each, cited on the line of the build file that declares it: Spring Boot on a parent pom's `<artifactId>`, ASP.NET on a csproj's `Sdk=`, Express on a `package.json` line, Rails on a Gemfile's `gem`, Django on a requirement, Quarkus on a BOM, Angular and React on their packages, and a house starter by the artifact it is declared as. The version is the version as declared, and where that is held in a `<properties>` entry, a parent pom, a `<TargetFramework>` or a dependency-management block, the line that writes it is cited beside it. A module that declares its own framework records it against that module.
- **The verifier checks it like any other fact, and then some.** The declaration must be written on the cited line, the version on that line or one cited with it, and the name must be the one that declaration means — so a framework can't be renamed, and a starter nobody here can read must be named as the build file writes it.
- **Where you see it:** `map.md` says **Built on** above the trust line, each framework linking to the line that declares it; Map › Trust says it for every repo in the estate; `atticus verify <repo>` prints it; and a framework fact is correctable in the Map like any other.
- **Your reading prompts carry your frameworks' rules, and not the others'.** A batch already carried only the rules its kinds of file need; now it carries only the frameworks the repo declares too. A Spring repo gets JPA's table naming and Spring Data REST's paths and no longer carries Django's, Rails', ASP.NET's or TypeORM's; a front end gets its own router's rules. Each framework's rules say more about it than the one compressed clause they replace. Measured on the same repo and commit: a back-end batch's instructions were 2,773 tokens for every repo alike and are now 2,698 for a Spring repo, 2,742 for a .NET one and 2,678 for an Express one; a front end's were 2,259 and are 2,138 for Angular, 2,175 for React. Before the first read, the repo's `type` in `repos.manifest.yaml` says which rules to carry; after it, the facts do.
- **A framework whose patterns are nowhere in the facts is a question for you**: a Spring Boot repo with no REST entry, a React app with no screen, a Sidekiq dependency with no job. `atticus verify` says so, the report keeps it under `frameworkGaps`, the map puts it in the open and Trust marks it. It usually means the real entry points were missed. It is never a rejection, and nothing decides it for you.
- **A house framework becomes a prompt to teach it**, `atticus frameworks learn <name> --from <repo>`, rather than having its annotations read as ordinary code — and until then your assistant is told that one of its annotations or wrapper clients may serve a route with nothing in the repo's own code to show it, and to ask rather than guess.
- **A repo's type follows the facts**: a framework the repo's own facts declare settles it ahead of anything guessed from the files, with the citation as the signal.
- **`Gemfile`, `*.gemspec`, `requirements*.txt` and `composer.json` are build files** in the reading order, so a Rails, Python or PHP repo's framework is read in the first batch with the rest.
- `/atticus-map` changed: run `atticus assistant-files` (or update the Claude Code plugin) to get the new rules.

### A repo can say what it is, in your own words (ADR 0128, workspace schema 2, C4 2.26.0)

- **Each repo in `repos.manifest.yaml` can carry a `description`**: one line (up to 300 characters) saying what the repo is for and who uses it, with who wrote it, when, and whether a person wrote or confirmed it (`verified`) or your assistant drafted it and nobody has yet (`suggested`). A repo id at a bank often says nothing; this line does.
- **Where you write it:** `atticus repo add <url> --description "…"` adds one repo and asks for the line when you don't give it and you're at a terminal; `atticus repo describe <repo> --description "…"` writes or changes it later, `--accept` confirms a draft, `--reject` drops it, and `atticus repo describe` with no repo lists the repos that say nothing and the drafts waiting for you. Set up › Repos shows and edits it; Onboarding lists it beside each id; VS Code asks for each line after a bulk add, one at a time, with a skip (`Atticus: Say what a repo is`), and IntelliJ runs the same command.
- **A bulk add never stops to ask.** `atticus repos discover` asks nothing; what's left shows up as a step in `atticus next` — one for the repos that say nothing, one for the drafts to confirm.
- **Your assistant drafts one.** On the first read of a repo that says nothing, your assistant drafts a line from what it read, citing the lines it came from (`atticus repo describe <repo> --drafted "..." --cites <file>:<line>`, or one `{"about": ...}` line in a batched read's answer). Atticus keeps it marked unconfirmed everywhere it is used until you accept, change or drop it, and never presents it as your words. `/atticus-map` changed: run `atticus assistant-files` (or update the Claude Code plugin) to get the new rule.
- **What it improves:** every reading prompt carries it, clearly marked as your note, which the code beats where the two disagree; `atticus repo-type detect` leans a tie or suggests a type when no fingerprint matched; `map --estimate` is readier to propose leaving out a documentation site, a text corpus or vendored code; the join's "Which repo answers on …?" shows what each repo is in its options; a repo is matched on what it is as well as on its id when knowledge and stories are placed; and `map.md`, the Repos screen and Onboarding show it.
- **Nothing you have stops working.** `WORKSPACE_SCHEMA_VERSION` is 2 and every version-1 file is still read as it is; `atticus upgrade` tidies the number (`manifest-schema-v2`), keeping your comments, and backs the file up first.

### One npm package: `npm install -g @maycombhq/atticus` (ADR 0124 addendum D-npm2)

- **Atticus is installed from npm as one package, `@maycombhq/atticus`** (`npm install -g @maycombhq/atticus@beta`), instead of `@maycombhq/cli` and 46 others. It carries the command line and its app, self-contained: Atticus's own code and the third-party JavaScript it uses are bundled inside (esbuild), with OpenSpec as a program of its own, everything the commands read at run time, and one `THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES.txt` for the 95 third parties inside. The model (`@maycombhq/embed-model`) and onnxruntime (the six `@maycombhq/onnxruntime-*` packages) stay packages of their own, optional dependencies as before. On Linux x64 npm now installs 5 packages, 106 MB from the mirror and 179 MB on disk, against 141 packages, 117 MB and 250 MB for 2.0.0-beta.1; without the model (`--omit=optional`) it is one package of 6 MB.
- **Why:** npm rate-limits a new account creating many package names, so beta.1's publish stalled at 25 of 47; a bank's mirror scanned 141 packages for one install; and the install is quicker.
- **Published from now on:** `@maycombhq/atticus`, `@maycombhq/sdk` (the plugin SDK, C4, now with what it uses of the core bundled in, depending only on `zod` and `yaml`), `@maycombhq/embed-model` and the six `@maycombhq/onnxruntime-*`. Every other workspace package is private. The 39 names beta.1 published that are now private are deprecated on npm with "Atticus is now one package: npm install -g @maycombhq/atticus" (`pnpm release:npm:deprecate`, a dry run unless given `--yes`).
- **Where it said `@maycombhq/cli`**, it says `@maycombhq/atticus`: `atticus doctor`'s fixes and upgrade advice, the VS Code and IntelliJ plugins' install hints, the Claude Code plugin's hook, `atticus ci snippet` (`npx --yes @maycombhq/atticus@…`), the install and admin guides and the README.
- **For whoever releases Atticus:** `pnpm build` builds `packages/atticus` (`scripts/bundle/npm-atticus.mjs`); `pnpm release:npm` publishes only the nine, after a pack check that also holds every file `@maycombhq/atticus` lists in `dist/files.json` to its sha256 and every bundled third party to an allowed licence present in `THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES.txt`; `pnpm licences` checks the built package too; `pnpm npm:test` installs `@maycombhq/atticus` from a throwaway mirror with scripts off and, with the network blocked, runs doctor, init, a practice metarepo, `index embed`, a search by meaning, `map <repo> --estimate` and `atticus ui` answering its API.

### A model per kind of step (ADR 0121 addendum)

- **Five kinds of AI step, each on its own model:** read (`atticus map`, scan verify), draft (feature, architecture, stories, small changes, spec and change drafts, surveys, tasks), critic (every lens, the hand-up lens), knowledge (the knowledge scan and find, what a change needs, knowledge ask) and quick (judgments, context expansion and ordering, names, short texts). Atticus decides each request's kind in its code, never from its prompt.
- **runtime.yaml version 2** names the organisation's model for each kind: `models: { read, draft, critic, knowledge, quick }`, each a model of the provider or `vendor/family` for an editor's model. A kind that names none runs on the one model, as before. `atticus upgrade` (step `runtime-v2`) moves a version 1 file to version 2 and changes nothing else; `atticus init` writes version 2.
- **VS Code:** settings `atticus.models.read`, `.draft`, `.critic`, `.knowledge` and `.quick` override runtime.yaml's for a person; `atticus.model` stays the fallback for all of them. **Atticus: Pick the model** asks "for every step, or for one kind", then lists every model VS Code offers by vendor, with VS Code's own note on each model when it gives one. A stage's draft and its critic lenses are answered on their own kinds' models. A model VS Code doesn't offer (an admin turned it off) is passed over for the next choice, and the step says so once; nothing else is ever picked. The Next view says "in VS Code: <model> for <kind> steps".
- **IntelliJ:** a field per kind in Settings → Tools → Atticus. Copilot in IntelliJ doesn't let a plugin choose its model, so the AI step's notice names the model the step expects, to pick in Copilot's own picker; the fields reach the command line as `ATTICUS_MODEL_<KIND>`.
- **Model mode and headless Claude Code** run each kind on its model: the person's (`ATTICUS_MODEL_<KIND>`), else runtime.yaml's, else `adapters.model`'s; a model `models.allowed` doesn't list, or an editor's, is passed over and said once.
- **Every run record says its kind and the model that ran it** (run record version 4; older records still read). `atticus handoff done <run> --model <vendor/family>` records an editor's model, and VS Code passes it. `atticus runs`, `atticus runs show` and the app's Model runs page show both.
- **`atticus doctor` lists each kind's model and where it came from** (your setting, runtime.yaml, fallback), and why a named one was passed over. `atticus next --json` gives each AI step's kind and runtime.yaml's models; a hand-off prints the model its run expects.
- [Choosing models at an organisation on Copilot](docs/guide/models.md): a default table and the cost reasoning.

### More pages: every page in the menu again (ADR 0124 addendum D-app, follow-up)

- **The menu has More pages again**, below the six entries and always there: one button that says how many pages it holds (26) and opens every page the six entries' tabs don't show, by section (the Map's older pages, a change's other pages by phase, Knowledge's, Set up's, then Prompt history, AI quality and Learning). It stays open or closed as you left it, works with the keyboard alone (Enter or Space opens it, Escape closes it) and fits an editor's panel. Before, those pages opened only by their address or with `atticus ui --full`.
- **Model runs is a tab of Set up**, beside AI: what Atticus asked a model, and what came back, where you set AI up.
- **Ctrl+K (Cmd+K on a Mac) finds every page**, with or without `--full`, and the menu says so; clicking the hint opens it too.
- **`atticus ui --full`** makes every page a tab of its section and opens More pages; it no longer adds a seventh entry.
- **Fixed: Atticus 1's pages failed on an Atticus 2 change.** The feature brief, prototypes, architecture review and API mappings asked for files an Atticus 2 change never has, and reconcile ran with no diagrams; each now says what is missing (and what Atticus 2 writes instead) without an error.
- **The VS Code extension offers everything by default**: the Knowledge, Repos, Catalog and Model runs views (collapsed below the three), Open the prompt history and every other command. `atticus.fullUi` now only adds Atticus 1's legacy commands and starts the app with `--full`.

### Calls through an Apigee gateway land on the service behind it (ADR 0124 addendum A-gateway)

- **`/atticus-map` reads an Apigee gateway.** A batch holding a proxy or shared flow bundle, a `targetservers.json` or a `kvms.json` gets the gateway's rules (and no other batch does): each conditional flow is a route at its base path and suffix, its auth the policy attached before the target (shared flows followed), and its forwarding call the path the service receives after `target.copy.pathsuffix` and AssignMessage or JavaScript rewrites, with its host from the TargetServer or KVM entry. Every part is cited and checked like any other line.
- **The join follows the route.** Type the gateway's repo `gateway` in `repos.manifest.yaml` (an Apigee bundle is recognised anyway): a UI back end's `GET /forge/v1/accounts/{id}` to the gateway joins the service's `GET /accounts/{id}`, `via: gateway`, with the route's auth and a GatewayRoute made from the facts, and the fields that cross. A TargetServer whose hosts differ by environment and belong to different repos is asked, not guessed.
- **The critic's auth check needs no legacy reader for it:** it reads the auth the joined Interface carries from the gateway's facts.
- **`/atticus-map` changed:** run `atticus assistant-files` (or update the Claude Code plugin) to get the new rule.

### Scopes, and a call through a chain of gateways (ADR 0124 addendum A-gateway2)

- **The scopes a route demands are read, cited and checked.** `/atticus-map` asks for them with the route: an OAuthV2's `<Scope>` (a space-separated list is one scope each), a VerifyJWT or VerifyAccessToken scope claim, a scope a `Condition` asserts on the token's claims, or the scopes a shared flow checks. Each rests on the line that names it and is verified like any other claim, so an invented scope or one cited on the wrong line is refused, with the message saying which. They fill the GatewayRoute's `scopes`, which used to be empty.
- **The critic reads them.** A route through the gateway that demands no scope is a finding ("checks who the caller is and nothing about what they may do"); a scope a story's acceptance criteria name that no route of the change demands is a finding; and a caller whose own callers' routes demand scopes, none of which the route downstream demands, is a question about how it gets a token. The four questions and the existing checks are unchanged.
- **A call is followed through every gateway on its way.** Where a route forwards into another gateway's route, the join goes on, so `caller → internal gateway → partner gateway → the vendor's service` reads as one call with its hops in order, each with its own auth, its scopes and whether it faces inside or out. Each hop is a GatewayRoute whose target is the next hop. `map.md`'s call lines and the app's Interface page ("The path of the call") print the whole path.
- **Internal or external is read, never guessed:** from the repo's manifest type (`gateway-external`, `gateway-internal`), else the proxy's `<VirtualHost>`, which `/atticus-map` now asks for as a config fact. A word neither known list covers leaves the hop a plain gateway.
- **A ring of routes is a question, not a loop.** A route that forwards back into a gateway the call has already been through, or a chain longer than four gateways, raises a question and leaves the call joined at the gateway it does reach.
- **The verifier checks an `authCite`'s policy kind.** It opens the policy the cited line attaches and refuses an auth that policy cannot set: a `VerifyAPIKey` is not `oauth2`, and a `SpikeArrest` authenticates nobody (`auth-policy-kind`). A policy Atticus doesn't know, or a citation outside a bundle, is not judged.
- **`/atticus-map` changed:** run `atticus assistant-files` (or update the Claude Code plugin) to get the new rules.

### A change is read from what its stages write (ADR 0124 addendum B-change, C4 2.25.0)

- **A change's footprint comes from its feature placement, target flows and tasks**, not from `proposal.md`, `interfaces.md` and `data.md`, which the stages never write. Risk, test kinds, conflicts between open changes, the critic's catalog slice and the stories' definition of done all read it. A legacy change's files are still read.
- **Risk sees what matters, and says where:** a call to an outside system (a vendor, a model provider), a change to who may do what, personal or consent data, a schema change, a breaking change and a change across repos each add a factor citing the target-flow steps that raised it. Each asks its own tests on top of the test strategy's row, with why: an outside call, contract and integration tests against a stub with its failure paths; an authorisation change or personal data, security tests. Weights, words and kinds are the change policy's to change.
- **The state shown follows the work:** `atticus change status` says "Stories done; the change is still in define: raise "Send to architecture"…", the app's cockpit and status API say the same, and `atticus next` gives the hand-off instead of running a done stage again. The hand-off PR still moves the state.
- **Conflicts** now also name the flows two open changes both change.

### Knowledge per story and task, and pages that disagree (ADR 0124 addendum B-task-knowledge, C4 2.25.0)

- **Each task lists the wiki and document pages that apply to it**, not only the change as a whole: how-tos and runbooks, standards, requests, patterns and decisions, kept when they are about what the task changes (or name the component it calls or its table). Each entry gives the page, section and line, its last update, author and owner where known, its copy in the snapshot, and why it applies. It shows in the story file under the task, `atticus task context` (and `--json`, the MCP tool, `task run`), the hand-down to the repo (`plan.md` or `.atticus/tasks/<id>.md`), `knowledge.yaml` (`tasks`) and the change's Knowledge panel.
- **Several pages on one topic are one entry**: the best leads and the others are listed as "also", ranked verified by a person, then newest, then owned, then the closer match; near-duplicate items are grouped.
- **Pages that disagree go to a person.** When a change or task relies on a topic two pages disagree on, Atticus asks once, in the change's questions, quoting both pages with their owners and dates and the page a signal suggests. Nothing is picked silently. When code verified in a repo holds one page's value (a path, URL or config key), the code settles it and the fact is cited. `atticus knowledge conflicts` lists them all.
- **Measured:** per task, recall and precision 1.00 on the four fixture cases of the new task-knowledge bench (before: 0.50 / 0.50 on the SSO change); the context bench's recall holds. [The knowledge guide](docs/guide/knowledge.md#7-knowledge-per-story-and-per-task) says how it works.
- **API (C4 2.25.0, additive):** `ChangeKnowledgeItem.tasks`, `ChangeKnowledgeView.conflicts`, `KnowledgeItemView.conflicts` and `KnowledgeConflictView` (decided by a person or the code; a signal's page as `suggested`).

### Atticus 2 on OpenSpec: specs that accumulate, short stories, a work-item export (ADR 0006 addendum; ADR 0124 addendum B-short)

- **OpenSpec agrees with Atticus about a change.** The `org-flow` schema is at version 2: proposal (feature stage), design (architecture), stories, specs and tasks (stories stage), in that order. A real change went from 3 of 9 artifacts and a failing `openspec validate` to all five done and valid. `atticus upgrade` writes the new schema and re-writes each change's stories from its kept answer (no model call).
- **The stories write OpenSpec spec deltas**, one capability per flow, a requirement per story and a scenario per criterion, ADDED or MODIFIED against `openspec/specs/`. `openspec validate --strict` gates every stories draft, the stories' hand-off and archive. `atticus change archive` now runs `openspec archive`, so `openspec/specs/` grows with each change.
- **The feature stage writes `proposal.md`** from the brief and the placement; **`design.md` has Rollout and rollback**, a line per table the change writes or alters, so the critic's rollback check no longer fires on every round.
- **Each stage starts from `openspec instructions`** for its artifacts, with `openspec/config.yaml`'s context and rules. `atticus next` reads which stage comes next from `openspec status`; `atticus change list` shows OpenSpec's task counts; a task's context says OpenSpec's progress (`instructions apply`).
- **Shorter story files:** each task once, its prompt in `tasks/<n>.prompt.md`, the flow's error paths left in the target flow it links to. The real change's stories went from 226, 335 and 273 lines to 153, 213 and 178. **Tasks are numbered per story without gaps** (1.1, 1.2, 2.1); `atticus task` still takes a task's old number.
- **`atticus change export <change> [ado|jira] --out file.csv`** writes Azure DevOps' work-item import CSV (a User Story per story, its Tasks under it, the criteria in Acceptance Criteria) or Jira's. [The guide](docs/guide/openspec.md) says how to import it.
- **Newer OpenSpec releases:** `atticus doctor` reports the OpenSpec beta features found from its own help and which path Atticus takes; stores are read with `openspec list` run in each store, from their files only when that can't run. A weekly job runs the adapter's suite against OpenSpec's newest release and beta.

### The command line starts quickly, and the Set up screen answers sooner

- **`atticus --version` answers without loading any of Atticus (about 40 ms of CPU, was about 900), and `atticus --help` loads no schema (about 75 ms, was about 900).** The editors, the app's server and the assistant's hooks run the command line often, and every run paid for loading every package before it read its arguments.
- **A command loads only what it uses.** The model providers, the context tools and the critic's code search load for the commands that may run a model or the critic, not for `next`, `history`, `runs`, `waiting`, `mcp`, `doctor`, `setup`, `verify`, `baseline`, `upgrade`, `support`, `change list` or `change status`. Measured CPU on the 120-repo test estate: `history` 970 → 470 ms, `change list` 1,040 → 750 ms, `verify` 1,030 → 710 ms, `doctor` 2,700 → 1,600 ms.
- **The app's Set up screen (`/api/setup`) answers sooner on a large catalog.** Checking whether `atticus upgrade` has catalog files to migrate no longer reads every record as YAML (a record whose text states its version plainly is checked from that line), and the catalog the app keeps in memory is no longer read again because `atticus doctor` wrote its probe file in `.atticus/`: only a change in the catalog or the org pack makes the app read it again. Its answer is unchanged.
- **For contributors:** `@maycombhq/core/light` exports `SCHEMA_VERSION` and `createLog` without loading a schema; `SCHEMA_VERSION` is defined in `packages/core/src/schema/version.ts` (still exported from `schema/common.ts` and the package). `packages/cli/test/startup.test.ts` fails if `--version` loads a workspace package.

### The commands you run all day answer in about a second

- **`atticus next`, `atticus change status` and `atticus questions list` no longer read the whole metarepo to answer.** On an estate the size of a real trial (about 1,500 catalog records, 2,300 facts, 400 flows, a change with 3 stories and 14 tasks), measured on the build machine: `next` 4.7 → 1.4 s, `change status` 3.9 → 1.2 s, `questions list` 1.0 → 0.6 s. Every one answers exactly what it answered before, to the letter.
- **A record file is parsed once and the parse kept.** `next` parsed 2,179 YAML files on every run and `change status` 1,951, most of them records neither command looked at. The catalog, the specs and the knowledge items are now read with their parses kept in `.atticus/cache/`, keyed by each file's size and modification time the way the search index already keys its own: a file that changed is read again, a file that didn't isn't, and the cache stays on this machine (Git ignores it). The first command after a scan still pays for the parse; the ones after it don't. `next` now parses 235 files and `change status` 54.
- **A change's footprint reads the flows it touches, not every flow in the estate.** It needed four of the estate's 400 and parsed all 400. The flows are indexed by their file names (`specs/<repo>/<id>.yaml`) and read one at a time.
- **`atticus next` runs `openspec status` only where its answer could move the change on.** The child process cost about 0.6 s per change and was run for every change, including those whose state is already past the stage OpenSpec could report.
- **`atticus questions list` and `atticus questions show` load what they use.** They read files only, so they no longer load the model providers, the critic or the whole planner (805 modules, now 257): the questions a stage holds back come from `@maycombhq/planner/waiting`.
- **For contributors:** `packages/synth`'s `writeTrialEstate` writes a metarepo in that trial's shape — invented Forge throughout — and `packages/m5-exit/test/perf-commands.test.ts` holds the three commands to a budget on it, first run and steady state. `readParsedFiles` (`packages/core/src/record-cache.ts`) is how a folder of record files is read with its parses kept.

### Reading another team's metarepo leaves it exactly as it was (ADR 0119 addendum)

- **Atticus no longer writes its own cache into a repository it was asked to read.** The cache that made `atticus next` and `change status` three times faster chose its folder from the files it was reading, so `atticus absorb`, a federated scan and a linked estate each left `.atticus/cache/…` and `.atticus/.gitignore` inside the other team's repository — and absorb then offered our own cache as content of theirs to translate. The cache is now kept only under the metarepo the command is working in, which the command names; a read that names no root keeps nothing and writes nothing.
- **Nothing of the speed is lost.** `next`, `change status` and `questions list` answer in the same time as before (1.4 s, 1.2 s and 0.6 s on an estate of about 1,500 records). A scan that reads a federated catalog keeps those parses in **your** metarepo, so even that read stays fast while their folder is untouched.
- **For contributors:** `readParsedFiles`, `readRepoSpec`, `readCatalog` and `readKnowledge` take `cacheRoot`; `metarepoRootOf` is gone, so nothing works a root out from the folder it is reading. `packages/adopt/test/read-only.test.ts` fingerprints a whole source metarepo — every file, path and byte, and every folder — before absorb, a federated scan and the conflict check, and asserts it byte-identical after; the M3 absorb bar does the same through the CLI for every source fixture.

### The critic costs less, and only a person closes its findings (ADR 0115 addendum, 2026-10-25)

- **An assistant can't silence the critic.** A live run's drafting assistant dismissed a finding itself (`resolution.by: claude`). Now an AI (an assistant, an agent, a runtime, a bot account: `assistantActor`) that dismisses, applies or edits a blocking or risk finding only proposes it: the finding stays open, "proposed by the assistant (claude) as not relevant, waiting on a person", in `atticus findings`, the gate's reasons and the app's stage tab. An assistant never waives. The stage stops for the person rather than draft again, and a person's `atticus finding <change> <id> not-relevant` closes it (with no reason, the assistant's is kept, saying who accepted it). `atticus finding` run in a coding assistant's terminal with no `--by` records the assistant. Older finding files an AI resolved are read as open.
- **Lenses once per draft worth reviewing.** A stage's draft that fails the critic's code checks goes back without a lens call, and the round doesn't count toward the gate's limit; a draft with nothing changed calls no lens and keeps its findings.
- **A redraft's lenses see the diff.** The lenses get what changed since the last reviewed draft and the findings still open about it; findings about parts that didn't change are carried as they were.
- **Each lens gets the estate that bears on its question,** cut to the entries naming what the draft names and capped at a share of its token budget; the round's record says each lens's size and what was cut. On the real change's 40 recorded lens prompts this halves the tokens (2.21M to 1.10M), before the calls no longer made.
- **Run records.** Only the newest round at a gate keeps its lenses' prompts and answers in review/; `.runs/` is pruned per runtime.yaml `retention.runsDays` as a stage runs.
- Contracts: C3 unchanged (proposals sit beside findings); C4 2.25.0 adds a stage finding's optional `proposal`.

### npm installs carry search by meaning (ADR 0124 addendum D-npm)

- **`npm install --global @maycombhq/cli` through a bank's npm mirror now gives search by meaning, with nothing downloaded after install and no install scripts.** `@maycombhq/embed-model` carries the model (all-MiniLM-L6-v2, 79 MB packed), its tokenizer, model card and Apache-2.0 licence; each `@maycombhq/onnxruntime-<platform>-<arch>` carries onnxruntime's CPU build for that platform and `@maycombhq/onnxruntime-wasm` its WebAssembly fallback, with onnxruntime's MIT licence. npm installs only your platform's runtime. On Linux x64 the install downloads 117 MB (141 packages). `--omit=optional` still leaves it out.
- **The files are filled when a package is packed, never at install:** each package's `prepack` takes them from `.cache/pinned` (filled by `pnpm model:fetch`), checks each against its `pins.json` and refuses to pack when one is missing or wrong. The repository still holds no binaries.
- **`atticus doctor` says the model is ready:** "Embedding model: ready: all-minilm-l6-v2@… (86 MB, Apache-2.0), installed with Atticus, in …; runtime: native".
- **Fixed: `atticus sandbox create` failed after an npm install** ("The Forge fixture isn't here"): `@maycombhq/learn` now carries the Forge practice fixtures.
- **`pnpm model:fetch` downloads through `HTTPS_PROXY`**, as the plugin's fetch step already did.
- **For whoever releases Atticus:** every package is published public (`publishConfig`); `pnpm release:npm [--dry-run]` runs the pack check, then `pnpm -r publish --access public --tag beta --no-git-checks`; `.github/workflows/publish-npm.yml` does it on a published GitHub release with the `NPM_TOKEN` secret; `pnpm npm:test` installs every package from a throwaway mirror with scripts off, the way a bank would, and searches by meaning with the network blocked. The licence check also fails a package that ships a third party's files without their licence text. [The admin guide](docs/guide/admin.md#publishing-to-npm) says how to publish.

## 2.0.0-beta.1 (2026-10-25)

The first beta of Atticus 2: the assistant reads, Atticus verifies. It was taken through a real trial before release: a 4,900-file Java and React monorepo the bench has never seen was read into 2,277 cited facts and 409 flows (0 rejected), joined from its front end to its API, and one real change was taken through feature, architecture and stories; everything that trial hit is fixed in the sections below. Run for real before this release: the web app on that metarepo (every page, no browser errors), the VS Code extension in VS Code 1.143 (68 checks from source, 29 from the .vsix), the IntelliJ plugin built against IntelliJ IDEA 2025.3.6 (64 tests, the Plugin Verifier, the IDE run with the plugin), and the embeddings plugin's model download through a proxy.

Not yet tried, so expect rough edges: Windows and macOS installs and `pnpm check` on them; the AI steps through Copilot's model in VS Code (`@atticus`), and Copilot in IntelliJ; the app page inside IntelliJ's panel (JCEF); a second real change, and an estate of many repos read with Copilot at tier 2.

### The model download works behind a proxy

- **`fetch-model.mjs` (the embeddings plugin's fetch step) now downloads through the proxy `HTTPS_PROXY` names.** Node's fetch ignores `HTTPS_PROXY` on its own, so behind a corporate proxy every download failed with a bare "fetch failed". The script runs again with `NODE_USE_ENV_PROXY=1` (Node 22.21 and later honour it), and a failure now names the network error, the proxy (never its credentials) and, on an older Node, that it needs 22.21 or later; `--list` and `--from` remain the way round. Tried for real: the model, onnxruntime and their licences downloaded through a proxy in about ten seconds, every file matching its pin.

### The IntelliJ plugin, run in IntelliJ IDEA (ADR 0099)

- **It builds without an IDE on the machine.** The build downloads IntelliJ IDEA 2025.3.6, since IntelliJ IDEA Community isn't published on its own from 2025.3, and its JUnit tests run (all 64 pass).
- **The Plugin Verifier finds it compatible with IntelliJ IDEA 2025.3.6 and 2026.2.3**, with no deprecated or experimental API left. `./gradlew verifyPlugin` downloads no Marketplace plugins, and `-Patticus.verifyIdes=<versions>` picks the IDEs to check.
- **It was run in the IDE with a practice metarepo open.** The status bar, the tool window and its tabs, the app started and stopped with the IDE, the Waiting tab, the gutter's read, the actions, Next Step, Run a Command… and the settings page all worked. `./gradlew runIde -Patticus.openProject=<metarepo>` opens a folder, and `./gradlew runIdeForUiTests` adds JetBrains' Remote Robot server.
- **Try a practice metarepo opens the metarepo,** not the sandbox folder around it.
- **The path to `atticus` is found on Linux and macOS as on Windows** when its extension is written in another case (`atticus.cmd` for `.CMD`).
- **The next step has the same words as in VS Code** for a small change, needs, the org pack's draft and checking knowledge, and a title for reading a repo (`map`) instead of its reason twice.

### The VS Code extension, run in a real VS Code (2026-10-25)

- **The extension was run in VS Code 1.143.1** (Linux, under Xvfb), from the source folder and installed from its `.vsix`, on a practice metarepo with an assistant's facts for forge-ledger, and in a window with no folder: 68 checks from the source folder, 29 from the package. Details in [the extension's changelog](packages/vscode/CHANGELOG.md).
- **Fixed: opening a repo on the canvas from VS Code landed on a change's Architecture tab** (`#/architecture`, Change › Architecture since the app's six entries). It opens the Map's estate canvas now (`#/canvas?node=…`).
- **`pnpm --filter atticus test:vscode` downloads VS Code** with `@vscode/test-electron` when none is installed (into `.cache/vscode-test`), runs as root under `xvfb-run`, and also runs with no folder; `test:vscode:packaged` tests the installed `.vsix`.

### The app on a real Atticus 2 metarepo, fixed (D-real, 2026-10-25)

- **Map › Trust counts each repo once.** A facts file's companions (`<repo>.questions.jsonl`, `.rejected.jsonl`, `.reviewed.jsonl`, `.corrections.jsonl`, `.verify.yaml`) were read as another repo's facts file and reported as unreadable.
- **The app says the Atticus 2 read, not a scan.** Home's set-up list, its next step, Onboarding, the Architecture tab, the estate canvas and the empty-catalog answers say `/atticus-map <repo>` (or `atticus map <repo>`), then `atticus verify <repo> --write`, and point at Map › Trust.
- **"How AI steps run" names the assistant `atticus probe` measured here, with its tier**, on Home, in the set-up list and in `atticus next --json`; runtime.yaml's default when nothing was measured.
- **The Ask box's example comes from your own catalog** (a table or entry point), else it is neutral. A question that names an outside system the code calls ("what calls the LLM?") searches its calls, in the app and in `atticus ask`, instead of listing every interface.
- **The estate canvas and coverage show the system the manifest names and the outside systems the code calls**; with no System record, the Ecosystem map says what each repo's facts hold (entries, tables, calls, flows).
- **A flow's sequence diagram draws its caller** when the join says which flow calls it.
- **A stage tab's Run next follows its own stage and the gate**: once stories are done, Stories says to raise the hand-off PR (or sync it, or what blocks it), not "at the feature stage".
- **All changes lists a staged change's stage artifacts first**, and the OpenSpec-era ones (proposal, specs, interfaces, mappings, data, ux, snow) only when their files are there.
- **The first-run tour stays closed** once skipped, finished or left behind on another page, for that metarepo.
- **At an editor panel's width (600 to 1000 px) the menu stays beside the page**, compact, instead of stacking above it and leaving the page a narrow column.

### Where code goes, with the right verb (B-place, ADR 0124 addendum B-place)

- **Three kinds of place for a new step.** A target flow's step now says `at: path:line` (the code there changes), `new: <path>` (a file the change creates, in the folder of its nearest kin) or `uses: path:line` (existing code it only calls, which doesn't change). Before, `at` meant all three, and a step with no place took a neighbour's, so 9 of 15 tasks of a real change named code the new code should only call.
- **The architecture stage checks them.** An `at` with no line is refused; so is a call-out, publish, lookup or read `at` the code it calls (another flow's call or read on that line, or a method the flow doesn't cite), with "use `uses:`". A `new` file must not exist yet and must sit in a folder the repo has; `uses` must name a file and a line the repo has. A step with no place is a note.
- **Stories and tasks say the verb**: "change `<file>:<line>`", "new file `<path>` (uses `<file>:<line>`)", or "new code — place not given"; never "beside". A new file's steps are one task. On a fixture mirroring the real change: 4 tasks from 14 steps, none naming a wrong file (8 of 8 wrong before).
- **A step the existing code already does is a note**, not a task: its `uses` line is an existing flow's step with the same write, read or call ("already done by `<file:line>`").
- **`atticus task context`** lists only the task's own places under "Files likely to change", and the code it calls under "Code it calls".
- **Smaller fixes from the same run.** A resumed stage no longer says "nothing changed" when the draft it finishes wrote files. A redraft after a finding or a critic's risk is told where its last draft is. Prompts no longer tell a hand-off to call a `check_draft` tool it doesn't have. A story's "Not judged yet" notes are one line with a count. `atticus init` no longer ignores `.atticus/metarepo.yaml`, the stamp `atticus upgrade` asks you to commit (upgrade step `stamp-committed` fixes an older `.gitignore`). The docs and slash commands say `atticus stories`.

### What a knowledge scan of a docs folder hit, fixed (K-docs, ADR 0083 addendum K-docs, ADR 0127)

- **`atticus knowledge scan` reads every page.** It used to put every page in one prompt, and the prompt silently dropped what didn't fit (76 of 103 pages) while recording those pages as read. Now it plans batches that fit the knowledge reader's prompt, from its budget or the model's limit, or `--batch-tokens`. A model or headless runtime reads batch after batch in one run. A hand-off hands one batch a run: it names the pages that wait, and after the resume says how many are left and to run the scan again. A page too big for the prompt on its own is named, with what to raise, and is never recorded as read.
- **A wiki source can `include` and `exclude` pages** by pattern (`runbooks/`, `drafts/**`, `architecture/*.md`). `sources.yaml` is now version 2, and `atticus upgrade` moves a version 1 file (step `sources-v2`, only the number changes). A page two sources cover is read once, by the source nearest to it; the other source lists it as skipped and says where it is read.
- **A quote that wraps inside a blockquote, or runs into the next list item, can be quoted.** The check leaves out a line's `>` and list markers; every word must still be on the page, in order.
- **Prose that names the system prompt is no longer held back as an instruction to AI tools.** The filter flags imperatives aimed at an AI ("ignore previous instructions", "you are now", "disregard the rules above", "your new system prompt is", hidden text), not a "System prompt" or "Instructions" heading or a sentence about how a product builds its prompts (`defences@1.1.0`).
- **The org-knowledge index covers what was snapshotted and scanned.** It indexes the pages a snapshot lists, and once the scan has read a source, only the pages it read. A comment line in a fenced code block is no longer indexed as a heading.
- **`atticus change knowledge <change> --confirm <id>` takes any knowledge item**, wiki or document section, listed or not. It applies like one a stage picked. Each `--confirm` or `--exclude` prints only what changed.

### What a real change's run through feature, architecture and stories hit (ADR 0087 addendum, 2026-10-25)

- **The critic's finding reaches the next draft, with what to fix.** A blocking finding from the round in which the assistant also asked a question is handed back with the answers; it was lost before. Each critic finding's message now says what to fix.
- **A risk gets one more draft.** The critic's risk findings go back once, with their fix, while the gate has a round left; they never block.
- **A fix to a rendered file is refused, with a pointer to the source.** `apply` and `edit-and-apply` refuse a patch to a file a stage renders (a story's .md, feature.md, design.md), and a recorded hand edit of only such files, naming the file to fix (`stage/stories-answer.yaml`, feature.yaml, target-flows/).
- **A held-back question is weighed only against answered questions about the same target**, not every question the change answered.
- **The questions over a stop's cap are seen.** The stage keeps them in its loop file, names them at the stop, and `atticus questions list` lists them by change and stage. A change's question says "Proposed scope: this change (<id>)".
- **An outside system a call fact names (a model provider, a vendor) is a call-out's target**, and the estate index lists it.
- **design.md labels a call to an entry that exists today `existing`**, not `planned-new`.
- **A critic finding is never dropped silently.** A quote under 12 characters counts when it is word for word on the line it cites; what is left out is said in the stage's notes.
- **A standard listed as a request** is refused as `stories.standard-as-request`: a standard is followed, not raised.
- **`atticus next` starts with the change in hand** (its questions, then its stage) and names the assistant a probe measured here.

### Tasks a developer takes, and shorter stories (B-tasks, ADR 0124 addendum B-tasks)

- **Steps that go into the same file are one task.** `atticus plan stories` used to make a task per target-flow step. Now consecutive steps whose code goes into one file are one task with all their criteria. A step that adds a table or columns stays a task of its own, since its migration lands first. A task keeps its first step's id and lists the steps it covers. An answer written per step still reads.
- **Steps existing code already does are notes, not tasks.** When a run of new steps is what an existing flow already does, step by step (the same call or table, no new field), the first step calls that code and the rest are noted as "already done by `<file:line>`".
- **New code says where it goes.** A new step with no place goes beside the nearest step of its flow that has one, or the code it calls, shown as "new code beside `<file>:<line>`". The architecture stage now asks for a new step's place in a new flow too.
- **Shorter story files.** Today's flow is one line with a link to the target flow, each criterion is written once under its task, and tasks point at their criteria. On the fixtures: 788 lines to 486 and 17 tasks to 7 for a one-page feature.

### What a trial on one repo found, fixed (ADR 0124 addendum C-join, the fallback)

- **A relative call joins the API that serves it.** A front end's `fetch("/api/...")` has no host, so the reader guessed a `to` the estate doesn't have, and `atticus join` joined none of its calls. Such a call (or one naming no target) now joins by method and path the one entry of its own repo that serves it, else the one of the estate, and is marked inferred: `atticus verify <repo> --write` says how many and where, `atticus join` prints "inferred" beside each. When two or more could answer, a person is asked which. A vendor the org pack lists keeps its name. `/atticus-map` now says to leave `to` out of a relative call, or name the component that serves it.
- **`atticus verify <repo> --write` says what it wrote in a few lines**: counts by record kind (`+` added, `~` changed, `-` removed) and "N in specs/<repo>/". `--verbose` lists every record and flow; `--json` gives them all.
- **`atticus init` makes the metarepo a git repository** (branch main) when the folder isn't inside one, and says so; a folder inside a repository is left alone. Its next step, and `atticus next` for a repo not read yet, say `/atticus-map <repo>` and `atticus verify <repo> --write`, not `atticus scan`.
- **`atticus repos discover` skips hidden folders, `node_modules` and nested worktrees**, so an assistant's worktree under `.claude/` no longer decides what a repo is.
- **`atticus doctor` says which repos' facts are read** (at which commit, or not read yet) instead of "Last scan".

### What the big repo's first read still missed (A-big2, ADR 0124 addendum A-big)

- **Vendored projects inside a repo are proposed.** `atticus map <repo> --estimate` now proposes a folder with its own LICENSE and build file (setup.py, pyproject.toml, pom.xml, package.json, build.gradle) that the root build doesn't include as a module (Maven modules, Gradle settings, npm, yarn or pnpm workspaces). It also proposes a folder with a LICENSE and no build file whose packages aren't the repo's own. Folders of text pages with no code (`.html`, `.htm`, `.xml`, beside `.txt` and `.md`) are proposed as documents. A reference folder holding both is proposed whole.
- **The parts are of product code.** Every folder that qualifies is proposed (the first 10 are printed, the `exclude:` block holds all). The estimate says the parts are planned over what is left with them out.
- **`atticus verify` summarises every list.** Entries without an `authCite`, entries no flow follows, rejected facts, refused questions and the rest are each a count, the first 5, and where the whole list is (`verify.yaml` or `rejected.jsonl`). It says how many questions were raised now, raised before, refused, and are open for the repo.

### A big repo's first read (A-big, ADR 0124 addendum A-big)

- **Coverage and the estimate count less that isn't code.** Translation bundles under a locale folder (`i18n/`, `locales/`, `lang/`, `translations/`) and JSON or XML files over 2,000 lines or 200 KB are no longer source. Build, config, API and migration files count whatever their size.
- **`atticus map <repo> --estimate` proposes what to leave out.** Folders that are mostly data or docs, or that hold a library's own LICENSE and no build file, are listed with their tokens, the tokens before and after, and a ready `exclude:` block for `org-pack/scan/<repo>.yaml`. Atticus never writes it: a person accepts it in a pull request.
- **A repo bigger than one session is read in parts.** Over 150,000 tokens of source, the estimate proposes parts by folder, balanced by tokens, and `/atticus-map` reads one part per session or sub-agent into the same facts file, verifying after each.

### `atticus verify`: what readers of a real repo asked for (A-read, ADR 0124 addendum A-read)

- **A flow's steps count toward coverage.** A file that only a flow's steps or error paths cite (a service, a helper it walks through) is now read, not `unknown`.
- **"Read, nothing here" has a place: `<repo>.reviewed.jsonl`.** One `{"path": "<file or glob>", "why": "..."}` per line, for files read and found to hold no fact (a pure computation library). Verify counts them apart ("6 read, 3 reviewed with nothing to record, 0 unknown"), never in the coverage ratio, and lists each line with its why and files in `<repo>.verify.yaml` and on the map's trust line. A path that isn't in the repo, or a glob that matches nothing, is rejected as `bad-reviewed`.
- **A `validate` step's rule is checked on its line**, like other steps' values: a range, `@NotNull` or a pattern the line doesn't write is rejected.
- **An entry's `auth` needs `authCite`**, the line that sets it (an annotation, middleware, policy or setting). An `auth` without one is left out of the kept fact and the catalog, and listed as a note; a cited one is checked like any part. Facts written before still parse.
- **Clearer runs.** When nothing is unknown or rejected, verify says the read is complete instead of asking for unknown files again. A fact with several faults has all of them reported in one run.

### OpenSpec stores (beta): compatible, never depended on (ADR 0126, ADR 0006 addendum)

- **The metarepo can be an OpenSpec store.** `atticus init --openspec-store` and `atticus upgrade --openspec-store` write `.openspec-store/store.yaml`. It holds `version: 1`, the org pack's name as a kebab-case `id`, and `remote` when the metarepo's origin is a URL. This is the shape the pinned OpenSpec 1.14.0 reads. A developer then runs `openspec store register <their clone>`. A store.yaml that is there is never replaced. A plain `atticus upgrade` never adds one. A repo adopted in place, whose OpenSpec folder isn't at its root, is refused.
- **Hand-down can add the metarepo to a code repo's `references:`.** Turn it on with `referenceInRepos: true` in the new, optional `org-pack/openspec.yaml` (C6, `OpenSpecSettings`, its own version 1; a pack without it is unchanged). The entry goes into the repo's existing `openspec/config.yaml`, keeping its comments and every other key, in the hand-down's commit. When the metarepo isn't a store yet, or the repo has no OpenSpec config, the hand-down's problems say so.
- **Existing stores are read, read-only and labelled.** `openspec.yaml` `stores:` names clones by path. Their open changes follow the metarepo's in `atticus change list` ("From OpenSpec stores (read-only, beta)"; `store` and `readOnly` in `--json`). `atticus openspec check` gives one line per store. `atticus feature spec` says when a store already has a spec for the capability. A later store.yaml version, or a folder with none, is read with a note.
- **`atticus doctor` says whether the installed OpenSpec has stores.** This is a warning only when the metarepo uses them.

### VS Code: /verify checks the facts

- **`@atticus /verify <repo>` and _Atticus: Verify a repo's facts against its code_ run `atticus verify`** (ADR 0124), with no model: the chat shows what was rejected and offers `--write`, or sends the rejected facts back with `/map`. They ran the older scanner's `scan verify` with Copilot's model before. That check stays, as `/scanverify` and _Verify a repo's last scan_, behind `atticus.fullUi`.

### A criterion is written once and accepts several tasks (B-once, ADR 0124 addendum B-once)

- **Stories are shorter.** A story criterion may list `also: [<task ids>]`, the other tasks of its story it accepts, so a behaviour a field takes through entry, validation, write and response is written once, not on each task. The stories prompt asks for this. On the deliverBy fixture the story says in 4 criteria what took 8.
- **The checks read a task's own and shared criteria.** A task needs criteria only when none accepts it; its fields may be checked, and its error case said, by a criterion written on another task. A criterion may check its `also` tasks' fields. An `also` naming a task the story doesn't have is refused (`stories.unknown-also`).
- **Every output carries them.** The story file lists each criterion once, with "(also accepts tasks 1.2, 1.3 and 1.4)"; each task's list and prompt, and `atticus task context`, give the task every criterion that accepts it; `atticus bench stories --change` puts a shared criterion on each step it accepts. An answer without `also` reads and renders as before.

### Lean batch prompts: each batch carries only the rules its files need (A-lean, ADR 0124 addendum A-lean)

- **`atticus map <repo>` (`--handoff`, `--copy`, `--mode model`) fits smaller models.** Each batch prompt now carries the reading protocol's core rules and only the topic rules its files need: front end, back end, routes, stores, deploy, config, gRPC, SOAP, GraphQL and events, and flows. A file's kinds come from its path. When the path can't say, the file gets the rule. The instructions were 2,971 tokens for every batch. They are now 2,648 for a back-end batch, 2,180 for a front end's and 1,419 for a deploy batch's. The Forge ledger fixture now batches from 6,814 tokens, down from 7,042.
- **The plan counts each batch's own rules**, so every prompt still fits `--max-tokens`. A read whose files leave too little room beside their rules is refused with the tokens it needs.
- **`/atticus-map` and the Claude Code plugin are unchanged.** An assistant that reads the whole repo still gets every rule, word for word.

### Org knowledge in every stage: the walk's design gaps (B2, ADR 0124 addendum B2, C4 2.24.0)

- **The feature stage picks the knowledge that applies.** From the brief and the flows and systems it leads to, it selects the patterns, standards, decisions, processes and requests, lists them in the change's `knowledge.yaml` and sends them to the placer, whose rules now ask it to name an item where it bears on a gap or question. The Feature design tab's Knowledge panel is filled from the first stage. The stage tests' pinned feature prompt was re-keyed, with its hand-written answer re-written for the new rule (its gap names the two requests that bear on it).
- **Every stage picks wiki and document sections, vendors and glossary terms too.** Apart from the estate and the items, each stage selects them by the index's own retrieval (BM25 over the index, fused with the title's search, ADR 0116) against its own input, within a share of its room (at most 15% and 1,500 tokens). The top three go to the drafter in the page's words with where they are read from, the rest one line each; a vendor or a term is one line, never its record, and a term nothing defines is left out. They are listed in `knowledge.yaml` with why, and can be confirmed or excluded like items. A page no item covers (an API guide's webhook section) now reaches a change.
- **The rules reach the prompts.** A pattern or standard the architecture stage sends in full carries its rules, and a decision what it decided. The stories stage sends the standards that apply, with their rules, beside the processes; an integration pattern stays the architecture stage's.
- **A document in `inputs/` is indexed once**, from the file (cited by its file, section and page), not again from its knowledge snapshot; a file the sync skipped (a copy of a wiki page, a file dropped twice, an older version) isn't indexed.
- **The Knowledge panel shows titles and why in plain words.** `knowledge.yaml` keeps each item's and near-miss's title; `GET /api/changes/{id}/knowledge` gains `title` and `reason` (C4 2.24.0, additive; `docs/api/openapi.json` regenerated), and an older list takes an item's title from its record. The panel shows the title with the id beside it, the kind in words, and "The change names it", "Linked to northwind-pay", "Its words match the change's title and words" instead of the search's ranks. `atticus change knowledge` and the hand-off PR's list show the same.
- **`atticus handoff done` checks a knowledge scan's answer** as the scan does (each quote on its page, the values tied to it, an id used twice, a superseded page, each conflict and flagged passage) and lists what fails, keeping the run open to fix; after three checks the answer goes in as it is. A `knowledge scan --resume` whose answer failed says which checks failed before handing the next attempt over.
- **`atticus knowledge correct --set` takes a field by the scan's name too** (`lead_time`, `handled_by`) and writes the record's (`leadTime`, `handledBy`).
- **A critic round a crash left behind doesn't use up the gate's rounds:** a round folder with neither its record nor a finding isn't counted.
- **`atticus knowledge ask` has its own agent,** `knowledge-asker`, introduced as what it does. An org pack may define its own; a budget set for `knowledge-reader` still holds for ask.

### What the second stories bench run found, fixed (B-run2-fixes, ADR 0124 addendum B-run2-fixes)

- **The store facts of a store with no migrations name the table's own files.** For each table the change's flows use, the architecture prompt lists the schema file that creates it and the entity mapped to it (`declaringFiles`), not the first four files any of the store's columns is cited in. A superclass or a model shared with other tables is left out.
- **A task's places are where what it adds or changes is declared.** A field a schema already holds is a change there only when the step makes it required; naming it is not. So a validator or a handler that cites one existing field is no longer a place, nor is an API contract no message of which changes. A read or lookup of a column adds no table files. A screen's fields that several screens' files share name the files today's flow cites.
- **Two tasks the target flow puts at the same file and line are one implementation step**, with both tasks and both tasks' criteria (in the story file and the bench draft). Tasks placed only after their neighbour stay apart.
- **The stories bench matches an error case with no field** (`not-found`, `forbidden`, `unavailable`) by the step (strict) or the entry (by entry), the rule and a word its `when` shares with the draft's, whatever field the draft keys it on.
- **The stories prompt says which rule word fits which failure** (`range` for bounds and dates, `format` for shape, `limit` for a total across requests or items, and the others).
- **`atticus repos discover` follows links to clones**, one level, never a link to its own folder or one above it, and a clone two links name once. The repo's folder is the link.
- **`atticus next` skips the reader path for a repo mapped from its verified facts** (its spec's template is `facts@1`): no survey, scan, verify or spec steps, straight to the change.
- **An answered question reaches the next prompt with what the option means and changes**, or the person's own words, not its label alone.
- **Schema problems say what is wrong:** "story 1's interface 1's field 1's side is text ("both"): write one of request, response, message", not "storie 1's interfaces: Invalid input". A field today's entry already returns or takes (its catalog Schema holds it, as a list's `count`) is no longer refused as given nowhere.
- **A new gRPC status on an rpc is an interface change** in design.md.
- **A change's estate index is its own estate's** in a metarepo several estates share: the repos it touches, their systems, the repos joined to them, and outside systems such as vendors.

### The estate fixes: publishes name their event, entries without a flow are listed, errors match branches (C-estate, ADR 0124 addendum C-estate)

- **`atticus verify` rejects a publish step that names no event.** A flow's `publish` step whose `ref` is missing, or isn't a call fact of `callType: event-topic` or `mq-queue`, is rejected as `publish-no-event` (a new reject code, additive like `call-out-no-call`), with the step, its line and what to name.
